# Decisions and the papers they rest on

One entry per science-facing decision: what was decided, by whom and when (**Decided**), where it is
recorded (**Where**), and what it rests on (**Basis**). A published basis names its papers by key from
[library.bib](library.bib), each with the claim it supports and where in the paper that claim is. A
decision that rests on something else — an institutional rule, the PI's decision, an engineering
default — says so in **Basis** instead, and is never given a paper it does not rest on. Where a
decision combines the two, the entry says which part is whose. A **Caveat**, where there is one, is
what the literature says against or beside a decision that was made knowing it. Each heading starts
with the decision's id in the inventory of 2026-10-08 (COL for color, DISP for display, and so on).
How to add a paper or an entry: [README.md](README.md). `tests/test_references.py` checks that every
entry has its three lines, that every link names a file that exists, and that every key resolves.

## Color and luminance

### COL-01 — The default calibration is standard sRGB; how closely the panel follows it is unverified and on the warnings list
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI ("Standard sRGB"; the ruling that asked for a default calibration and a warnings list is in the demo-mode spec's §14).
- **Where:** [engine spec §7 item 1](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#7-color-and-luminance-n4-nr4-nr5), [demo-mode spec §14](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-06-demo-mode-design.md#14-parked-2026-10-07-what-the-reviews-found-and-what-comes-first), [display-state note §1.6](../research/2026-10-07-engine-display-state.md#16-color-gamma-lut-s4-9-wl_xconphotometrypy-s1a-line-276-pitfalls-p19); the brainstorm notes, [§4, Batch 1](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#4-color-and-luminance).
- **Basis:** PI decision, 2026-10-07. What "standard sRGB" means: [@stokes1996standard] — primaries R (0.64, 0.33), G (0.30, 0.60), B (0.15, 0.06), D65 white (0.3127, 0.3290), the piecewise transfer (a linear segment of slope 12.92 at or below 0.00304, otherwise 1.055·x^(1/2.4) − 0.055) and a reference display luminance of 80 cd/m² (Part 2, "Definition of the sRGB color space": Tables 0.1 and 0.2, Eqs. 1.2a-b). That breakpoint is the 1996 draft's: the page notes that "During standardization, a small numerical error caused by rounding error was corrected", and 0.0031308 is IEC 61966-2-1's corrected value (not opened: UNVERIFIED). The page marks itself obsolete and superseded by [@iec1999multimedia], the standard that defines sRGB (its description page read; its text not opened). Why the panel's conformance is a warning and not an assumption: [@abuhaila2025recent] — three consumer displays calibrated to γ = 2.2 fitted γ 1.98-2.30 across channels afterwards, grayscale 2.01-2.29 (Results, "Linearity and gamma function", Table 3), and performance "cannot be assumed or guaranteed" across units of one model (Discussion, "Caveats and limitations").

### COL-02 — In a recording session the default calibration is refused for a task that specifies a color or makes contrast a design factor or a procedure-controlled value; training and piloting run on it with a warning
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (Batch R4, "Yes, from the task file"); widened 2026-10-08 (build B's Q2, "Any brightness setting blocks it"): any light or contrast a parameter sets blocks recording on the default, so no lab task records on it until a panel is measured.
- **Where:** [engine spec §7 item 2](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#7-color-and-luminance-n4-nr4-nr5); the brainstorm notes, [Batch R4](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#the-design-reviews-questions-asked-2026-10-07), and "The morning after (asked 2026-10-08)" on engine-b.
- **Basis:** PI decision, 2026-10-07 and 2026-10-08. Supporting its premise, that stimulus values are known only for a characterized display: [@brainard2002display] — accurate rendering means choosing digital values from the measured primary spectra and the measured gamma functions of the display in use (pp. 177-179, "Characterization using the standard CRT model"); [@abuhaila2025recent] — performance depends on the monitor's settings and firmware (abstract) and cannot be assumed across units of one model (Discussion, "Caveats and limitations").

### COL-03 — Isoluminance needs a measured calibration in every session; equating against the calibration's named observer is enough
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (Batch 1; Batch R4, "A named observer is enough", offered against a per-animal measured null, which was recommended). The observer was named on 2026-10-08 (COL-17).
- **Where:** [engine spec §7 item 3](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#7-color-and-luminance-n4-nr4-nr5); the brainstorm notes, [§4, Batch 1](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#4-color-and-luminance) and [Batch R4](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#the-design-reviews-questions-asked-2026-10-07).
- **Basis:** PI decision, 2026-10-07. The named observer is a published standard: [@cie2015fundamental] — cone-fundamental-based luminous efficiency functions for 2° and 10° fields, defined as linear combinations of the L and M fundamentals (abstract), following [@sharpe2005luminous] (abstract). Partial support for photometric isoluminance in macaque cortex: [@gegenfurtner1994chromatic] — the null planes of MT neurons "varied from neuron to neuron, but all lay close to the plane of constant photometric luminance" (abstract). The literature on macaque and individual luminosity argues the other way (the caveat).
- **Caveat:** Isoluminance here is the CIE 10° standard observer's (V_F,10), not the animal's. Macaque red-green equiluminance points differ from humans' and between animals, consistent with L:M ratios near 1:1 against 2:1 [@dobkins2000comparison]; monkeys are more sensitive than humans to 15 Hz red-green isoluminant modulation in a way optics do not explain [@lindbloombrown2014spectral]; the macaque luminance plane differs from the human definition [@horwitz2015what]; if "human and monkey cone fundamentals are indeed substantially different", then "a stimulus that is equiluminant for a human may possess luminance contrast for a monkey" [@conway2014color, section "MT"]; even among 22 young humans, settings against the Stockman–Sharpe 2° observer spanned M:L cone-contrast ratios of −6.1 to −0.7 [@he2021modeling]; per-observer flicker photometry is the published remedy where silencing luminance matters [@brainard2002display, p. 183]. Against them, MT null planes lay close to photometric isoluminance [@gegenfurtner1994chromatic]. A methods section states that the isoluminance is the standard observer's; a result that rests on it (laminar M/P/K mapping, chromatic motion) says so.

### COL-04 — Color is written device-independently; a calibration names whose luminous efficiency it used, since "a macaque V(lambda) is not a human one"; gamut clipping silently breaks isoluminance
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, engineering, after review (S1a §12; pitfall P19, 2026-09-01); partly superseded by COL-01 to COL-03, which load color on the default calibration outside recording and accept a named human observer (inventory disagreements 6 and 7); and on 2026-10-10 by COL-17 as built (engine build A2): isoluminance is the lab observer's V_F,10, named in code, and the unstated-observer finding retired, a measured calibration naming its own photometry's observer or not loading.
- **Where:** [S1a §12](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S1a-vocabulary-design.md#12-added-2026-08-31-after-review--what-the-graph-could-not-see), "Colour, device-independently"; [pitfalls P19](../pitfalls.md#expanded-notes) (the table and the expanded note).
- **Basis:** Engineering default. Supporting facts: xyY is CIE chromaticity plus luminance [@cie2018colorimetry] (abstract: chromaticity coordinates, standard observers); DKL is a space of modulations about a white point, with an isoluminant plane [@derrington1984chromatic] (abstract), and its coordinates depend on the background [@brainard1996cone] (p. 564; p. 575). "A macaque V(λ) is not a human one": [@dobkins2000comparison] — macaque red-green equiluminance points showed relatively greater sensitivity to green than humans', consistent with L:M cone ratios near 1:1 in macaques and 2:1 in humans (abstract); [@horwitz2015what] — macaque detection thresholds for flicker lay on a plane that differed from the formal definition of luminance, indicating "a different spectral sensitivity than that of the average human observer" (the text on the human-macaque difference, around Fig. 3); [@lindbloombrown2014spectral] (abstract). That a requested color may lie outside the gamut: [@brainard1996cone] — "There is no guarantee that these differential coordinates can be achieved within the gamut of a physically realizable device" (p. 572, said of a worked example's coordinates). That clipping such a color breaks isoluminance is arithmetic (a clipped color has neither the requested chromaticity nor the requested luminance), not Brainard's statement.

### COL-05 — `visual_search` keeps its isoluminant red and green, `DKL(lum=0, l_m=±0.08)`, and waits for a measured calibration; a training variant with ordinary red and green loads on the default with the warning — its colors superseded by COL-33
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (Batch 2, "A plain-color variant"); refined 2026-10-08 (build B's Q3, "Each its own setting"): the variant's red and green are the sRGB primaries' colors, each with its own luminance setting starting at 15 cd/m². The ±0.08 is an engineering default in the task file, with no stated basis; on 2026-10-10 its scene, the PI's answer to the engine A2 plan's Q1: his 2023 red and green, carried over by his published numbers and made isoluminant in the lab's observer, on a D65 gray at their own luminance, one level for all rigs, the brightest each rig's measured screen can make with a small margin, 16 cd/m² until the panels are measured, and the training variant on the same gray; since engine build A2 it runs on a measured calibration with spectra (COL-33).
- **Where:** [engine spec §7 item 3](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#7-color-and-luminance-n4-nr4-nr5) and [§24](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#24-builds-in-order) (build B); [`tasks/visual_search.py`](../../tasks/visual_search.py) (the module docstring; `RED`, `GREEN`); the brainstorm notes, [§4, Batch 2](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#4-color-and-luminance), and "The morning after (asked 2026-10-08)" on engine-b; [tasks/visual_search.py](../../tasks/visual_search.py), BACKGROUND, RED and GREEN.
- **Basis:** PI decisions, 2026-10-07 and 2026-10-08. The ±0.08 rests on no paper. *Corrected 2026-10-10 (the engine A2 plan's review, S-C1): this said "no paper gives a DKL or pooled-cone-contrast magnitude for isoluminant red-green search items"; the PI's own does.* His 2023 pop-out search showed red and green circles "rendered isoluminant to a human observer at 2.8 cd/m2 on a uniform gray background", with cone contrasts red C_L = 0.27, C_M = −0.54, C_S = −0.94 and green C_L = −0.06, C_M = 0.13, C_S = −0.87, about a background of cone excitations 1.86, 0.94 and 0.023 [@westerberg2023feedforward] (Methods, "Task design: Pop-out search"; read 2026-10-10 in its Europe PMC full text). Those contrasts were computed in another cone set, by Cole and Hine's (1992) method (the paper's ref. 94). Carried over by the numbers (taken as contrasts in the CIE 2006 10° observer about a D65 gray, L and M moved to the nearest point isoluminant under V_F,10 (COL-17), S kept) and read as DKL, the items are `l_m` +0.603 and −0.143, a difference of about 0.75, sharing an S decrement near 0.9; carried over by the paper's lights instead (its xy, converted through the CIE's matrix against a D65 gray), +0.643 and −0.118, a route that depends on the gray's chromaticity, which the paper does not give (both computed 2026-10-10). The task's ±0.08 differ by 0.16 with no S change, about a fifth of either. The 2026-10-08 search (PubMed for macaque color pop-out search methods; the abstracts of Schall & Hanes 1993 and Thompson et al. 1996 state neither their stimuli's isoluminance nor their cone contrast) missed that paper. Under the hybrid normalization (COL-07), `l_m = 0.08` is a pooled cone contrast of 0.08 [@brainard1996cone] (p. 568, Eq. A.4.2), inside the isoluminant L−M ceilings the A2 research note computed for modeled displays (0.15-0.26 pooled; its §0 item 3, a computation, not a measurement or a paper).
- **Caveat:** COL-03's caveat applies to the isoluminance claim: it is the CIE 10° standard observer's, not the animal's [@dobkins2000comparison; @lindbloombrown2014spectral; @horwitz2015what; @conway2014color; @he2021modeling].

### COL-06 — Color spaces: xyY (absolute); cone contrast about the background, for cone-isolating stimuli; DKL defined in cone-contrast terms with one stated normalization
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, engineering, from the science review ("Taken without asking, from the reviews"), in the spec the PI approved that day.
- **Where:** [engine spec §7 item 4](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#7-color-and-luminance-n4-nr4-nr5); the brainstorm notes, [§4](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#4-color-and-luminance) ("Settled before the element") and ["Taken without asking, from the reviews"](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#the-design-reviews-questions-asked-2026-10-07).
- **Basis:** Engineering default resting on published definitions. [@cie2018colorimetry] — the CIE's tristimulus values and chromaticity coordinates (abstract). [@brainard1996cone] — cone contrast is each cone's change in excitation divided by the background's, Eq. A.4.1 (p. 564); DKL is built on the same idea (p. 563), and its normalization constants "must be explicitly specified whenever the space is used" (p. 575). [@derrington1984chromatic] — the luminance, constant-S and constant-L&M axes through a white point (abstract), drawing on [@macleod1979chromaticity] (abstract: a constant-luminance chromaticity diagram of cone excitations).

### COL-07 — DKL: the luminance axis in luminance contrast, the color axes in pooled cone contrast
- **Decided:** 2026-10-08, the PI (A2 Q1: "Hybrid", offered against pooled cone contrast, Psychtoolbox `DKLDemo.m`'s per-axis scaling, and no DKL); open at the inventory's commit.
- **Where:** [A2 research note §2](../research/2026-10-08-engine-a2-color-research.md#2-dkl-normalizations-in-use) and its [Q1](../research/2026-10-08-engine-a2-color-research.md#questions-for-the-pi-the-agents-framing-to-be-asked-in-plain-terms); [engine spec §7.4](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#7-color-and-luminance-n4-nr4-nr5), which requires one stated normalization; the brainstorm notes, "The morning after (asked 2026-10-08)", on engine-b; [wl_xcon/photometry.py](../../wl_xcon/photometry.py), cone_contrast (engine build A2; its axes as built: COL-32).
- **Basis:** [@derrington1984chromatic] — the space: a luminance axis and two isoluminant axes, "constant B" and "constant R & G", through a white point (abstract). [@brainard1996cone] — luminance contrast is the natural scaling for the luminance axis, and there is none for the two color axes (p. 571); scaling each axis to unit response for its isolating stimulus of unit pooled cone contrast does not depend on apparatus or observer (p. 572; pooled cone contrast is Eq. A.4.2, p. 568); the constants and the background must be stated whenever the space is used (p. 575); Derrington et al.'s own normalization, unit response at the monitor's largest in-gamut modulation, "makes the definition of the color space apparatus dependent" (pp. 571-572), and angular coordinates depend on the normalization (p. 574). [@conway2014color] — the true geometry of cone-opponent spaces "is contingent on the instrument used to generate them" ("Questions of Perception"). Taking Brainard's natural choice for one axis and his adopted one for the other two is the PI's decision among the options offered; no single paper prescribes the combination, so a methods section states it. Its consequence, that the axes then carry different units and the gamut check needs a limit per axis, is the research note's (§2).

### COL-08 — Realizability of every color is tested by full conversion to primary weights in [0, 1], replacing the `magnitude() <= max_cone_contrast` gate
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, engineering, from the science review (finding 7b), in the revised spec.
- **Where:** [engine spec §7 item 4](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#7-color-and-luminance-n4-nr4-nr5); [A2 research note §0](../research/2026-10-08-engine-a2-color-research.md#0-corrections-to-current-repo-text) item 3 and [§3](../research/2026-10-08-engine-a2-color-research.md#3-cone-contrast-to-primary-weights).
- **Basis:** Engineering default on a published method. [@brainard2002display] — the primary intensities that produce desired cone coordinates are w = M⁻¹s, with M the cone excitations of the measured primaries (pp. 177-178, Eq. 14), and intensities are numbers in [0, 1] (p. 173, Eq. 1). [@brainard1996cone] — a requested cone-contrast or DKL modulation carries "no guarantee" of lying within a device's gamut (p. 572, said of a worked example's coordinates).

### COL-09 — The default background is black; a task using DKL, cone contrast or Weber contrast declares its own non-black background; xyY colors and Michelson contrast run on black
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (Batch 2, "Black"; Batch 3, "It must declare one").
- **Where:** [engine spec §7 item 5](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#7-color-and-luminance-n4-nr4-nr5) and [§4.12](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#412-contrast); the brainstorm notes, [§4, Batches 2 and 3](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#4-color-and-luminance); [wl_xcon/check.py](../../wl_xcon/check.py), cone-color-on-black and cone-color-two-backgrounds (engine build A2).
- **Basis:** PI decision for black, 2026-10-07. Why background-relative quantities need a non-black background: [@brainard1996cone] — cone contrast "makes sense primarily when there is a background with respect to which contrast may be computed" (pp. 563-564), and DKL coordinates require "an explicit specification of the cone excitation coordinates of the background" (p. 575); [@pelli2013measuring] — Weber contrast is the target's difference from the background luminance L_b divided by L_b, while Michelson contrast uses only the maximum and minimum luminance (the opening paragraph, where both are defined inline, unnumbered). On black, L_b = 0 and the background's cone excitations are zero, so Weber and cone contrast are undefined (arithmetic, not a citation).

### COL-10 — The calibration is measured at a reference background; limits for a task's own background come from the full conversion, not a stored maximum
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, engineering default, in the engine spec.
- **Where:** [engine spec §7 item 5](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#7-color-and-luminance-n4-nr4-nr5).
- **Basis:** Engineering default. Supporting: [@brainard1996cone] — cone-contrast coordinates depend on the background (p. 564), and "the construction of the conversion matrix depends on the cone excitation coordinates of the background" (p. 571); [@brainard2002display] — the conversion to primary intensities is a function of the target's cone coordinates (p. 178, Eq. 14), so a contrast limit stored for one background does not carry over to another.

### COL-11 — The transfer is a measured table per channel; one calibration for the whole panel, the stereoscope's two eyes assumed equal; an imbalance appears as eye dominance, so dichoptic tasks counterbalance the masked eye
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (Batch 1, "A measured table"; Batch 3, "One for the panel"; Batch R4, "Assume equal, as decided", offered against measuring each eye's mirror path, which was recommended).
- **Where:** [engine spec §7 item 6](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#7-color-and-luminance-n4-nr4-nr5); the brainstorm notes, [§4, Batches 1 and 3](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#4-color-and-luminance) and [Batch R4](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#the-design-reviews-questions-asked-2026-10-07).
- **Basis:** PI decision, 2026-10-07. The measured per-channel table: [@brainard2002display] — each channel's output is a measured function of its digital value, inverted to find the value for a desired intensity (pp. 177-179, "Gamma correction"). The stated limitation: [@zhou2013effect] — prior studies indicate "that an interocular luminance difference can induce a functional imbalance between the two eyes in normal observers, reducing binocular function, altering interocular interactions and shifting sensory dominance to the eye with the higher luminance level" (Introduction); in their data a 1 log-unit attenuation of one eye moved the balance point from 1.03 to 0.91 (Results, "Normal subjects"). Assuming the halves equal runs against published panel measurements (the caveat).
- **Caveat:** Published consumer-OLED uniformity is 4-7% worst case [@abuhaila2025recent, Results "Luminance uniformity", Fig. 8], the left half worse on every consumer display tested [@abuhaila2025recent, Discussion], so "equal halves" is an assumption V9's per-half photometry checks. The stated eye-dominance effect is supported: a 1 log-unit attenuation of one eye moved the balance point from 1.03 to 0.91 [@zhou2013effect]. How large it is at a few percent was not measured: Zhou et al. attenuated one eye by 10× to 1000×, with three observers. Not stated in the spec: a dimmer eye's signal is also delayed (about 38 ms in V1 for a 1.5 log-unit filter [@chadnova2018interocular]), the Pulfrich effect, which counterbalancing the masked eye does not remove from a motion-in-depth task. At a few percent the delay should be far smaller; no measurement at that size was found. A motion-in-depth task checks V9's measured difference first.

### COL-12 — The stimulus calibration record carries a per-mode transfer per half of the panel, and the gamma and luminance ramp test screen runs per half
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, engineering default (S4); superseded by COL-11, S4's amendment pending (inventory disagreement 4).
- **Where:** [S4 §9](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S4-stimulus-presentation-design.md#9-stimulus-calibration-and-the-id-the-manifest-already-wants) and [§10](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S4-stimulus-presentation-design.md#10-test-screens) item 3; [S9 §6](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S9-operations-console-design.md#6-test-screens).
- **Basis:** Engineering default, superseded. Its published basis: [@abuhaila2025recent] — on a 3 × 3 grid, worst-case luminance differences from the center were 10.7% (LG LCD), about 4% (ASUS OLED) and about 7% (Samsung QD-OLED TV), and all three consumer displays were worse on their left half (Results, "Luminance uniformity", Fig. 8; Discussion). On a panel split between the eyes, a left-right difference is an interocular difference.

### COL-13 — Overall brightness is capped so the panel's ABL never engages, at a level V9 measures (including one half's fill dimming the other); a display exceeding it is refused and the record states the cap
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (Batch 2, "Stay below it").
- **Where:** [engine spec §7 item 7](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#7-color-and-luminance-n4-nr4-nr5); [S0 §5.4](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S0-hosts-and-hardware-design.md#54-panel-acceptance-test--written-now-before-the-panel-exists) item 2; [S4 §9](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S4-stimulus-presentation-design.md#9-stimulus-calibration-and-the-id-the-manifest-already-wants); the brainstorm notes, [§4, Batch 2](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#4-color-and-luminance).
- **Basis:** PI decision, 2026-10-07, supported by [@dimigen2026advantages] — ABL "is an inherent technical limitation of OLED panels", and monitors "vary with regard to the APL at which this mechanism kicks in" (Introduction); on an ASUS PG27AQDM with its uniform-brightness mode off, luminance "changed drastically with the APL" above the 40% brightness setting (about 140 cd/m²), and "at settings of 40% or less, no ABL occurred" (Results, "Auto-brightness limiting behavior"), so operating below a measured level prevents ABL. [@abuhaila2025recent] — ABL is triggered in proportion to the average picture level (Metrics, "Filling factor"). [@brainard2002display] — the CRT forerunner, "dynamic brightness stabilization", makes each pixel depend on the whole frame and is detected with a fixed test patch and a changing surround (p. 180, failure 6 and test 3), the test V9 applies across the two halves. S0 §5.4 item 2 credited Abu Haila et al. with an earlier study's fill-factor finding (a Sony PVM-2541, quoted in their Introduction); corrected there on 2026-10-08.

### COL-14 — Output is 10-bit (1024 levels per channel), verified at V1; dithering, if it is the fallback, is deterministic (so the exact drawer models it) and on the warnings list
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (Batch 1, "10-bit, verified at V1").
- **Where:** [engine spec §7 item 8](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#7-color-and-luminance-n4-nr4-nr5); the brainstorm notes, [§4, Batch 1](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#4-color-and-luminance).
- **Basis:** PI decision, 2026-10-07, supported by [@brainard2002display] — "The 256-level quantization of the 8-bit DACs is too coarse to measure threshold on a uniform background"; finer steps come from more-than-8-bit DACs or from spatial or temporal dithering (p. 183, "Fine control of contrast"); [@pelli1991accurate] — accurate low contrast needs more than 8 bits, and their attenuator renders low contrasts with 12-bit accuracy (abstract); [@tyler1997colour] — bit-stealing gives 11-12 bits of luminance resolution, cutting the smallest mid-range step "from 1.5% to > 0.2%" (abstract, as PubMed gives it: read literally, more than 0.2%, probably a typo for less; the publisher's text could not be opened). Dithering as a fallback: [@allard2008noisybit] — random dithering is perceptually equivalent to continuous luminance resolution at ordinary spatiotemporal resolutions (abstract). Their method is random; making the fallback deterministic so the exact drawer can model it is an engineering choice with no paper behind it (none of these argues for deterministic over random dithering).

### COL-15 — Cone fundamentals: one lab-wide default, Stockman & Sharpe 10° (CIE 2006); a task may name the 2° set for foveal work; no macaque set; the spectroradiometer's spectra are stored so any set converts
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (Batch R4; Batch R5, the 10° standard "unless there is a better macaque alternative"); confirmed 2026-10-08 (A2 Q4, "Human 10°, switchable later", after a deeper search; COL-16). Its citation — "virtually identical" is Schnapf et al. 1988, the macaque peaks Baylor et al. 1987 — the PI kept on 2026-10-08 ("Yes, keep it"), confirming the research note's correction of the spec.
- **Where:** [engine spec §7 item 9](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#7-color-and-luminance-n4-nr4-nr5); [A2 research note §0](../research/2026-10-08-engine-a2-color-research.md#0-corrections-to-current-repo-text), [§1](../research/2026-10-08-engine-a2-color-research.md#1-cone-fundamentals-data) and [§5](../research/2026-10-08-engine-a2-color-research.md#5-macaque-vs-human); the brainstorm notes, [Batches R4 and R5](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#the-design-reviews-questions-asked-2026-10-07), and [§4, Batch 4](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#4-color-and-luminance), which named the 2° set as the example, superseded; for the citation, "The morning after (asked 2026-10-08)", on engine-b; the table, bundled in [wl_xcon/cie/](../../wl_xcon/cie/NOTICE.md) and read by [wl_xcon/cones.py](../../wl_xcon/cones.py) (engine build A2; ADR-0012).
- **Basis:** PI decision, 2026-10-07, on a literature check. [@stockman2000spectral] — L- and M-cone spectral sensitivities for 2° and 10° central fields (abstract). [@cie2006fundamental] — the CIE 2006 cone fundamentals, derived from the Stiles & Burch 10° color-matching functions following Stockman & Sharpe, tabulated for 10° and 2°, adjustable to any field size from 1° to 10° and to age (abstract). [@stockman2023formulae] — the CIE "sanctioned the cone spectral sensitivity estimates of Stockman and Sharpe" as "physiologically-relevant" standards (Abstract; Introduction); standard macular density at 460 nm is 0.350 for 2° and 0.095 for 10°, the pigment being "largely absent by about 10 deg" ("Formulae for standard observers", "Macular and lens optical density spectra"), which is why 2° suits foveal stimuli. Macaque and human cones: [@schnapf1988spectral] — red and green cones "from a human retina were virtually identical to those of macaque cones" (abstract; one human retina, L and M only); [@baylor1987spectral] — suction-electrode spectra of five blue, twenty green and sixteen red cones of *Macaca fascicularis*, peaking near 430, 531 and 561 nm, and human color matching well predicted from the monkey cones (abstract); [@horwitz2015what] — cone absorption spectra "are very similar" in the two species (the opening section). Common practice in macaque work: [@li2022cone] — macaque V1 cone-isolating and DKL stimuli computed with the Stockman & Sharpe 10° fundamentals (Methods, "Cone-isolating calculation", "DKL Hue"); [@conway2014color] — "human cone fundamentals are routinely used to define color stimuli for testing responses of cells in monkeys" (section "MT"); [@spitschan2018method] recommend the CIE 2006 fundamentals ("Spectral sensitivities assumed"). Storing spectra: [@brainard2002display] — a spectroradiometer is needed to use any description of color vision other than the CIE system's (p. 185, "Instrumental considerations"). "No macaque set" is an absence the searches of 2026-10-07 and 2026-10-08 found ("not found", not "does not exist": the A2 research note §5), not a published claim.
- **Caveat:** For nonhuman observers "it is crucial to customize the rendering calculations for the spectral sensitivities appropriate for the species" [@brainard2002display, p. 183], and to account for one monkey's detection data by its lens alone, the macaque lens "would have to be ~60%" of the average human lens density [@horwitz2015what]. The unadjusted human observer is nonetheless the field's routine practice [@conway2014color; @li2022cone], and the 2026-10-08 computation put the effect near the CIE's own 2° against 10° difference and below the spread between animals. The observer's parameters are recorded and the macaque lens or macular setting can be switched on later.

### COL-16 — Cone fundamentals: the CIE 10° observer, unadjusted for macaques
- **Decided:** 2026-10-08, the PI (A2 Q4: "Human 10°, switchable later", offered against a macaque lens at 60%, lens plus no macular pigment, and no macular pigment only), after a literature search; the 10° default itself on 2026-10-07 (the brainstorm notes' N§R5).
- **Where:** [engine spec §7.9](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#7-color-and-luminance-n4-nr4-nr5), [A2 research note §5](../research/2026-10-08-engine-a2-color-research.md#5-macaque-vs-human) and its [Q4](../research/2026-10-08-engine-a2-color-research.md#questions-for-the-pi-the-agents-framing-to-be-asked-in-plain-terms); the brainstorm notes, "The morning after (asked 2026-10-08)", on engine-b; [wl_xcon/cones.py](../../wl_xcon/cones.py), CIE2006_10, which records the observer's standard parameters (engine build A2).
- **Basis:** [@stockman2000spectral] — the 10° cone fundamentals themselves (Table 2, pp. 1732-1734); that the CIE 2006 observer is this set is [@cie2006fundamental]'s statement (abstract). [@schnapf1988spectral] — the red and green cones of one human retina were "virtually identical" to macaque cones (abstract). [@baylor1987spectral] — macaque cone peaks near 430, 531 and 561 nm, and human color matching well predicted from the monkey cones (abstract). [@horwitz2015what] — fitting rhesus detection data needs a macaque lens about 60% of the average human lens density, put down to the monkeys being younger and smaller (the text on Fig. 3); [@lindbloombrown2014spectral] — monkeys had lower blue-to-green threshold ratios than humans at 0.5-7°, "consistent with a lower lens optical density" (abstract). Not adjusting for it is the PI's decision. It rests on the search's own computation that a macaque lens or macular setting changes stimuli by about the CIE's own 2° vs 10° difference (summarized in the brainstorm notes; not a published result), and on that being smaller than the spread between animals. The spread is published — macular pigment's central peak 0.42-1.0 across primate retinas, falling to low, fairly constant levels within 1 mm [@snodderly1984macular] (abstract), and zeaxanthin differing up to fourfold between individual monkeys [@handelman1991biological] (abstract) — but comparing it with the computed effect is the search's own. Common practice uses the unadjusted human observer: [@conway2014color] (section "MT"); [@li2022cone] (Methods). The observer's parameters are recorded so the setting can be switched on later, which the standards provide for: fundamentals for other conditions are derived from the low-density absorbances with lens, macular and pigment densities [@cie2006fundamental] (abstract), and [@stockman2023formulae] give formulae for non-standard observers with individual macular, lens and photopigment densities (Abstract; "Formulae for non-standard observers and for changes with eccentricity").
- **Caveat:** For nonhuman observers "it is crucial to customize the rendering calculations for the spectral sensitivities appropriate for the species" [@brainard2002display, p. 183], and to account for one monkey's detection data by its lens alone, the macaque lens "would have to be ~60%" of the average human lens density [@horwitz2015what]. The unadjusted human observer is nonetheless the field's routine practice [@conway2014color; @li2022cone], and the 2026-10-08 computation put the effect near the CIE's own 2° against 10° difference and below the spread between animals. The observer's parameters are recorded and the macaque lens or macular setting can be switched on later.

### COL-17 — Which luminosity function defines isoluminance and the DKL luminance axis: the cone-based V_F,10; cd/m² stays on CIE V(λ), so the record carries two luminances
- **Decided:** 2026-10-08, the PI (A2 Q5, "Cone-based V(λ), 10°", offered against classic CIE V(λ) throughout, following the task, and measured per animal); open at the inventory's commit.
- **Where:** [A2 research note Q5](../research/2026-10-08-engine-a2-color-research.md#questions-for-the-pi-the-agents-framing-to-be-asked-in-plain-terms); the brainstorm notes, "The morning after (asked 2026-10-08)", on engine-b; [wl_xcon/cones.py](../../wl_xcon/cones.py), V_F10, held to the CIE's tabulated XYZ_F,10 [@cie2015cie] by tests/test_cones.py (engine build A2).
- **Basis:** PI decision, 2026-10-08. [@cie2015fundamental] — cone-fundamental-based spectral luminous efficiency functions for the 2° and 10° observers, as linear combinations of L and M (abstract), following [@sharpe2005luminous] — a luminosity function consistent with the Stockman & Sharpe L and M fundamentals, based on flicker photometry, "the standard method for defining luminance" (abstract); [@stockman2023formulae] — the CIE adopted Stockman & Sharpe's fundamentals "and their associated measures of luminous efficiency" (Introduction). Keeping cd/m² on CIE V(λ) is SI photometric convention; no photometry standard was opened for it.
- **Caveat:** COL-03's caveat applies: isoluminance under V_F,10 is the CIE 10° standard observer's, not the animal's [@dobkins2000comparison; @lindbloombrown2014spectral; @horwitz2015what; @conway2014color; @he2021modeling].

### COL-18 — Cone-based colors on the sRGB default convert through the inverse of the CIE's LMS-to-XYZ_F,10 matrix, its use there recorded as outside the CIE's definition
- **Decided:** 2026-10-08, the PI (A2 Q2, "CIE's published matrix", offered against a lab best-fit matrix and no cone colors on the default; the research note called it a toss-up); open at the inventory's commit.
- **Where:** [A2 research note Q2](../research/2026-10-08-engine-a2-color-research.md#questions-for-the-pi-the-agents-framing-to-be-asked-in-plain-terms) and [§4](../research/2026-10-08-engine-a2-color-research.md#4-converting-the-default-calibration-without-spectra); the brainstorm notes, "The morning after (asked 2026-10-08)", on engine-b; [wl_xcon/photometry.py](../../wl_xcon/photometry.py), Calibration.cones (engine build A2).
- **Basis:** PI decision, 2026-10-08. [@cie2015fundamental] — publishes the linear transformations from the cone fundamentals to X_F, Y_F, Z_F (abstract). [@stokes1996standard] — sRGB is defined through CIE 1931 XYZ (Part 2). [@brainard2002display] — when only each primary's XYZ is known, cone coordinates follow "to a good approximation" from a linear transform of XYZ (p. 178, Eqs. 9-10). Applying the XYZ_F matrix to CIE 1931 XYZ is not a CIE-defined use, as recorded; its error on real panels is unknown (the research note's §4 computed it only for modeled displays).

### COL-19 — The cone-to-primary conversion assumes additive, independent channels, no ABL, a uniform screen and 10-bit quantization; three primaries cannot also silence rods or ipRGCs
- **Decided:** 2026-10-08, stated in the A2 research note from published sources.
- **Where:** [A2 research note §3](../research/2026-10-08-engine-a2-color-research.md#3-cone-contrast-to-primary-weights).
- **Basis:** Published. [@brainard2002display] — the standard display model rests on pixel independence and channel independence (p. 176), each to be tested (pp. 179-181); where rods matter, "one can consider constructing custom four-primary displays" (p. 183). [@abuhaila2025recent] — channel additivity held on all four systems tested, including a QD-OLED TV (Results, "Channel additivity", Table 2); uniformity and fill-factor results (Figs. 8-9). [@dimigen2026advantages] — ABL is inherent to OLED panels (Introduction). [@spitschan2018method] — to vary one photoreceptor class while holding the other n − 1 constant, "at least n primary lights are necessary"; cones plus melanopsin need four, and adding rods five ("The method of silent substitution", "Fundamentals"); [@estevez1982silent] — silent substitution generalized to any number of photoreceptor classes (abstract).

### COL-20 — The color instrument is a spectroradiometer (full spectra, so any observer, macaque included); calibration is automated from the console with the instrument at the eye point, writing a dated record
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (the brainstorm notes' §9, Batch 1).
- **Where:** [engine spec §12 item 1](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#12-calibration-procedures-and-test-screens-n9-nr5); the brainstorm notes, [§9, Batch 1](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#9-calibration-procedures-and-test-screens).
- **Basis:** PI decision, 2026-10-07, supported by [@brainard2002display] — "A spectroradiometer provides the most general characterization of the spectral properties of a display", and is necessary for any description of color vision other than the CIE system's (p. 185, "Instrumental considerations"); measuring the primaries' spectra is "the best way" to obtain the cone-excitation matrix (p. 178); and for nonhuman observers "it is crucial to customize the rendering calculations for the spectral sensitivities appropriate for the species" (p. 183); and "the light sensor should generally be in approximately the same position as the observer's eye" (p. 173), the published basis for measuring at the eye point. Automation from the console is the PI's ruling (he chose "Automated, from the console", the option that put the instrument at the eye point; the brainstorm notes' §9, Batch 1), with no paper behind it.

### COL-21 — A calibration never expires but carries its age; past 30 days it is a warning
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (Batch 3, in his words: "never expires, but there is an age that is associated with the calibration. A warning pops after 30 days").
- **Where:** [engine spec §7 item 10](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#7-color-and-luminance-n4-nr4-nr5); the brainstorm notes, [§4, Batch 3](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#4-color-and-luminance).
- **Basis:** PI decision, 2026-10-07. That displays drift, which is why age is tracked: [@brainard2002display] — warm-up of 45-60 min with luminance rising by up to 20%, lifetime decay of CRT phosphors and LCD backlights, and one CRT's cutoff rising by about six levels (of 255) per month (p. 182, "Temporal homogeneity"); [@dimigen2026advantages] — an OLED monitor reached 99% of its asymptotic luminance after 36 min, and its luminance drifted with panel temperature over minutes (Results, "Operating time", "Slow temperature-luminance interactions"); [@spitschan2018method] — device output drifts "need to be either calibrated, or at least characterized" ("Challenges to silent substitution", "Device uncertainty"). The 30-day interval has no published source: none of these, nor a PubMed and web search for OLED aging or recalibration intervals in vision science, gives one; they support tracking age, not 30 days.

### COL-22 — A photograph is read as standard sRGB and converted through the rig's calibration; the luminance of its white is declared (under the cap); the record names the calibration
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (the brainstorm notes' §1, Batch 3).
- **Where:** [engine spec §4.6](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#46-images-and-movies); the brainstorm notes, [§1, Batch 3](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#1-what-can-be-shown).
- **Basis:** PI decision, 2026-10-07. Reading untagged images as sRGB follows what the paper that introduced sRGB proposed for the web: [@stokes1996standard] — "We propose that all page elements defined in the style sheets be assumed to be in the sRGB color space unless embedded ICC profiles indicate otherwise" (Part 3, "Implementation on the Web"), a proposal about style-sheet page elements, not a rule for image files; [@iec1999multimedia] is the standard (its text not opened, so what it says of untagged content is UNVERIFIED). The white's luminance must be declared because sRGB's 80 cd/m² is a reference viewing condition, not a property of the image file [@stokes1996standard] (Part 2, Table 0.1).

### COL-23 — Matching an image set (mean luminance, RMS contrast, optionally the amplitude spectrum) is a declared preparation step, off unless named, computed in linear luminance after the calibration and recorded
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (the brainstorm notes' §1, Batch 3), on the science review's finding 7(e).
- **Where:** [engine spec §4.6](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#46-images-and-movies); the brainstorm notes, [§1, Batch 3](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#1-what-can-be-shown).
- **Basis:** PI decision, 2026-10-07. The practice it formalizes: [@willenbockel2010controlling] — the SHINE toolbox normalizes and equates mean luminance, contrast and Fourier amplitude spectra across images to "minimize potential low-level confounds" (abstract). Why linear luminance: [@brainard2002display] — emitted intensity is a nonlinear (gamma) function of the digital value (pp. 178-179, Fig. 4, Eq. 16), so statistics of pixel values are not statistics of light. Off unless named, and recorded: the PI's.

### COL-24 — Every contrast is written with its convention (Weber, Michelson, RMS); once build A migrates the reference tasks a bare contrast is refused at load
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (the brainstorm notes' §1, Batch 1).
- **Where:** [engine spec §4.12](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#412-contrast); the brainstorm notes, [§1, Batch 1](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#1-what-can-be-shown).
- **Basis:** PI decision, 2026-10-07, supported by [@pelli2013measuring] — Weber, Michelson and RMS contrast are different quantities (the opening paragraph, where all three are defined inline, unnumbered), and "Weber contrast is preferred for letter stimuli, Michelson contrast is preferred for gratings, and RMS contrast is preferred for natural stimuli and efficiency calculations", so a bare number does not say which was meant.

### COL-25 — A patterned fill's mean luminance defaults to what is behind it; a fill whose mean differs (a luminance step under the pattern) is always a warning, in every session kind
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (Batch R3, "Always a warning", offered against refusing it in recording), on the science review's finding 4.
- **Where:** [engine spec §4.3](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#43-what-each-fill-must-do-n1-batches-2-5-6); the brainstorm notes, [Batch R3](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#the-design-reviews-questions-asked-2026-10-07).
- **Basis:** PI decision, 2026-10-07. The default follows the usual definition of a contrast stimulus as a modulation of a uniform background: [@brainard1996cone] (pp. 563-564, Fig. A.4.1); [@pelli2013measuring] — contrast "quantifies its relative difference in luminance from the background" (the opening paragraph, which has no heading). A warning rather than a refusal: the PI's.

### COL-26 — Each edge block states whether it is a contrast envelope (the default for patterned fills) or an opacity ramp, and what size means for it (a Gaussian's cut-off and σ declared separately)
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, engineering, from the science review's finding 4; the PI ruled on its companion (Batch R3; COL-25).
- **Where:** [engine spec §4.2](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#42-the-first-set-of-blocks).
- **Basis:** Engineering default from the science review. No paper prescribing the convention was found (searched 2026-10-08: PubMed for Gaussian envelopes with truncation or windowing in grating and Gabor methods; the science review's finding 4 cites nothing). That the convention is common in methods sections is the lab's impression, not a sourced claim.

### COL-27 — An achromatic stimulus not defined against a background is D65 white at an absolute luminance in cd/m²
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (Build A's plans, "Absolute cd/m²", offered against a fraction of the panel's capped white, which was recommended).
- **Where:** the brainstorm notes, [Build A's plans](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#build-as-plans-asked-2026-10-07-while-planning); the luminance `Param` comments of [`tasks/fixation_detection.py`](../../tasks/fixation_detection.py), [`tasks/adaptive_detection.py`](../../tasks/adaptive_detection.py), [`tasks/visual_search.py`](../../tasks/visual_search.py) and [`tasks/calibration.py`](../../tasks/calibration.py).
- **Basis:** PI decision, 2026-10-07. D65 is the CIE standard daylight illuminant [@cie2018colorimetry] (abstract: the standard illuminants) and the sRGB white point (0.3127, 0.3290) [@stokes1996standard] (Table 0.2), so "white" means the same on the default calibration and on a measured one. Absolute rather than relative: the PI's.

### COL-28 — The reference tasks' fixation points and targets start at 40 cd/m² on the black background
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (Build A's plans, "40 cd/m²"); kept as the start on 2026-10-08 (build B's Q1).
- **Where:** the brainstorm notes, [Build A's plans](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#build-as-plans-asked-2026-10-07-while-planning) and [The morning after](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#the-morning-after-asked-2026-10-08); `fix_luminance` and `target_luminance` in [`tasks/fixation_detection.py`](../../tasks/fixation_detection.py) and [`tasks/adaptive_detection.py`](../../tasks/adaptive_detection.py), `fix_luminance` only in [`tasks/visual_search.py`](../../tasks/visual_search.py) (its targets are DKL colors) and `target_luminance` only in [`tasks/calibration.py`](../../tasks/calibration.py); the search task's fixation point is on its gray since engine build A2 (COL-33).
- **Basis:** PI decision, 2026-10-07. No published convention was found (searched 2026-10-08: the web for typical fixation-point luminance in macaque work; no convention or review was found). That reported values vary over orders of magnitude with the paradigm is the lab's impression from that search, not a sourced figure.

### COL-29 — Until V9 measures the panel's cap, luminance settings are bounded: at 100 cd/m² from 2026-10-07, at 0-80 cd/m² from 2026-10-08, white on the default calibration counting as the published sRGB 80 cd/m²
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI ("Up to 100 cd/m²", offered against 200); lowered 2026-10-08 (build B's Q1, "80, the standard", offered against 100 and no absolute scale).
- **Where:** the brainstorm notes, [Build A's plans](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#build-as-plans-asked-2026-10-07-while-planning) and [The morning after](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#the-morning-after-asked-2026-10-08); the luminance `Param`s of the four reference tasks under [`tasks/`](../../tasks/README.md) (`high=100.0` until engine build B lowers it; inventory disagreement 8).
- **Basis:** PI decisions, 2026-10-07 and 2026-10-08. The 80 cd/m² is the sRGB reference display luminance [@stokes1996standard] (Part 2, Table 0.1). For scale only: [@dimigen2026advantages] measured no ABL at settings up to about 140 cd/m² on a related WOLED monitor (Results, "Auto-brightness limiting behavior"); that is not the lab's panel.

### COL-30 — Color noise and colored Mondrians are allowed on the default calibration, with the warning
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (the brainstorm notes' §4, Batch 4, "Yes, with the warning").
- **Where:** the brainstorm notes, [§4, Batch 4](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#4-color-and-luminance).
- **Basis:** PI decision, 2026-10-07: a rule about which session kinds may use an uncharacterized display, with no scientific claim to source.

### COL-31 — Known panel hazards the color design answers: a QD-OLED's transfer is not a power law; ABL dims the panel with fill; low contrasts need fine steps (hence a measured table, a cap below ABL, 10-bit)
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, stated as known in the brainstorm notes, from an earlier analysis in CHECKPOINT (the display-state note §1.6); never measured, and no one decided it.
- **Where:** the brainstorm notes, [§4](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#4-color-and-luminance) ("Settled before the element"); [display-state note §1.6](../research/2026-10-07-engine-display-state.md#16-color-gamma-lut-s4-9-wl_xconphotometrypy-s1a-line-276-pitfalls-p19) and [§7](../research/2026-10-07-engine-display-state.md#7-inference-what-a-design-can-settle-now-and-what-cannot-be-settled).
- **Basis:** Mixed. "Low contrasts need fine steps" is published: [@brainard2002display] (p. 183, "Fine control of contrast"); [@pelli1991accurate] (abstract). "ABL dims the panel with fill" is published for OLEDs in general: [@dimigen2026advantages] (Introduction; Results, "Auto-brightness limiting behavior"); [@abuhaila2025recent] (Metrics, "Filling factor"). "A QD-OLED's transfer is not a power law" has no source, and the one QD-OLED measurement found does not support it: after DisplayCAL calibration to γ 2.2 a Samsung QD-OLED TV's channels fitted power functions with γ 2.29-2.30, differing by at most 0.01, and "all three consumer-level displays ... follow a predictable gamma power function" [@abuhaila2025recent] (Results, "Linearity and gamma function", Table 3; Discussion). That is the calibrated output, which they say "is determined by the ICC calibration profile, besides the behavior of the display hardware", so it says nothing of the native transfer; and it is one unit of another product in a chosen picture mode, so the claim may still hold for the PG27UCDM in its sRGB mode; it is unmeasured, and both documents mark it UNVERIFIED since 2026-10-08. The three remedies stand on COL-11, COL-13 and COL-14 regardless.

### COL-32 — DKL's axes as built: `lum` scales the background in every cone; `l_m` moves L and M at the ratio that holds V_F,10 still, S fixed, in unit pooled cone contrast; `s_lm` moves S alone; `+l_m` raises L, `+s_lm` raises S
- **Decided:** 2026-10-10, engineering (engine A2 plan, call 14), building the PI's hybrid (COL-07) and V_F,10 (COL-17).
- **Where:** [wl_xcon/photometry.py](../../wl_xcon/photometry.py), cone_contrast; [the engine A2 plan](../superpowers/plans/2026-10-09-engine-a2.md#calls-taken-in-this-plan), call 14.
- **Basis:** The three directions are DKL's: a luminance axis at fixed chromaticity, a "constant B" axis on which S is fixed and L and M vary, and a "constant R & G" axis on which only S varies [@derrington1984chromatic] (abstract); each scaled to unit response for its isolating stimulus of unit pooled cone contrast, the scaling Brainard adopts for the color axes, with luminance contrast for the luminance axis (COL-07) [@brainard1996cone] (pp. 571-572; pooled cone contrast is Eq. A.4.2, p. 568). The isoluminant ratio is V_F,10's, by construction (COL-17). The signs are engineering: no paper prescribes them, and angular coordinates depend on the normalization chosen [@brainard1996cone] (p. 574); they follow the existing docstring's "red/green" and "violet/lime".
- **Caveat:** DKL coordinates require "an explicit specification of the cone excitation coordinates of the background" [@brainard1996cone] (p. 575). Here a background is written in CIE 1931 terms (a D65 gray), so on two panels with different spectra the same background has slightly different cone excitations and the `l_m` direction differs a little: the A2 research note computed an isoluminant M:L ratio of -2.21 against -2.26 on two modeled displays (its §2; a computation, not a measurement). A methods section states the background and the panel's calibration.

### COL-33 — `visual_search`'s scene: the PI's 2023 red and green, carried over by his numbers and made isoluminant in the lab's observer, on a D65 gray at their own luminance, one level for all rigs, the brightest a measured screen makes with a small margin: 16 cd/m² until the panels are measured; its training variant on the same gray
- **Decided:** 2026-10-10, the PI (the engine A2 plan's Q1), once engine build A2 refused a cone color on the black default background (COL-09). His first answer: "My old studies were limited by the brightness of the crt we used. the gray should be made isoluminant with whatever the brightness is of the stimuli in the scene." Asked back in plain terms, he chose "your 2023 red and green, the gray at the same brightness, one level for all rigs — the brightest each rig's measured screen can make with a small margin: about 16 cd/m² on a standard-color screen, re-set when the panels are measured (build J)". The 16 cd/m² is the controller's reading of "about 16"; carrying the items over by his numbers rather than his lights is engineering (the plan's call 30).
- **Where:** [tasks/visual_search.py](../../tasks/visual_search.py), BACKGROUND, RED and GREEN; [tasks/visual_search_training.py](../../tasks/visual_search_training.py); [the engine A2 plan](../superpowers/plans/2026-10-09-engine-a2.md#questions-for-the-pi), Q1 and call 30.
- **Basis:** PI decision. The PI's published macaque pop-out designs showed their isoluminant red and green items "on a uniform gray background" [@westerberg2020priming] (Materials and Methods, "Experimental design and behavior"); in 2023 red and green circles "rendered isoluminant to a human observer at 2.8 cd/m2 on a uniform gray background", with cone contrasts red C_L = 0.27, C_M = −0.54, C_S = −0.94 and green C_L = −0.06, C_M = 0.13, C_S = −0.87 about a gray of cone excitations 1.86, 0.94 and 0.023 [@westerberg2023feedforward] (Methods, "Task design: Pop-out search"); both read in their Europe PMC full texts (2026-10-09 and 2026-10-10). The colors here carry the paper's numbers over: those contrasts, computed in another cone set by Cole and Hine's (1992) method, taken as contrasts in the CIE 2006 10° observer about a D65 gray, and L and M moved to the nearest point isoluminant under V_F,10 (COL-17), by at most 0.022, S kept (computed 2026-10-10). The paper's lights (its xy) converted through the CIE's matrix against a D65 gray instead give `l_m` +0.643 and −0.118, a route that depends on the gray's chromaticity, which the paper does not give. The level is the PI's rule, the brightest the screen makes with a small margin: on sRGB-like primaries at an 80 cd/m² white the red, made mostly by the red primary (17.0 cd/m² at full drive) with a little green beside it, needs that primary at full drive on a 17.7 cd/m² gray, so 16 cd/m² leaves 9.4% (computed 2026-10-10); on the lab's panels it is set by their own red primaries, unmeasured (XC-310). The fixation point's starting 40 cd/m² (COL-28) stays brighter (Weber +1.5).
- **Caveat:** The isoluminance is the CIE 10° observer's (COL-03's caveat), where the 2023 paper's was computed by Cole and Hine's (1992) method, whose fundamentals its abstract does not name; on the sRGB standard's colors these items lie just outside the gamut at any gray (their S decrement: at 16 cd/m² the red needs a blue weight of −0.006, the green a red of −0.0034 and a blue of −0.0054), so whether the lab's panels make them is known only when they are measured. The training variant's items start at 15 cd/m², 1 cd/m² darker than the gray until a person sets them (its red reaches 16 at 94% of its red primary).

## Display and timing

### DISP-01 — The display is a tandem QD-OLED at 4K/240 Hz, 10-bit, the ASUS PG27UCDM (26.5 in); tandem for ABL headroom and burn-in resistance, so the figure that matters is sustained full-field luminance at 100% APL
- **Decided:** 2026-09-26, the PI (QD-OLED is a requirement), and 2026-09-27 (the PG27UCDM, replacing the 32-inch PG32UCDM Gen 3); the tandem argument is S0's engineering reasoning. The spec map and controller architecture §16 item 3 still said "32-inch-class … 57 cm" until their correction on 2026-10-08 (inventory disagreement 1).
- **Where:** [S0 §5.1](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S0-hosts-and-hardware-design.md#51-panel-class) and [§8](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S0-hosts-and-hardware-design.md#8-open-items) item 3; [direct-view spec §2](../superpowers/specs/2026-09-28-direct-view-design.md#2-geometry-per-setup); [`tasks/rig.py`](../../tasks/rig.py).
- **Basis:** PI decision, supported as follows. [@asus2026rog] — the maker's document: "Panel Technology: Tandem QD-OLED", 3840 × 2160, 0.153 mm pitch, vertical frequency 48–240 Hz, VRR (FreeSync Premium Pro and G-SYNC Compatible), "3 years (including panel burn-in)" (spec page, read 2026-10-08); [@asus2026rogb] — the maker's document: "Tandem QD-OLED", "true 10-bit color", "DisplayPort 2.1a UHBR20 provides a full 80Gbps bandwidth to support uncompressed 4K visuals at a stunning 240Hz", and the vendor's claim that the tandem panel "offers greater longevity compared to previous OLED technologies" (product page, read 2026-10-08; a claim, not a measurement); [@fung2016tandem] — the current efficiency of a tandem OLED of N electroluminescent units "should be N times" that of one unit, "potentially useful for constructing high-efficiency, high-luminance, and long-lifetime OLED displays" (abstract), the physical basis for "a given luminance at lower per-layer current"; that this buys ABL headroom on this panel is S0's inference; [@abuhaila2025recent] — current consumer OLED displays are "adequate to fulfill the requirements of some critical vision science experiments", and their performance "is often highly dependent on specific monitor settings and firmware" (Abstract); the one QD-OLED they tested (a Samsung TV) held luminance independent of fill factor and background (Results, "Filling factor"); [@dimigen2026advantages] — a 240 Hz consumer OLED (an ASUS PG27AQDM, a WOLED, not a QD-OLED) had CRT-like transition times, wide viewing angles and good uniformity, and ABL "can be prevented with appropriate settings" (Abstract); ABL was absent at brightness settings of 40% or less (about 140 cd/m²) (Results, "Auto-brightness limiting behavior"; Discussion, "Controlling OLED-specific luminance artifacts"), which supports S0's "running well below peak luminance" on a related panel; [@ito2013evaluation], [@cooper2013assessment] — earlier OLED monitors judged suitable or preferable for vision research for uniformity, gamut, contrast and temporal response (abstracts). No published characterization of the PG27UCDM, or of any tandem QD-OLED monitor, for vision science was found (searched 2026-10-08: PubMed, "QD-OLED" or "quantum dot OLED" with vision, psychophysics, stimulus or display characterization; no relevant hits).

### DISP-02 — Panel acceptance test (V9): burn-in protection fully defeatable; ABL measured as interocular coupling by a fill-factor sweep; per-half uniformity; gamma, additivity, channel independence; pixel response per mode; sustained 100% APL luminance; failing 1 or 2 disqualifies
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, engineering (S0, written before the panel existed); burn-in mitigation kept off the stimulus by ruling the same day.
- **Where:** [S0 §5.4](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S0-hosts-and-hardware-design.md#54-panel-acceptance-test--written-now-before-the-panel-exists); [S4 §9](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S4-stimulus-presentation-design.md#9-stimulus-calibration-and-the-id-the-manifest-already-wants); [controller architecture §13.2](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-controller-architecture-design.md#132-procurement); [pitfalls P4](../pitfalls.md#expanded-notes).
- **Basis:** Engineering default resting on published characterization practice. [@abuhaila2025recent] is "the JOV study" S0 cited: it measures luminance uniformity on a 3 × 3 grid, fill factor (APL), gamma, channel additivity, channel dependency, gamut, pixel response and waveform (Material and methods, "Metrics"); the IPS LCD (LG 27GN950-B) differed from center by up to 10.7%, its left half "far worse than the right half", the ASUS OLED by about 4% and the Samsung QD-OLED TV by about 7% (Results, "Luminance uniformity", Fig. 8), so S0 §5.4 item 3's figures were right, but its "27-inch OLED" is a WOLED and the QD-OLED in the same study was the less uniform OLED; [@dimigen2026advantages] — burn-in features ("screen move", "pixel cleaning", "screen saver", "adjust logo brightness") switched off for testing (Methods, "Display properties and settings"); "pixel shifting" shifts "the entire screen image by a few pixels" (Introduction, "Potential drawbacks of OLEDs"), and that this corrupts a calibrated gaze-to-pixel map is the backfill's inference; ABL makes luminance depend on the average picture level (Introduction; Results, "Auto-brightness limiting behavior"); [@elze2013evaluation] — on medical OLEDs "luminance saturation effects were observed when too many pixels were active simultaneously" (a small white patch near 400 cd/m², full-screen white 162 cd/m²) (abstract); [@brainard2002display] — the standard display model assumes pixel independence and channel independence (p. 176), with tests for their failures (pp. 180–181), including a bar on a surround of varying luminance for intensity that depends on the rest of the screen (p. 180, item 3), the CRT ancestor of the fill-factor sweep; [@cooper2013assessment] — "verifying that the desired and displayed visual patterns match along dimensions such as luminance, spectrum, and spatial and temporal frequency is an essential part of developing controlled experiments", and gamma is well predicted by a power function after "correct adjustment of the settings" (abstract). S0 §5.4 item 2 credited Abu Haila et al. with luminance that "drops drastically" above ~40% fill factor; that is an earlier study of a Sony PVM-2541, quoted in their Introduction, which the display backfill identifies as [@ito2013evaluation], whose abstract (all that could be opened) does not mention fill factor, so the figure is cited only through Abu Haila et al. On their own panels luminance held across fill factor except the green channel near its maximum (Results, "Filling factor", Figs. 9–10). S0 was corrected on 2026-10-08. Treating ABL as an interocular coupling on a split panel is S0's own reasoning; no paper on it was found.

### DISP-03 — V1 and V9 are re-run on any new panel, even of the same model
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, engineering (S0).
- **Where:** [S0 §5.1](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S0-hosts-and-hardware-design.md#51-panel-class) ("Schedule mitigation").
- **Basis:** Engineering, on published findings. [@abuhaila2025recent] — "similar performance across identical models cannot be assumed or guaranteed"; nor "can we guarantee that different exemplars of the same display devices will perform similarly" (Discussion, "Caveats and limitations"): the sentence S0 quoted from "the JOV authors", attributed there since 2026-10-08; [@dimigen2026advantages] — three units of one model, bought in one shipment, each showed "a consistent but largely idiosyncratic pattern" of luminance inhomogeneity (Results, "Spatial uniformity"), and "it seems prudent to occasionally verify and recalibrate the monitor's output" (Discussion, "Not tested: Motion, color, and longevity"); [@wang2011lcd] — "no suitability for research should be assumed but each model should be tested thoroughly" (Discussion).

### DISP-04 — Avoid display stream compression (4K/240 10-bit over DP 2.1 UHBR20 uncompressed); if it is unavoidable, its effect is measured, since "visually lossless" is a claim about human judgement of natural images
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, engineering (S0); the GPU that makes it possible chosen by the PI on 2026-09-26.
- **Where:** [S0 §5.3](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S0-hosts-and-hardware-design.md#53-mode-is-a-rig-configuration), [§4](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S0-hosts-and-hardware-design.md#4-task-pc-hardware) and [§8](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S0-hosts-and-hardware-design.md#8-open-items) item 4.
- **Basis:** Engineering default. [@vesa2026dsc] — a standards body's document: DSC "features low latency and visually lossless performance" (VESA's DSC page, read 2026-10-08); [@allison2018perspectives] — "visually lossless" means losses that are "visually imperceptible"; the research behind it concerns "the limits of human vision when viewing natural images/video"; "a universally or even largely accepted definition of visually lossless remains elusive", and levels of it are best "quantified in terms of the testing paradigm" (abstract, publisher-deposited, read through Crossref). These support S0's point that the claim is defined by human testing on natural content; that it says nothing about gratings, random-dot stereograms or a monkey's V1 is the backfill's inference. [@asus2026rogb] — the maker's document: uncompressed 4K/240 over DP 2.1a UHBR20. The bandwidth (3840 × 2160 × 240 Hz × 30 bit ≈ 59.7 Gbps) is arithmetic; DP 1.4's ~25.9 Gbps and UHBR20's ~77 Gbps payload rates were not checked against VESA's DisplayPort specification, which is not public: UNVERIFIED.

### DISP-05 — Display mode is rig configuration: V1 runs in every mode used; each mode carries its own calibration and deg/px; no code assumes a frame period (the PG27UCDM has no FHD/480 mode)
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, engineering (S0).
- **Where:** [S0 §5.3](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S0-hosts-and-hardware-design.md#53-mode-is-a-rig-configuration); [S4 §4](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S4-stimulus-presentation-design.md#4-displayadapter) item 2.
- **Basis:** Engineering default. [@bridges2020timing] — timing must be validated "for their own stimuli and computer configuration" (Abstract); a graphics card driving a high-resolution display may "send fewer screen updates per second, so that a nominally 60 frames per second display runs at, say, 30 Hz or becomes irregular" (Discussion, "Stimulus differences"); [@saunders2014direct] — "a higher frame rate does not necessarily result in lower latency": input lag differs between displays and modes, so each configuration is measured (Results; Discussion, "Reducing system latency"); [@elze2010misspecifications] — report the refresh rate and frame count with every duration (Conclusion). [@asus2026rog] — the maker's document: vertical frequency 48–240 Hz, no 480 Hz mode listed.

### DISP-06 — The session's rate comes from the display's reported mode, checked against the rig file; a task is checked at each rate it may run at (240 and 120 Hz) or at its declared required rate
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI ("Any rate", the brainstorm notes' §3, Batch 2); the check against the rig file is engineering.
- **Where:** [engine spec §6 item 1](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#6-when-n3-nr3); the brainstorm notes, [§3](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#3-when) (Batch 2; "Settled before the element").
- **Basis:** PI decision. [@bridges2020timing] — a nominal refresh rate can silently become a lower or irregular one (Discussion, "Stimulus differences"), and settings such as Windows screen scaling can add a frame of lag that only hardware measurement reveals (Discussion, "Visual lags from monitor and operating systems"): support for reading the real mode rather than trusting configuration; [@elze2010misspecifications] — a systematic review in which durations were commonly specified by summing frames, which presumes the true refresh rate (Results; Conclusion).

### DISP-07 — Durations are converted to frames once at load; an ordinary duration rounds to the nearest frame; a design-level duration is exact (whole frames or refused); two levels on one frame count are refused; time procedures step in frames; a sequence declares per-item or cumulative rounding
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI ("Declared tolerance", the brainstorm notes' §3, Batch 1; exact by default for design levels, Batch R3).
- **Where:** [engine spec §6 item 2](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#6-when-n3-nr3); [S1a §2](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S1a-vocabulary-design.md#2-guards) (`AfterFrames`); the brainstorm notes, [§3, Batch 1](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#3-when) and [Batch R3](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#the-design-reviews-questions-asked-2026-10-07).
- **Basis:** PI decision. [@elze2010misspecifications] — on raster displays "the frame is the essential temporal unit to control stimulus durations" (Introduction, "Operating standard computer monitors"); experimenters "should always specify the number of presentation frames, the monitor model and technology, the refresh rate" (Conclusion); summing frames misstates brief durations, so durations are best reported in frames (Discussion, "Alternatives to the SOF method"). Refusing two levels on one frame count has no source; it follows from the quantization.

### DISP-08 — A decision on frame N shows on frame N+1; motion is a function of the frame number
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, engineering, settled before the element (the brainstorm notes' §3); motion as a function of the frame since S4, 2026-08-31.
- **Where:** the brainstorm notes, [§3](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#3-when) ("Settled before the element"); [S4 §5](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S4-stimulus-presentation-design.md#5-motion-is-a-function-not-a-trajectory); [controller architecture §14](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-controller-architecture-design.md#14-latency-budgets--requirements-not-measurements).
- **Basis:** Engineering default. [@saunders2014direct] — the "sample-and-then-wait" scheme (sample, render, block until the next refresh), in which the next refresh shows the update, and its latency cost against "wait-and-then-sample" (Discussion, "Reducing system latency"). No source was found for defining motion by frame number: Watson, Ahumada & Farrell 1986 ("Window of visibility") was wanted and could not be opened (Optica refused the fetch), and Saunders & Woods cover only the sample-render-flip half.

### DISP-09 — A late frame never stops a trial and is marked; it repeats the previous content for one refresh and the sequence continues (no frame number skipped); m-sequence and other reverse-correlation trials with any repeat are marked
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI ("Always continue, mark", the brainstorm notes' §3, Batch 1); marking reverse-correlation trials taken without asking, from the reviews.
- **Where:** [engine spec §6 item 3](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#6-when-n3-nr3) and [§8 item 5](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#8-sync-and-evidence-n5-nr3-nr5); the brainstorm notes, [§3, Batch 1](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#3-when) and ["Taken without asking, from the reviews"](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#the-design-reviews-questions-asked-2026-10-07).
- **Basis:** PI decision. [@reid1997use] — the m-sequence method modulates each region "in time by a pseudorandom binary signal, known as an m-sequence", and recovers the receptive field from that known sequence; "several strategies for displaying the m-sequence stimulus are presented" (abstract; in cat). That the sequence steps one element per displayed frame, so that a repeated frame shifts the delivered sequence against the assumed one and must be marked, is the backfill's inference from the method, not a statement in the abstract. [@bridges2020timing] — software can detect a dropped frame and log it (Discussion, "Visual lags from monitor and operating systems").

### DISP-10 — Dropped frames are detected in hardware, live, from the flip-patch comparator at the display surface, not from the engine's frame-interval accounting
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, engineering (S4; the controller architecture).
- **Where:** [S4 §11](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S4-stimulus-presentation-design.md#11-measurement); [S6 §3](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S6-io-subsystem-design.md#3-digital-input-change-detection-not-polling); [controller architecture §3.3](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-controller-architecture-design.md#33-what-the-two-photodiode-patches-change) and [§11.5](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-controller-architecture-design.md#115-refresh-and-dropped-frame-monitoring).
- **Basis:** Engineering default, resting only on the general case for validating timing in independent hardware: [@plant2004selfvalidating] — in-situ hardware benchmarking finds timing errors that range "from poor scripting practices, to incorrect timing specifications, to hardware variability" (abstract). No paper was found that makes hardware the authority for dropped frames. Bridges et al. 2020, cited for it until 2026-10-08, do not support it: they write that "some timing errors (such as a dropped frame) can be detected by the software packages themselves", and their call for hardware tests concerns monitor-side delays such as image processing in the monitor (Discussion, "Visual lags from monitor and operating systems").

### DISP-11 — Temporal modulation: sine at any frequency, sampled per frame; square-wave flicker needs whole frames per half-cycle or is refused
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI ("Smooth ok, hard refused", the brainstorm notes' §3, Batch 1).
- **Where:** [engine spec §6 item 4](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#6-when-n3-nr3); the brainstorm notes, [§3, Batch 1](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#3-when).
- **Basis:** PI decision. The whole-frames rule for square waves follows from frame quantization ([@elze2010misspecifications], Introduction). The literature limits "any frequency": [@shannon1949communication] — Theorem 1, a function containing no frequencies higher than W "is completely determined by giving its ordinates at a series of points spaced 1/2W seconds apart" (§II, "The sampling theorem"; read in the 1998 Proc. IEEE reprint), so a sinusoid sampled once per frame is represented only below half the refresh rate (120 Hz at 240 Hz; 60 Hz at 120 Hz), and above it appears at a lower, aliased frequency; [@dimigen2026advantages] — a 240 Hz OLED shows 60 Hz flicker at four frames per cycle, but 240 Hz is "insufficient for simultaneous attentional tracking at multiple neighboring frequencies (e.g., at 60 and 64 Hz)" (Discussion, "Temporal properties support fast, time-critical paradigms").

### DISP-12 — Onset: the record holds each frame's start and each stimulus's vertical extent; the panel scans top to bottom and both setups put the light sensors at the bottom, so V1 measures the scan delay with patches top and bottom and analysis applies it by height
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI ("Frame start + height", the brainstorm notes' §3, Batch 2).
- **Where:** [engine spec §6 item 5](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#6-when-n3-nr3) and [§21](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#21-asks-of-other-repositories-sent-once-this-spec-is-approved) (the ask of wl-sync); the brainstorm notes, [§3, Batches 1 and 2](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#3-when).
- **Basis:** PI decision, with peer-reviewed support beside the Blur Busters web article the spec cites. [@dimigen2026advantages] — "Since the OLED updates from top to bottom, this adds some delay relative to the flip latency" (Results, "Transition times"; an ASUS WOLED at 240 Hz); [@saunders2014direct] — "In … (CRT), (LCD), and (OLED) displays, the image in memory is drawn from top to bottom in horizontal scan lines", so time must be added "for the vertical refresh process to reach a particular location", at most one refresh period at the bottom (Introduction; Method, "Measurement of system latency"); [@wang2011lcd] — different parts of the screen "are illuminated at different times, delays being especially large along the vertical axis", "sometimes up to 10 ms" (Discussion; a sentence about every monitor tested, LCDs among them run at 60-120 Hz); [@elze2010misspecifications] — "it takes almost one frame to build up the display from the topmost line to the last line" (Discussion, "Alternatives to the SOF method"). The PG27UCDM's own scan remains UNVERIFIED until V1 measures it, as the engine spec says.

### DISP-13 — The longest trial is 5 minutes (a frame cap from 300 s); longer presentations use the continuous mode; a capped trial is recorded as "hang"
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI ("5 minutes", the brainstorm notes' §3, Batch 4). The notes say a capped trial ends as a fault, the engine spec's §6 item 8 says `"hang"` (inventory disagreement 17).
- **Where:** [engine spec §6 item 8](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#6-when-n3-nr3); the brainstorm notes, [§3, Batches 3 and 4](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#3-when).
- **Basis:** PI decision; no published basis (an operational cap; nothing searched beyond the OLED literature). Related, not supporting: the caveat.
