- **Caveat:** On an OLED, a high-contrast stimulus held for tens of seconds to a minute leaves a local luminance difference of about 0.7-1.5% of peak that takes up to minutes to fade [@dimigen2026advantages, Results "Slow temperature-luminance interactions"] (a WOLED, not our panel). After a long adapter the luminance is not the calibrated one; an adaptation design states it until V9 measures it on the PG27UCDM.

### DISP-14 — Contingent changes record their delay (capture time to frame start, plus scan delay and panel latency), labeled a lower bound until V1 and V3 exist; a task may declare a maximum and trials past it are marked
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI ("Measured, declared limit", the brainstorm notes' §3, Batch 3).
- **Where:** [engine spec §6 item 10](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#6-when-n3-nr3); the brainstorm notes, [§3, Batch 3](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#3-when).
- **Basis:** PI decision. [@saunders2014direct] — system latency is the sum of eye-tracker delay, computer delay, waiting for the next refresh (uniform between 0 and one frame), the scan to the stimulus's height and the display's input lag (Introduction; Method); manufacturer figures "can contain errors, as well omissions", so "a more reliable approach would be to measure the end-to-end system latency directly" (Introduction), and measured latencies "should be reported … in place of estimations based on manufacturers' reported device parameters" (Conclusion): support for labeling the computed sum a lower bound until V1 and V3 measure it; [@dimigen2026advantages] — measured saccade-onset-to-display latencies on a 240 Hz OLED, broken into detection, flip and photodiode-onset intervals (Methods, "Practical test 2: Intra-saccadic stimulation"; Discussion).

### DISP-15 — Movies: each frame held a whole number of refreshes (30 fps = 8, 24 fps = 10 at 240 Hz); a rate that does not divide is refused until re-timed beforehand, the re-timing recorded (25 to 24 fps changes speed and pitch by 4%); decoded beforehand or streamed, a missed streamed frame recorded late
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (the brainstorm notes' §1, Batch 3; §6, Batch 2).
- **Where:** [engine spec §4.6](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#46-images-and-movies); the brainstorm notes, [§1, Batch 3](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#1-what-can-be-shown) and [§6, Batch 2](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#6-media-and-sound).
- **Basis:** PI decision; arithmetic (240/30 = 8, 240/24 = 10, 25/24 = 1.042). The whole-refresh rule follows from frame quantization ([@elze2010misspecifications], Introduction, "Operating standard computer monitors"). No source on re-timing movies for neurophysiology was found (searched 2026-10-08: PubMed, movie or video stimuli with frame or refresh rate and frame repetition, pulldown or temporal resampling, in visual cortex or neurons; one hit, not relevant).

### DISP-16 — The GPU drawer matches the exact (numpy) definition within one output level per pixel; edges are anti-aliased and sub-pixel
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (the brainstorm notes' §7, Batch 1).
- **Where:** [engine spec §10 items 1-2](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#10-the-drawer-n7) and [§4.9](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#49-the-slow-exact-drawer); the brainstorm notes, [§7, Batch 1](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#7-the-drawer).
- **Basis:** PI decision. [@bach1997antialiasing] — "Anti-aliasing is a technique for improving spatial resolution at the cost of luminance resolution", used to render small optotypes correctly (abstract); [@bach2001freiburg] — "Anti-aliasing allows for disparities finer than the pixel raster" (abstract). The one-output-level tolerance has no published source (searched 2026-10-08: PubMed, anti-aliasing or sub-pixel rendering with stimulus, display or psychophysics, which found only the Bach papers).

### DISP-17 — A pattern near the pixel limit (about 28-31 c/deg at the center in direct view) is allowed with a warning
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (the brainstorm notes' §7, Batch 1; offered against: refused).
- **Where:** [engine spec §10 item 3](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#10-the-drawer-n7); the brainstorm notes, [§7, Batch 1](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#7-the-drawer).
- **Basis:** PI decision. [@shannon1949communication] — the sampling theorem (§II, Theorem 1): a pixel grid represents spatial frequencies only below half its sampling rate, so 56.8 px/deg at the center gives about 28.4 c/deg and the 62.9 px/deg mean about 31.4 c/deg (arithmetic). [@merigan1990spatial] — macaque grating acuity peaks "at about 38 c/deg at the fovea" (two macaques; abstract), so patterns at this panel's limit are within what the animal resolves, which is why a warning is warranted rather than silence.
- **Caveat:** Macaque foveal grating acuity is about 38 c/deg [@merigan1990spatial]; direct view resolves about 28 c/deg at the center (about 36 through the stereoscope, by the sampling theorem [@shannon1949communication]). The rig cannot present gratings at the animal's foveal acuity limit in direct view, and artifacts near the pixel limit are within what the animal resolves; hence the warning rather than silence.

### DISP-18 — Image size is declared per stimulus, in degrees (resampled with a stated filter) or pixel for pixel; any common format, a lossy one (JPEG) with a warning
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (the brainstorm notes' §1, Batch 3; §6, Batch 1; offered against: lossless only).
- **Where:** [engine spec §4.6](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#46-images-and-movies); the brainstorm notes, [§1, Batch 3](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#1-what-can-be-shown) and [§6, Batch 1](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#6-media-and-sound).
- **Basis:** PI decision. [@ccitt1992information] — the standard: JPEG's DCT-based processes "are lossy, thereby allowing substantial compression to be achieved while producing a reconstructed image with high visual fidelity" (§4.2, "Lossy and lossless compression", p. 14), the basis for warning that a JPEG is not the pixel values its source had. Recording the resampling filter has no specific source (not searched separately).

### DISP-19 — The panel's care features (pixel shift, brightness mode, proximity sensor, variable refresh) must be off, checked at bring-up, a mismatch a warning; the OLED's maintenance cycle runs outside sessions or a session refuses to open while it is due
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, engineering (S0); the engine's check and the maintenance rule by the PI on 2026-10-07 (the brainstorm notes' §8, Batch 2).
- **Where:** [engine spec §11 items 6 and 8](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#11-lifecycle-n8-nr3); [S0 §5.1](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S0-hosts-and-hardware-design.md#51-panel-class), [§5.4](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S0-hosts-and-hardware-design.md#54-panel-acceptance-test--written-now-before-the-panel-exists) test 1 and [§8](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S0-hosts-and-hardware-design.md#8-open-items) item 3; the brainstorm notes, [§8, Batch 2](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#8-lifecycle).
- **Basis:** PI decision and engineering. [@asus2026rogb] — the maker's document: "Screen Move: pixel locations are slightly shifted periodically to help prevent burn-in"; the Neo Proximity Sensor "transitions to a black image" when the user is away (described as a function the user activates, which suggests it can stay off: UNVERIFIED until checked on a unit); "Pixel cleaning … activates automatically when the monitor is turned off" after "several hours" of use; "Uniform Brightness" lowers peak brightness to keep levels consistent across window sizes (product page, read 2026-10-08); [@asus2026rog] — the maker's document: VRR present (FreeSync Premium Pro, G-SYNC Compatible); [@dimigen2026advantages] — burn-in features were switched off and "the variable refresh rate feature was deactivated for all tests" (Methods, "Display properties and settings"); "while several screen protection features must be disabled for precise stimulation, others, such as 'pixel cleaning,' can be run between experiments" (Discussion), direct support for running the maintenance cycle outside sessions; [@poth2018ultrahigh] — with G-Sync (VRR) a frame is shown "as soon as [it has] been rendered … without having to wait for the next screen refresh" (abstract), so VRR abandons the fixed frame period the engine's timing rests on; [@saunders2014direct] — display modes such as "Dynamic Contrast" add latency and "cause unexpected time-varying changes to the contrast and brightness", and researchers should deactivate them (Discussion, "Reducing system latency"); [@abuhaila2025recent] — results depend on settings and firmware, which they report exhaustively (Abstract).

### DISP-20 — The animal's screen is black when no session runs; the pause screen is the task's background when non-black, black otherwise; the flip patch alternates through pauses
- **Decided:** 2026-09-27, the PI (the P4d-2b rulings), and 2026-10-07 (the brainstorm notes' §8, Batch 1; Batch R3).
- **Where:** [engine spec §11 items 2 and 5](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#11-lifecycle-n8-nr3); [P4d-2b spec §5.0](../superpowers/specs/2026-09-26-P4d2b-browser-console-design.md#50-rulings-pi-2026-09-27-asked-in-plain-terms) ("Rulings"); the brainstorm notes, [§8, Batch 1](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#8-lifecycle) and [Batch R3](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#the-design-reviews-questions-asked-2026-10-07).
- **Basis:** PI decision; no published basis found (searched 2026-10-08: PubMed, light or luminance adaptation between trials with background luminance in monkey or macaque; nothing that addresses the choice).

### DISP-21 — A windowed display is "development: not timing-valid"; the graphics stack is pinned per rig and V1 re-run after any change; no screen sharing during recording
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, engineering (S0 §2.4; pitfalls P4 and P4a; controller architecture §7.5; S9 §7); the windowed display's label and the warning after a graphics change by the PI on 2026-10-07 (the brainstorm notes' §8, Batch 2).
- **Where:** [engine spec §11 items 4 and 7](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#11-lifecycle-n8-nr3); [S0 §2.4](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S0-hosts-and-hardware-design.md#24-what-gets-pinned-and-re-validated); [pitfalls P4 and P4a](../pitfalls.md#expanded-notes); [controller architecture §7.5](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-controller-architecture-design.md#75-remote-and-programmatic-access); [S9 §7](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S9-operations-console-design.md#7-remote-and-one-action-launch); the brainstorm notes, [§8, Batch 2](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#8-lifecycle).
- **Basis:** Engineering and PI decision. [@bridges2020timing] — "If you measure the system and establish that the timing is good but then stop measuring further, you are still susceptible to changes introduced as the operating system or the audio/graphics card drivers are updated (e.g., Apple silently introducing a 1-frame lag …)"; seemingly innocuous OS features such as screen scaling can add a frame of lag (Discussion, "Visual lags from monitor and operating systems"); turn off unnecessary network services and memory-heavy applications while running (Discussion, "Miscellaneous"); [@plant2016reminder] — "care needs to be taken to ensure that studies can be replicated on current computer hardware and software" (abstract). No source on windowed against full-screen timing, or on screen sharing, was opened.

### DISP-22 — Before every session: a quick frame-timing check (ten seconds of flips timed by the sensor, warning past a rig-set late-frame rate) and the light-sensor test
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (the brainstorm notes' §9, Batch 1).
- **Where:** [engine spec §12 item 5](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#12-calibration-procedures-and-test-screens-n9-nr5); the brainstorm notes, [§9, Batch 1](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#9-calibration-procedures-and-test-screens).
- **Basis:** PI decision. [@plant2004selfvalidating] — benchmark paradigms "in situ and without modification" with independent hardware, and "stress the importance of such independent validation" (abstract); [@bridges2020timing] — keep measuring, because drivers and OS updates change timing silently (Discussion, "Visual lags from monitor and operating systems"). The ten-second length and the threshold are engineering choices with no source.

### DISP-23 — The display engine is chosen by V1's photodiode comparison; our own thin drawer is built now so V1 can compare it with PsychoPy
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, the PI ("decide after V1", ADR-0002), and 2026-10-07 (the engine spec's §10 item 7).
- **Where:** [ADR-0002](../design/decisions/ADR-0002-display-engine.md#decision) (Decision; ["Ruling 2026-08-31: decide after V1"](../design/decisions/ADR-0002-display-engine.md#ruling-2026-08-31-decide-after-v1)); [engine spec §10 items 4-7](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#10-the-drawer-n7) and [§3.1](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#31-three-programs-on-the-rig-pc-n-how-it-fits-n7-n8); the brainstorm notes, ["Decided before the elements"](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#decided-before-the-elements-2026-10-07) and [§7](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#7-the-drawer).
- **Basis:** PI decision. [@bridges2020timing] — the citation ADR-0002 carries, checked: PsychoPy on Ubuntu had visual-onset variability 0.34 ms and lag 4.71 ms (Psychtoolbox on Ubuntu 0.18 ms and 4.53 ms) (Table 2), measured with a Black Box Toolkit photodiode at the top center of a 60 Hz AOC LCD driven by a GTX 1050 Ti under Ubuntu 18.04 (Methods), so the numbers are right but describe that LCD system, not a 240 Hz OLED; the paper stresses "the importance of scientists making their own timing validation measurements for their own stimuli and computer configuration" (Abstract), the basis for choosing by V1; [@peirce2019psychopy] — PsychoPy is "an application for the creation of experiments … with precise spatial control and timing of stimuli" (abstract; the reference a methods section cites if V1 picks PsychoPy).

### DISP-24 — Display gates for M2 (proposed): onset variability below 1 ms and drops below 0.1% over a 2 h stress run
- **Decided:** 2026-08-30, proposed in the roadmap (engineering; not ratified).
- **Where:** [roadmap M2](../roadmap.md#m2--display-validated-on-rig-hardware).
- **Basis:** Engineering default. [@bridges2020timing] — the best lab packages had mean visual-onset variability under 1 ms (Table 2; Abstract: Psychtoolbox, PsychoPy, Presentation and E-Prime "all with mean precision under 1 millisecond"), so the 1 ms gate is achievable by known software. The 0.1% drop gate has no source.

### DISP-25 — The flip patch alternates every refresh unconditionally (a frame clock); the task patch toggles on every change, never for live-driven or frame-dependent content
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, the patch roles, fixed by wl-sync's copper (S3); 2026-10-07, the PI (the brainstorm notes' §5, Batch 1; Batch R3).
- **Where:** [engine spec §8 item 1](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#8-sync-and-evidence-n5-nr3-nr5); [S4 §7](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S4-stimulus-presentation-design.md#7-photodiode-patches-are-a-display-module-obligation); [S3 §4](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S3-sync-integration-design.md#4-our-obligations-to-the-sync-fabric) item 4 and [§8](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S3-sync-integration-design.md#8-photodiode-patches-cameras-and-reconstruction); the brainstorm notes, [§5, Batch 1](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#5-sync-and-evidence) and [Batch R3](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#the-design-reviews-questions-asked-2026-10-07).
- **Basis:** PI decision and wl-sync's hardware. Photodiode verification of onsets is standard practice ([@bridges2020timing], Methods: a photodiode "positioned at the center top of the display"; [@saunders2014direct], Introduction and Discussion, photodiode-based latency methods). No published source was found for a patch that alternates on every refresh (a web search for a photodiode "sync square" every frame found only the Allen SDK's documentation of one that changes every 60 frames, not opened as a source).

### DISP-26 — Photodiode patches sit outside both eyes' viewports (a flickering flip patch would be a distractor): a bottom strip made by the stereoscope's mask, and in direct view under each sensor's opaque housing near (±30°, −18°), outside the ±15° stimulus region; verified dark from the eye position at bring-up
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, engineering (S4, S3, the optics drawing); the mask by the PI on 2026-09-28, and the housings in the direct-view spec the same day. S3 §10 item 3 named a central strip until its correction on 2026-10-08 (inventory disagreement 15).
- **Where:** [S4 §7](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S4-stimulus-presentation-design.md#7-photodiode-patches-are-a-display-module-obligation); [S3 §8](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S3-sync-integration-design.md#8-photodiode-patches-cameras-and-reconstruction); [optics drawing §5](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-stereoscope-optics-drawing.md#5-the-field-stop-and-the-photodiode-strip) and [§7](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-stereoscope-optics-drawing.md#7-build-and-verification-checklist); [direct-view spec §4](../superpowers/specs/2026-09-28-direct-view-design.md#4-the-screen-timing-light-sensors) and [§7](../superpowers/specs/2026-09-28-direct-view-design.md#7-verification-additions); [controller architecture §3.3](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-controller-architecture-design.md#33-what-the-two-photodiode-patches-change); [M0 review §3.2](../M0-REVIEW.md#32-rig-and-animals-5) item 9.
- **Basis:** Engineering default, supported by [@williams2004entrainment] — in macaque V1, spikes phase-locked to a 60 Hz and a 100 Hz video refresh although "humans are perceptually oblivious to this flicker" (abstract), so a flickering patch in view could drive visual neurons even above flicker fusion; at 240 Hz the flip patch alternates at 120 Hz, at 120 Hz at 60 Hz (arithmetic); [@yantis1984abrupt] — "an abrupt onset in a visual display would capture visual attention" (abstract; human observers), and the task patch's event-locked toggles are such onsets. Both support keeping the patches out of view.

### DISP-27 — A state can wait for photodiode-confirmed onset (`Onscreen`); a missing task-patch edge after a scene that should produce one is a recorded fault (`PHOTODIODE_MISSING`), never a silence
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, engineering (S1a, S6, S2, the controller architecture).
- **Where:** [S1a §2](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S1a-vocabulary-design.md#2-guards); [S6 §3](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S6-io-subsystem-design.md#3-digital-input-change-detection-not-polling); [S2 §5.1](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S2-event-vocabulary-design.md#51-in-wl-exptasks-ranges-ours-to-allocate-no-amendment-required); [controller architecture §5.4](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-controller-architecture-design.md#54-state-primitives-that-are-not-obvious).
- **Basis:** Engineering default. [@bridges2020timing] — a flip reported by software does not prove the screen changed: "we can at least detect when the graphics card has flipped a frame buffer (and then hope that the screen updated reasonably quickly after that)" (Discussion, "Audio timing"), and monitor-side delays need hardware to detect (Discussion, "Visual lags from monitor and operating systems"); [@plant2004selfvalidating] — independent hardware validation of presentation timing (abstract).

### DISP-28 — Latency budgets, as requirements: eye sample to decision within one frame plus the staleness ceiling; decision to display on the next flip; saccade onset to display change inside saccadic suppression
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, engineering (the controller architecture).
- **Where:** [controller architecture §14](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-controller-architecture-design.md#14-latency-budgets--requirements-not-measurements).
- **Basis:** Engineering default. [@ressmeyer2026openirisdpi] — the figures the controller architecture carries, checked: frame processing "1.1±0.1 ms (median ± IQR)", occasionally up to 50 ms, "infrequent (2% of frames ≥ 10 ms)"; the analog output added 3–4 ms, "which may limit the use of this signal for gaze-contingent applications" (§3.1); [@ross2001changes] — saccades trigger "a suppression of visual sensitivity, specific to the magnocellular pathway" (abstract; a review including monkey physiology), the published basis the controller architecture left uncited; [@saunders2014direct] — a target of under 25 ms mean latency at screen midpoint for masking central vision, and latency measured end to end (Discussion, "What is an acceptable level of system latency?"); the "5–25 ms" within which they say suppression lessens is their summary of other studies, not opened here, so it is not cited as theirs; [@dimigen2026advantages] — the challenge is "timing the display change so that it happens during the eye movement, while the viewer's visual thresholds are elevated" (Introduction, "Practical test 2"); on a 240 Hz OLED the change reached more than 90% luminance 7.61 ms after online saccade detection, under 13 ms from saccade onset (Discussion, "Temporal properties support fast, time-critical paradigms"; one human observer, an author).

### DISP-29 — The screen is fixed 50 cm from the eyes, physically, in both setups, on a locked stand, measured at setup and re-checked
- **Decided:** 2026-09-28, the PI (44, 50 and 57 cm were shown). The spec map and controller architecture §16 item 3 said 57 cm until their correction on 2026-10-08 (inventory disagreement 1).
- **Where:** [direct-view spec §1](../superpowers/specs/2026-09-28-direct-view-design.md#1-decisions-pi-2026-09-28-asked-in-plain-terms); [S0 §5.1](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S0-hosts-and-hardware-design.md#51-panel-class), [§5.2](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S0-hosts-and-hardware-design.md#52-geometry-as-a-formula) and [§5.5](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S0-hosts-and-hardware-design.md#55-direct-view); [optics drawing §1](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-stereoscope-optics-drawing.md#1-inputs); [S4 §7](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S4-stimulus-presentation-design.md#7-photodiode-patches-are-a-display-module-obligation); [architecture, "The display"](../design/architecture.md#the-display-direct-view-and-stereo-as-viewports); [`tasks/rig.py`](../../tasks/rig.py).
- **Basis:** PI decision: one screen at one place, the stereoscope removable ("I want the screen to be the same physical distance from the animal in stereoscope and the direct viewing. The stereoscope is a device that is removable and everything else is fixed", S0 §5.2). No published basis was needed or sought; the 57 cm convention is a small-angle identity (1/tan 1° = 57.29), not a finding.

### DISP-30 — Direct view's field is the whole panel at Z: ±30.5° × ±18.4° (62.9 px/deg mean, 56.8 at the center), computed from the maker's active area, less the sensors' housings
- **Decided:** 2026-09-28, engineering arithmetic.
- **Where:** [S0 §5.2](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S0-hosts-and-hardware-design.md#52-geometry-as-a-formula) and [§5.5](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S0-hosts-and-hardware-design.md#55-direct-view); [direct-view spec §2](../superpowers/specs/2026-09-28-direct-view-design.md#2-geometry-per-setup).
- **Basis:** Arithmetic on the maker's figures. [@asus2026rog] — the maker's document: "Display Viewing Area (HxV): 589.97 x 332.93 mm", "Pixel Pitch: 0.153mm", 3840 × 2160 (spec page, read 2026-10-08). Checked: atan(294.985/500) = 30.54°, atan(166.465/500) = 18.41°; 1920 px / 30.54° = 62.9 px/deg; at the center 500 mm × tan 1° / 0.15364 mm = 56.8 px/deg.

### DISP-31 — Most experiments, and the first animal task, run in direct view; the operator picks the setup at session start with no default and no presence switch; a task declares `view`
- **Decided:** 2026-09-27 and 2026-09-28, the PI.
- **Where:** [direct-view spec §1](../superpowers/specs/2026-09-28-direct-view-design.md#1-decisions-pi-2026-09-28-asked-in-plain-terms) and [§3](../superpowers/specs/2026-09-28-direct-view-design.md#3-which-setup-a-session-runs-in); [S0 §5.5](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S0-hosts-and-hardware-design.md#55-direct-view); [`tasks/fixation_detection.py`](../../tasks/fixation_detection.py) (`view`).
- **Basis:** PI decision (the program's design and the rig's operation); no published basis.

## Stereo geometry and vergence

### STE-01 — Positions are cyclopean degrees and a task never names a pixel; each setup maps degrees by `D · tan` per axis, so each field is a rectangle in degrees
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, engineering default (S4 §2); restated as settled in the engine brainstorm, 2026-10-07.
- **Where:** [S4 §2](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S4-stimulus-presentation-design.md#2-coordinates-cyclopean-degrees-and-nothing-else-crosses-a-boundary), "Coordinates"; [S1 §2.4](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S1-task-model-design.md#24-stimuli); [S5 §6](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S5-eye-tracking-design.md#6-the-gaze-mapping-is-one-versioned-object); [architecture, "The display"](../design/architecture.md#the-display-direct-view-and-stereo-as-viewports); the brainstorm notes, [§2, "Settled before the element"](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#2-where).
- **Basis:** Engineering default (a coordinate convention). No published source for the convention was found (searched 2026-10-08: PubMed, "cyclopean eye" AND (visual direction OR egocentric direction); the hits, Erkelens & van Ee 2002 and Ono, Mapp & Howard 2002, debate whether a cyclopean egocenter exists, which does not bear on using the midpoint of the eyes as a coordinate origin, so neither is entered). The `D · tan` mapping is geometry.

### STE-02 — A split-screen mirror stereoscope on one panel: two viewports on one framebuffer, one flip, no genlock; panel left-right nonuniformity is an interocular mismatch that biases binocular combination, so both halves are photometered
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, the PI (controller architecture D6); amended 2026-09-28 (direct view is the other setup).
- **Where:** [controller architecture §2](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-controller-architecture-design.md#2-decisions-taken-2026-08-31) (D6) and [§8.2](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-controller-architecture-design.md#82-stereo-as-viewports); [M0 review §1](../M0-REVIEW.md#1-the-decisions-this-rests-on); [architecture, "The display"](../design/architecture.md#the-display-direct-view-and-stereo-as-viewports); [S4 §9](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S4-stimulus-presentation-design.md#9-stimulus-calibration-and-the-id-the-manifest-already-wants).
- **Basis:** PI decision, with published precedent. [@wheatstone1838contributions] — the mirror stereoscope itself: two plane mirrors, one before each eye, each reflecting its own picture (pp. 374–375, figs. 8–9; full text, archive.org scan). [@cox2019temporal] — macaque V1 work in which "images on the right side of the forward-positioned display were viewed by the right eye and images on the left by the left eye", through a custom mirror stereoscope, the monitor divided by a black nonreflective septum (Methods, "Visual stimulation"; full text). [@mitchell2022stimulating] — the same single-display split-screen arrangement (STAR Methods, "Visual apparatus"; full text). [@dougherty2021binocular] — the same, with infrared-passing cold mirrors, "two pairs of mirrors angled so that the subjects fused the left and right sides of the display" (Materials and Methods, "Visual display", and Fig. 1A; full text). [@carmel2010how] — the split-screen mirror stereoscope as a standard method for presenting a different image to each eye (Protocol §2, "Mirror stereoscope", Fig. 3; full text). For the photometry half of the decision: [@ding2006gaincontrol] — in binocular combination each eye exerts gain control on the other in proportion to its own contrast energy, so gratings differing in contrast between the eyes shift the perceived (cyclopean) phase toward the stronger eye (abstract). That an uncorrected left-right panel difference acts this way is our inference from it; the paper tests contrast, not mean-luminance, differences.

### STE-03 — Angles use the math convention (0° right, counter-clockwise positive, +y up) for positions, motion and orientation; a bar at 0° is horizontal; a grating's orientation names its bars and its drift direction is a separate angle; a direction not across the bars is refused
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (the brainstorm notes' §2, Batch 1; the drifting-grating ruling in "Build A's plans").
- **Where:** [engine spec §5 item 1](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#5-where-n2-nr5); the brainstorm notes, [§2, Batch 1](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#2-where), and ["Build A's plans"](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#build-as-plans-asked-2026-10-07-while-planning) (the drifting grating).
- **Basis:** PI decision. The angle convention is a convention, with no published source sought; none of the papers read for this area states one. The refusal has a published basis: [@adelson1982phenomenal] — "When a moving grating is viewed through an aperture, only motion orthogonal to its bars is visible, as motion parallel to the bars causes no change in the stimulus" (abstract, first sentence); a drift direction that is not across the bars therefore cannot be shown as itself.

### STE-04 — (0°, 0°) is straight ahead; the rig file records where that falls on the screen (default its center)
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (the brainstorm notes' §2, Batch 1, "Straight ahead").
- **Where:** [engine spec §5 item 1](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#5-where-n2-nr5); the brainstorm notes, [§2, Batch 1](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#2-where).
- **Basis:** PI decision (a convention). No published basis was sought beyond the papers read for this area, none of which states one.

### STE-05 — One size number is the full width (diameter, side length, full bar length); each block's definition states it
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (the brainstorm notes' §2, Batch 4, "Full width").
- **Where:** [engine spec §5 item 2](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#5-where-n2-nr5); the brainstorm notes, [§2, Batch 4](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#2-where).
- **Basis:** PI decision (a convention). No published basis was sought beyond the papers read for this area, none of which states one.

### STE-06 — Away from the center a stimulus keeps true visual angle by default (a grating keeps its c/deg), or the screen center's scale, declared per task (at 30° out a degree covers 15–33% more screen)
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (the brainstorm notes' §2: Batch 1, "Declared per task"; Batch 2, "True visual angle" as the default).
- **Where:** [engine spec §5 item 3](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#5-where-n2-nr5); the brainstorm notes, [§2, Batches 1 and 2](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#2-where).
- **Basis:** PI decision; common practice found in mouse work, not macaque. [@marshel2011functional] — on a flat display 10 cm from the eye, "spherical altitude correction was applied to sinusoidal gratings to hold SF and TF constant throughout the visual field" (Experimental Procedures, "Visual Stimulation"; full text). The 15–33% is geometry: on a flat screen one degree at eccentricity e spans 1/cos e (tangential) to 1/cos² e (radial) times the central extent, 1.155 and 1.333 at 30° (our arithmetic; it matches the documents).

### STE-07 — Disparity is in degrees, near (crossed) negative; left eye at x − d/2, right eye at x + d/2; the sign is verified in an animal by its vergence response to a disparity step
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (the brainstorm notes' §2, Batch 1, "Near is negative"); the per-eye formula is pinned in the engine spec §5 item 4; the vergence check is kept from S4 §10 item 6 (2026-08-31).
- **Where:** [engine spec §5 item 4](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#5-where-n2-nr5); the brainstorm notes, [§2, Batch 1](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#2-where); [S4 §2](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S4-stimulus-presentation-design.md#2-coordinates-cyclopean-degrees-and-nothing-else-crosses-a-boundary) and [§10 item 6](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S4-stimulus-presentation-design.md#10-test-screens).
- **Basis:** PI decision, matching one of two conventions in the macaque literature. [@tanabe2004rejection] — "negative and positive values refer to crossed and uncrossed disparities, respectively" (Materials and Methods, "Electrophysiology"; full text): the convention chosen here. [@cumming1999binocular] — the opposite: "positive disparities are crossed (near), and negative disparities are uncrossed (far)" (Materials and Methods; full text). The formula's sign is geometry: with d < 0 the left eye's image lies right of the right eye's, which is crossed (our check; it matches the engine spec §5). For the vergence check: [@busettini1996shortlatency] — in three rhesus monkeys, disparity steps applied to large dichoptic patterns elicited vergence at about 52 ms without training; responses to crossed steps "were always in the correct (i.e., convergent) direction", but responses to uncrossed steps were divergent only for small steps (< 1.5–2.5°) soon after a saccade and reversed to convergence for larger ones (abstract, items 2, 4 and 5).
- **Caveat:** Crossed-negative, as here, follows [@tanabe2004rejection]; Cumming and Parker use crossed-positive [@cumming1999binocular]. A methods section states the sign, and a comparison with the Cumming-Parker papers flips it.

### STE-08 — A stimulus may sit at a different position in each eye (rivalry, nonius lines, fusion tests), each per-eye position being that eye's direction after the vergence offset; both per-eye positions and a disparity on one stimulus are refused
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (the brainstorm notes' §2, Batch 3, "Free per-eye positions").
- **Where:** [engine spec §5 item 4](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#5-where-n2-nr5); the brainstorm notes, [§2, Batch 3](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#2-where).
- **Basis:** PI decision; the uses it names are published. [@leopold1996activity] — monkeys reported the perceived orientation of rival orthogonal gratings, one per eye (abstract). [@carmel2010how] — nonius lines ("lines that center on the image from several directions") as a binocular element for stable vergence (Protocol §2, Fig. 1D), and a divider so each eye sees only its own image (Protocol §2, Fig. 4; full text). [@dougherty2021binocular] — a fusion test in a macaque split-screen stereoscope: "showing a central fixation cue to the left eye and right eye in alternation, monitoring for any change in eye position" (Materials and Methods, "Visual display"; full text). The refusal of both per-eye positions and a disparity is engineering (one stimulus, one way to specify it).

### STE-09 — Monocular and dichoptic presentation are first-class (`eye` left/right/both); `eye` other than both, any disparity, or a random-dot stereogram requires `view="stereoscope"`
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, engineering default (S1a §4.1); the setup rule follows the PI's direct-view decision of 2026-09-28.
- **Where:** [S1a §4.1](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S1a-vocabulary-design.md#41-eye-is-not-an-afterthought); [direct-view spec §3](../superpowers/specs/2026-09-28-direct-view-design.md#3-which-setup-a-session-runs-in); [architecture, "The display"](../design/architecture.md#the-display-direct-view-and-stereo-as-viewports).
- **Basis:** Engineering default; the uses are published. [@hubel1968receptive] — macaque V1 is organized in ocular dominance columns, and in layer IV most cells are driven by one eye only (abstract), which is what monocular receptive-field mapping measures. [@leopold1996activity] — binocular rivalry in monkeys (abstract). [@dougherty2021binocular] — monocular against binocular stimulation to measure interocular suppression in macaque LGN, through a split-screen stereoscope (Materials and Methods; full text). [@mitchell2022stimulating] — gratings to the left eye, the right eye, or both (Results, Fig. 1B; full text). Why the stereoscope is required: [@carmel2010how] — with red-blue goggles "there will always be at least some 'bleed-through' - each eye will see some of the other eye's image", whereas mirrors deliver a different image to each eye (Protocol §2; full text); a single direct-view screen cannot separate the eyes at all (our inference).

### STE-10 — A random-dot stereogram carries `correlation` +1, 0 or −1 (anticorrelation as the control); disparity-defined form (`Corrugation`, `Slant`) is a disparity field; a stereogram declared for one eye is refused
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, settled in S1a §10: engineering, with a published basis.
- **Where:** [S1a §10](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S1a-vocabulary-design.md#10-settled-2026-08-31--disparity-defined-form), "Settled 2026-08-31 — disparity-defined form".
- **Basis:** Engineering (splitting a stereogram's appearance, `RDS` with its `correlation` field, from disparity-defined form, `Corrugation` and `Slant`), on published stimuli. [@julesz1960binocular] — computer-generated stereo pairs "When viewed monocularly … appear completely random, but if viewed binocularly, certain correlated point domains are seen in depth" (abstract, p. 1125): the basis for refusing a one-eye stereogram; and a pair in which one picture is the negative of the other ("all points are complemented") cannot be fused and rivals (pp. 1140–1141, Figs. 15–16; full text, archive.org scan): an anticorrelated stereogram (the paper makes no claim to be the first). [@cumming1997responses] — anticorrelated random-dot stereograms give no depth percept, yet disparity-selective V1 neurons signal their disparity (abstract): why anticorrelation is the control. [@tanabe2004rejection] — the full control set in one macaque design: correlated and anticorrelated RDS at nine disparities (for the first monkey; thirteen for later cells), left-eye-only and right-eye-only presentations, and a binocularly uncorrelated RDS (Materials and Methods, "Electrophysiology"), the anticorrelated one made by reversing dot contrast in one eye only ("Task and visual stimulation"; full text). [@nienborg2004receptive] — RDS "in which disparity varied as a sinusoidal function of vertical position ('corrugations')" in awake macaques (abstract; full text read). [@nguyenkim2003disparitybased] — planar surfaces "depicted by random-dot stereograms containing a linear gradient of horizontal disparities", parameterized by tilt and slant, in macaque MT (abstract; full text read).

### STE-11 — On the stereoscope each eye may have its own background (default the same for both)
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (the brainstorm notes' §1, Batch 7).
- **Where:** [engine spec §4.4](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#44-combining-grouping-and-layouts); the brainstorm notes, [§1, Batch 7](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#1-what-can-be-shown).
- **Basis:** PI decision. No source for per-eye backgrounds was found. Beside the default, though neither discusses backgrounds: [@carmel2010how] — "vergence-stabilizing identical elements include a fixation point … and a frame around the images", and the frame "can have any shape as long as it is identical in both eyes" (Protocol §2, Fig. 1; full text), while allowing that a frame may be undesirable on a uniform background; [@cox2019temporal] — in the macaque split-screen stereoscope, "To facilitate fusion, an oval aperture was displayed at the edge of each half-screen" (Methods, "Visual stimulation"; full text). Both put identical elements in the two eyes (frames, fixation points, nonius lines, an aperture); that identical backgrounds help fusion is our inference from that, not their claim.

### STE-12 — Through the stereoscope the optical path is D = Z + HW − E, 63.15 cm at E = 1.6 cm (62.85–63.25 cm over IPD 30–38 mm): field ±13.15° × ±14.77°, 73 px/deg mean (71.7 px/deg, 0.84 arcmin per pixel, at the center); the 1 cm ≈ 1° convention does not hold
- **Decided:** 2026-09-28, engineering, following the PI's fixed physical distance Z = 50 cm.
- **Where:** [S0 §5.2](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S0-hosts-and-hardware-design.md#52-geometry-as-a-formula); [optics drawing §1](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-stereoscope-optics-drawing.md#1-inputs), [§3](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-stereoscope-optics-drawing.md#3-the-numbers-that-follow) and [§4.4](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-stereoscope-optics-drawing.md#44-the-screen-is-fixed-the-path-follows); [direct-view spec §2](../superpowers/specs/2026-09-28-direct-view-design.md#2-geometry-per-setup).
- **Basis:** Geometry and arithmetic; no published basis needed. Re-derived here: D = 50 + 14.749 − 1.6 = 63.149 cm; atan(14.749/63.149) = 13.15°; atan(16.647/63.149) = 14.77°; 0.015364/63.149 rad = 0.836 arcmin; 1920 px / (2 × 13.15°) = 73.0 px/deg over the field, and 631.49 mm × tan 1° / 0.15364 mm = 71.7 px/deg at the center (our arithmetic; all match).

### STE-13 — The stereoscope needs ±10°; a removable matte mask at the panel sets the field, starting at ±12°, with mirrors cut for the full viewport; a stimulus may pass the field's edge only when declared
- **Decided:** 2026-09-27, the PI (±10°), and 2026-09-28 (the mask).
- **Where:** [S0 §5.2](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S0-hosts-and-hardware-design.md#52-geometry-as-a-formula); [optics drawing §5](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-stereoscope-optics-drawing.md#5-the-field-stop-and-the-photodiode-strip); [S4 §7](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S4-stimulus-presentation-design.md#7-photodiode-patches-are-a-display-module-obligation); [S3 §8](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S3-sync-integration-design.md#8-photodiode-patches-cameras-and-reconstruction); [engine spec §5 item 8](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#5-where-n2-nr5); [`rig.py`](../../tasks/rig.py) (`mask_deg`).
- **Basis:** PI decision. Common practice, weakly: [@dougherty2021binocular] — the per-eye stereoscope calibration in a macaque split-screen rig placed targets "up to 10°" from each half's center (Materials and Methods, "Visual display"; full text), so ±10° per eye is a field in use. No source for the mask was sought; it is engineering.

### STE-14 — Two first-surface mirrors at 45° per eye (two reflections keep parity); second-surface mirrors excluded (a ghost is an uncorrelated second image to one eye); faces 45° ± 0.25°; everything matte black and baffled
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, engineering default (the optics drawing).
- **Where:** [optics drawing §2](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-stereoscope-optics-drawing.md#2-the-arrangement-a-periscope-per-eye) and [§7](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-stereoscope-optics-drawing.md#7-build-and-verification-checklist), "Build and verification checklist".
- **Basis:** Engineering default, with published practice. [@carmel2010how] — "A mirror stereoscope consists of four mirrors": two near the eyes at 45° to each line of sight, and one on either side of them facing the stimuli at 45° (Protocol §2, Fig. 3; full text). [@dougherty2021binocular] — "two pairs of mirrors" in a macaque rig (Fig. 1A legend; full text). [@edmundoptics2026what] — manufacturer page: with a second-surface mirror "there are often ghost images due to two reflections (one from the glass, one from the coating)" (full text; undated page, so its `year` is the year read). That two reflections preserve image parity is plane-mirror optics, not taken from a source here. The ± 0.25° tolerance has no source.

### STE-15 — Symmetric nasal and temporal field per eye, which fixes the near mirror at a = E(1 + 1/tan θ): "the thing least likely to surprise an analysis"
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, the PI (the rule corrected 2026-09-28).
- **Where:** [optics drawing §4](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-stereoscope-optics-drawing.md#4-adjustability-what-moves-and-the-rule-it-holds), "Adjustability"; [M0 review §3.2](../M0-REVIEW.md#32-rig-and-animals-5) item 7.
- **Basis:** PI decision; the rule is geometry. No published source: Carmel et al., the only stereoscope build guide read [@carmel2010how], does not discuss the nasal and temporal split.

### STE-16 — A matte midline divider blocks rays past the mirror ridge, which would otherwise show each eye the other viewport's outer 2E
- **Decided:** 2026-09-28, the PI.
- **Where:** [optics drawing §4.2](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-stereoscope-optics-drawing.md#42-the-ridge-is-not-a-stop).
- **Basis:** PI decision, with a published precedent. [@carmel2010how] — "in many cases each eye will also have a line of vision to the other image … To block this undesired line of vision, place a divider … extending from the stereoscope's midline, between the observer's eyes, toward the center of the display"; "it is preferable to make the divider out of matte material, as shiny material will reflect light emitted by the monitor and create glare" (Protocol §2, Fig. 4; full text). [@dougherty2021binocular] — in a macaque rig, "A black, non-reflective septum was placed between the monitor and the back side of the mirrors … to prevent light scatter from one side of the monitor to the opposing eye" (Materials and Methods, "Visual display"; full text); same purpose, a different position from the optics drawing's divider (which runs from the ridge toward the face).

### STE-17 — The ridge clip is soft (0.84° of fade per mm of pupil); a pupil wider than about 2.9 mm sees the ±12° mask's nasal edge dimmed; the 5 mm mirror margin covers a pupil to 5.4 mm; the macaque pupil under the rig's luminance is a bring-up measurement
- **Decided:** 2026-09-28, engineering arithmetic (the optics drawing); the pupil left UNVERIFIED.
- **Where:** [optics drawing §3.1](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-stereoscope-optics-drawing.md#31-mirror-sizes-the-45-footprint-is-a-trapezoid), [§4.2](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-stereoscope-optics-drawing.md#42-the-ridge-is-not-a-stop) and [§8](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-stereoscope-optics-drawing.md#8-what-is-still-open) item 8.
- **Basis:** Arithmetic, re-derived: 1 mm / 68.5 mm = 0.84°; 2(16 − 68.5 · tan 12°) = 2.9 mm; √2/(1 − tan 13.15°) = 1.85 (our arithmetic; all match). No published absolute diameter of the alert macaque's pupil at a known luminance was found (searched 2026-10-08, PubMed and Europe PMC: Clarke, Zhang & Gamlin 2003 and Pong & Fuchs 2000 give pupil dynamics but no absolute diameters in their abstracts, and the journal refused the full text; Gamlin et al. 2007 was not served in full; the abstract of Ebitz, Pearson & Platt 2014 gives none; the only millimeter value in the full text of Ostrin et al. 2018 is a pharmacologically dilated pupil, about 8.5 mm). It stays a bring-up measurement, as the documents say.

### STE-18 — Vergence is set in software as a constant horizontal offset, 2·atan(E/D) = 2.9° at E = 1.6 cm (2.7–3.5° over IPD), never by angling the mirrors; without it a zero-disparity stimulus is seen at optical infinity while accommodation sits at the optical path
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, engineering default (the optics drawing §6). The controller architecture §8.2 and S0 §5.2 said mirror angles set vergence until their correction on 2026-10-08.
- **Where:** [optics drawing §6](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-stereoscope-optics-drawing.md#6-vergence-is-a-software-constant-not-a-mechanical-one); [S4 §2](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S4-stimulus-presentation-design.md#2-coordinates-cyclopean-degrees-and-nothing-else-crosses-a-boundary); [architecture, "The display"](../design/architecture.md#the-display-direct-view-and-stereo-as-viewports); [engine spec §5 item 4](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#5-where-n2-nr5).
- **Basis:** Engineering default; the reason for an offset is published. [@hoffman2008vergenceaccommodation] — "the uncoupling of vergence and accommodation required by 3D displays frequently reduces one's ability to fuse the binocular stimulus and causes discomfort and fatigue" (abstract; human observers). [@cumming1986disparityinduced] — monkeys tracking a haploscopic target "showed only a modest ability to separate their accommodation and vergence responses when presented with conflicting blur and disparity cues" (abstract): in macaques too, putting zero disparity at the screen's optical distance avoids a standing conflict. Mirror angle is the other published way to set vergence: [@cumming1999binocular] rotated the haploscope mirrors on galvanometers to set vergence (Materials and Methods; full text) and [@carmel2010how] adjusts mirror angles "to attain stable vergence" (Protocol §2; full text); neither argues against a software offset. Re-derived: 2·atan(1.6/63.149) = 2.90°; 2.72° and 3.46° at IPD 30 and 38 mm (our arithmetic).
- **Caveat:** Accommodative amplitude is not the limit (young rhesus have at least 17-18 D voluntary [@smith1984behavioral]), but monkeys separate accommodation from vergence only modestly under conflicting cues [@cumming1986disparityinduced]. At about 0.55 D of vergence demand per degree of disparity, a large disparity in the stereoscope may pull accommodation off the screen and blur the stimulus; the software vergence offset removes the conflict at zero disparity only. No macaque measurement of the blur cost was found.

### STE-19 — In direct view vergence and accommodation agree; in the stereoscope a disparity δ shifts vergence by about δ/IPD (0.55 D per degree at 32 mm); rhesus accommodative amplitude far exceeds the 1.3–2.2 D demand
- **Decided:** 2026-09-27, a research note (the panel comparison §7); no decision was taken on it.
- **Where:** [panel comparison §7](../research/2026-09-27-panel-27-vs-32.md#7-what-the-viewing-distance-costs-direct-view).
- **Basis:** Published. [@hoffman2008vergenceaccommodation] — the quote the panel comparison carries is in the abstract, as stated. [@bito1982agedependent] — "The mean accommodative amplitude of 1- to 5-year-old rhesus monkeys was a remarkable 34 D, while animals over 25 years of age averaged 5 D" (abstract); note this is drug-induced (cholinomimetic) maximal amplitude, not voluntary accommodation. [@smith1984behavioral] — measured behaviorally, "young monkeys have at least 17 to 18 D of voluntary accommodation" (abstract; two young rhesus): the better source for "far exceeds the demand" in alert animals. 0.55 D per degree: (π/180)/0.032 m = 0.545 m⁻¹ (our arithmetic; matches). [@cumming1986disparityinduced] — see STE-18 and the caveat: amplitude is not the limit; coupling is.
- **Caveat:** Accommodative amplitude is not the limit (young rhesus have at least 17-18 D voluntary [@smith1984behavioral]), but monkeys separate accommodation from vergence only modestly under conflicting cues [@cumming1986disparityinduced]. At about 0.55 D of vergence demand per degree of disparity, a large disparity in the stereoscope may pull accommodation off the screen and blur the stimulus; the software vergence offset removes the conflict at zero disparity only. No macaque measurement of the blur cost was found.

### STE-20 — The optics are adjustable per animal: half-IPD E comes from a per-animal settings file; the stereoscope is built for IPD 30–38 mm (an animal outside it is refused; nominal E 1.6 cm, not a measurement); IPD is measured per animal
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, the PI (adjustable), and 2026-09-29 (the settings file); the 30-38 mm range has no stated basis.
- **Where:** [optics drawing §1](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-stereoscope-optics-drawing.md#1-inputs) and [§4](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-stereoscope-optics-drawing.md#4-adjustability-what-moves-and-the-rule-it-holds); [S0 §7.1.3](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S0-hosts-and-hardware-design.md#713-the-ipd-adjustment-system-requirements); [direct-view spec §2](../superpowers/specs/2026-09-28-direct-view-design.md#2-geometry-per-setup); [M0 review §3.2](../M0-REVIEW.md#32-rig-and-animals-5) item 7; [`rig.py`](../../tasks/rig.py) (`half_ipd_range_cm`); [`reference_subject.py`](../../tasks/reference_subject.py).
- **Basis:** PI decision; per-animal measurement is published practice and the range can be checked against published values. [@walton2019rhesus] — "the small interpupillary distance in rhesus monkeys (typically 25–35 mm)" (Methods, "Data Analysis"; stated without a source) and "We measured the interpupillary distances of both monkeys to be approximately 34 mm" (Results, "Fixation of a Full Field Near Target"; full text). [@hadjidimitrakis2011fix] — "The interocular distance of the monkeys M1 and M2 was 35 mm and 32 mm" (Materials and Methods, "Behavioral task"; full text). [@ferrea2025frontal] — "the interpupillary distance of the monkeys was measured (Monkey Y = 33.2 mm, Monkey Z = 33.1 mm). The setup software and screen projections were then calibrated to these individual values" (Materials and methods, "Stereoscopic 3D virtual reality setup"; full text). [@wirth2017gazeinformed] — disparity calibrated "using the actual interpupillary distance of the monkeys (3.1 cm for monkey K and 3.0 cm for monkey S)" (Materials and methods, "Behavioral methods and setup"; full text). [@cumming1999binocular] — convergence of 2.25° and 2.32° at 89 cm (Materials and Methods; full text), which implies IPDs of about 35 and 36 mm (our arithmetic). Ten animals in these sources span about 30–36 mm.

### STE-21 — Each eye's optical path, viewport center, deg/px and vergence offset are measured (V9), not derived; until then computed from the rig file; a change (a carriage moved for another animal) is a discontinuity like a parameter change and invalidates the gaze map
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, engineering default (S4, the controller architecture, the optics drawing).
- **Where:** [S4 §2](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S4-stimulus-presentation-design.md#2-coordinates-cyclopean-degrees-and-nothing-else-crosses-a-boundary) and [§9](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S4-stimulus-presentation-design.md#9-stimulus-calibration-and-the-id-the-manifest-already-wants); [S0 §5.2](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S0-hosts-and-hardware-design.md#52-geometry-as-a-formula); [optics drawing §4.4](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-stereoscope-optics-drawing.md#44-the-screen-is-fixed-the-path-follows), [§4.5](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-stereoscope-optics-drawing.md#45-the-consequence-for-operations) and [§7](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-stereoscope-optics-drawing.md#7-build-and-verification-checklist); [controller architecture §8.2](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-controller-architecture-design.md#82-stereo-as-viewports); [S5 §6](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S5-eye-tracking-design.md#6-the-gaze-mapping-is-one-versioned-object).
- **Basis:** Engineering default. Partial published practice: macaque split-screen rigs re-check the stereoscope's alignment "At the beginning of each experimental session" [@cox2019temporal] (Methods, "Visual stimulation"; full text), [@dougherty2021binocular] (Materials and Methods, "Visual display"; full text). Measuring each eye's path rather than deriving it has no source in the papers read.

### STE-22 — A per-eye alignment/vergence target (Nonius or vernier) is run at every session start on the stereoscope, its residual recorded and checked against a tolerance in preflight
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, engineering default; superseded in the engine spec by STE-23 (inventory disagreement 5; the engine spec's §22 lists the S4 §10 amendment as pending).
- **Where:** [S4 §10](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S4-stimulus-presentation-design.md#10-test-screens) item 1; [S9 §2](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S9-operations-console-design.md#2-preflight) and [§6](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S9-operations-console-design.md#6-test-screens); [optics drawing §4.5](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-stereoscope-optics-drawing.md#45-the-consequence-for-operations) and [§7](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-stereoscope-optics-drawing.md#7-build-and-verification-checklist); [controller architecture §11.3](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-controller-architecture-design.md#113-console); [roadmap M5](../roadmap.md#m5--operations-complete).
- **Basis:** Engineering default. Every-session timing has macaque precedent [@cox2019temporal], [@dougherty2021binocular] (as STE-21). The nonius method itself is a perceptual judgment: [@larson1982uncertainty] — "Fixation disparity was measured by means of limits within which nonius alignment was perceived" (abstract; human observers); [@carmel2010how] — nonius lines as a vergence aid for human observers (Protocol §2; full text).

### STE-23 — Stereoscope alignment is two monocular presentations of the 13-point grid: each eye's map from its own grid, the offset between eyes at each point reported (mirrors plus the animal's phoria); no threshold, the experimenter accepts and the acceptance is recorded
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (his method, the brainstorm notes' §9, Batch 1); the 0.25° tolerance of Batch 2 replaced by the experimenter's acceptance in Batch R5.
- **Where:** [engine spec §12 item 3](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#12-calibration-procedures-and-test-screens-n9-nr5); the brainstorm notes, [§9, Batches 1 and 2](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#9-calibration-procedures-and-test-screens), and [Batch R5](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#the-design-reviews-questions-asked-2026-10-07).
- **Basis:** PI decision; it matches the published macaque alignment procedure, a check of the mirrors in which gaze is compared across eyes at the same locations. Fitting each eye's map from its own grid is not described in these papers. [@cox2019temporal] — "At the beginning of each experimental session, the stereoscope was calibrated via a behavioral task …, which required the animals to fixate on the same location in visual space while being cued in one eye only. Gaze position was measured for each fixation location and compared across eyes. When gaze position was comparable for cueing in each eye, the mirrors were considered aligned" (Methods, "Visual stimulation"; full text); the same text in [@mitchell2022stimulating] (STAR Methods, "Visual apparatus"; full text). [@dougherty2021binocular] — the grid made explicit: a cue at the center of the left half shown to the left eye only, then at "eight other locations one-at-a-time, spaced equidistant from the left monitor's center up to 10°", repeated for the right half, gaze compared at corresponding locations: "Overlapping gaze positions for corresponding cue locations suggest the mirrors are optically aligned" (Materials and Methods, "Visual display"; full text). None of the three states a numeric threshold, which matches "the experimenter accepts". For the phoria term: [@svede2015monocular] — in a monocular calibration "the corresponding vergence state represents the heterophoria (the resting vergence position)" (abstract; human, video tracker). Cox et al. and Mitchell et al. cite Maier et al. 2008 (Nat Neurosci, doi 10.1038/nn.2173) for the procedure; its main text (read in full via PubMed Central) describes only a nine-point calibration and anaglyph filters, so it is not cited here; its supplement was not read.

### STE-24 — Disparity is verified separately with a target at known disparity, confirmed fused at the intended depth; disparity is never calibrated by the gaze map
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, engineering default (S4).
- **Where:** [S4 §10](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S4-stimulus-presentation-design.md#10-test-screens) item 6 and [§3](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S4-stimulus-presentation-design.md#3-calibration-targets-are-presented-at-zero-disparity); [S9 §6](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S9-operations-console-design.md#6-test-screens).
- **Basis:** Engineering default, with published practice for a vergence-based check. [@wirth2017gazeinformed] — "We confirmed the animals perceived images with the depth of stereoscopic projection by measuring vergence as a small object moved from an apparent 50 cm in front of the screen to 150 cm behind the screen" (Materials and methods, "Behavioral methods and setup"; full text). [@busettini1996shortlatency] — disparity steps drive vergence in rhesus monkeys at short latency without training (abstract), though large uncrossed steps can drive convergence (STE-07).

### STE-25 — Head-free chaired sessions use a nominal head position, recorded and on the warnings list; a task may declare it needs a fixed head and is then refused in chaired sessions
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (the brainstorm notes' §2, Batch 2, "Nominal, recorded").
- **Where:** [engine spec §5 item 9](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#5-where-n2-nr5); the brainstorm notes, [§2, Batch 2](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#2-where).
- **Basis:** PI decision. No published basis was found (searched 2026-10-08: Europe PMC, head-free macaque eye tracking; the full text of Ryan et al. 2019, Front Behav Neurosci 13, doi 10.3389/fnbeh.2019.00039, says nothing on how head position enters stimulus coordinates).

### STE-26 — The cage-side kiosk is one screen, monocular by construction: the zero-disparity path with no vergence offset
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, engineering default (S13); the kiosk itself was the PI's request, 2026-08-31.
- **Where:** [S13 §3](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S13-kiosk-design.md#3-what-is-present).
- **Basis:** Engineering default; no published basis needed.

## Eye tracking and calibration

### EYE-01 — The eye tracker is OpenIrisDPI (digital dual-Purkinje, 500 Hz binocular) on its own PC; in-vivo macaque precision 0.39-0.44 arcmin against 1.55-1.82 for pupil-CR on the same rig
- **Decided:** 2026-08-30, recorded (the OpenIrisDPI note); the choice is the lab's, made before this repository existed.
- **Where:** [OpenIrisDPI note, "System facts"](../research/openiris-dpi.md#system-facts-verified); [controller architecture §3.1](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-controller-architecture-design.md#31-topology); [architecture, "Per-rig topology"](../design/architecture.md#per-rig-topology); [S0 §7](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S0-hosts-and-hardware-design.md#7-parts-lists-addendum-2026-09-27-revised-the-same-day).
- **Basis:** The lab's choice, on published work. [@ressmeyer2026openirisdpi] — the system: DPI tracking as an OpenIris plugin, binocular 500 Hz on a consumer-grade CPU (Introduction; §2.1; §3.1), validated in two rhesus macaques (§2.4); the precision figures are its white-noise RMS (spectral power above 200 Hz) between saccades, DPI 0.39′ ± 0.06′ azimuth and 0.44′ ± 0.07′ elevation against P-CR 1.55′ ± 0.20′ and 1.82′ ± 0.26′ computed from the same video, 11 sessions (§3.3.1, Fig. 6), and P-CR also carries slow drift that DPI lacks (§3.3.2). The documents' "precision" is this frame-to-frame noise measure, not total error (the paper defines its terms in Appendix A). [@sadeghi2024open] — the OpenIris framework: C#, plugins for cameras, tracking and calibration pipelines, remote control over a network interface, binocular pupil tracking above 500 Hz (abstract). [@cornsweet1973accurate] — the DPI principle: an infrared tracker built on the first and fourth Purkinje images, about 1′ of sensitivity and accuracy, nothing attached to the eye (abstract). [@crane1985generationv] — the analog Generation-V DPI that digital DPI replaces (abstract). [@kimmel2012tracking] — a known weakness of pupil-based tracking in macaques: the EyeLink's pupil-CR position moved 0.2-1° with luminance-driven pupil changes (up to 2° with background steps) that a simultaneous search coil did not show (abstract; Results, "Experiment 2, analysis 1: luminance/position interaction"); they nonetheless found the artifact "generally modest for the luminance range of a typical task" and concluded that optical performance "now rivals that of the search coil, rendering optical systems appropriate for many if not most applications".

### EYE-02 — The raw gaze signal is CR1 − CR4 (P1 − P4) per eye; the map is affine [1, dx, dy] or second-order [1, dx, dy, dx², dy², dx·dy], fixed by wl-preproc; only the procedure is ours
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, wl-preproc's (an external contract, read at `f7fb10a`, S5 §1); its model is taken from OpenIrisDPI's tutorial notebook.
- **Where:** [S5 §1](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S5-eye-tracking-design.md#1-most-of-the-calibration-is-already-decided-and-not-by-us) and [§8](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S5-eye-tracking-design.md#8-what-we-publish); [M0 review §2](../M0-REVIEW.md#2-what-has-actually-been-established-that-wasnt-known-on-2026-08-30) item 3.
- **Basis:** An external contract, on a published method. [@ressmeyer2026openirisdpi] — the raw DPI signal is the difference between the CR and P4 centers, and the authors' calibration regresses fixations onto target positions "using a quadratic model" (§2.4). [@cornsweet1973accurate] — eye rotation is read from the first against the fourth Purkinje image (abstract). [@wu2023highresolution] — the P1-P4 separation is monotonic and close to linear in eye rotation and little affected by eye translation (Introduction; "Measuring eye movements from Purkinje reflections", Figs. 2B and 3C). [@kimmel2012tracking] — in macaques a biquadratic map (constant, linear, squared and cross terms per axis) was more accurate than linear calibration (Methods, "Offline calibration", Eqs. 1-2); its cross term is fitted per corner quadrant, ours is one global term. [@blignaut2014mapping] — the second-order polynomial with an interaction term, X = a0 + a1x + a2x² + a3y + a4y² + a5xy (and likewise for Y), is a standard mapping, used by earlier work it cites (p. 3). The tutorial notebook is software, not a paper, and is not entered.

### EYE-03 — P4 is visible to about 10° of gaze in macaques; beyond the DPI's reach gaze falls back to pupil − CR1 with its own map and a per-sample method flag (design pending)
- **Decided:** 2026-09-27, the PI (the fallback); its design is pending.
- **Where:** [S5 §1](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S5-eye-tracking-design.md#1-most-of-the-calibration-is-already-decided-and-not-by-us); [S0 §5.1](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S0-hosts-and-hardware-design.md#51-panel-class); [direct-view spec §1](../superpowers/specs/2026-09-28-direct-view-design.md#1-decisions-pi-2026-09-28-asked-in-plain-terms) and [§5](../superpowers/specs/2026-09-28-direct-view-design.md#5-the-eye-camera-and-its-light); [panel comparison §1](../research/2026-09-27-panel-27-vs-32.md#1-answer) item 4 and [§6](../research/2026-09-27-panel-27-vs-32.md#6-p4-at-15-the-synthetic-check-the-same-for-both-panels).
- **Basis:** PI decision, on a published basis. [@ressmeyer2026openirisdpi] — "P4 was visible for eye positions up to 10° from central position in any direction before becoming obscured by the iris", in rhesus macaques with their oblique cameras (§2.2); the narrow range is a limitation common to DPI trackers (Introduction); a hybrid "that dynamically switches between methods", DPI for fixational precision and P-CR for larger gaze angles, is proposed there as future work (§4.2). [@cornsweet1973accurate] — the original DPI worked over a field 10-20° in diameter (abstract). [@wu2023highresolution] — "the occlusion of P4 by the pupil will limit the range of measurable eye movements, as it happens with the analog DPI" ("Measuring eye movements from Purkinje reflections"). The fallback signal's known weakness is the caveat.
- **Caveat:** Pupil-CR positions in macaques move 0.2-1° and more with luminance-driven pupil changes [@kimmel2012tracking], and drift with pupil dynamics even at constant luminance [@ressmeyer2026openirisdpi, §3.3.2, §4.1]. The fallback map holds at the luminance it was fitted at; the per-sample method flag (XC-006) keeps that visible.

### EYE-04 — P4's reach scales with pupil size, so a dimmer background would extend tracking at the cost of photometry (raised, not decided); a hot mirror is not recommended
- **Decided:** 2026-09-27, raised in a research note (the panel comparison) as a recommendation for the PI; not decided.
- **Where:** [panel comparison §5](../research/2026-09-27-panel-27-vs-32.md#5-the-alternative-a-hot-mirror), [§6.3](../research/2026-09-27-panel-27-vs-32.md#63-what-would-extend-the-reach) and [§12](../research/2026-09-27-panel-27-vs-32.md#12-decisions-this-raises-for-the-pi-to-be-asked-not-filed) item 2.
- **Basis:** Open: a recommendation, not a decision. The mechanism is published; the proportional scaling is the lab's own synthetic model, anchored to the paper's 10°, and no published measurement of DPI reach against pupil diameter was found. [@wu2023highresolution] — P4's occlusion by the pupil limits the range ("Measuring eye movements from Purkinje reflections"). [@kimmel2012tracking] — background luminance sets pupil size in macaques: a gray rather than black background was used "to increase the overall stimulus luminance so as to reduce the pupil size" (Methods, "Experiment 1: instructed saccade task"). [@pong2000characteristics] — macaque pupillary constriction grows linearly with the log of stimulus luminance (abstract; brief flashes, not steady backgrounds). Hot mirror: [@ressmeyer2026openirisdpi] — most earlier DPI trackers use one; it minimizes nonlinearity but "adds complexity by filtering the light between the display and the subject", and the oblique configuration removes the need (§3.1); [@kimmel2012tracking] — a 45° hot mirror in front of macaques' eyes, with stimulus luminance photometered from behind it (Methods, "Apparatus"). The luminance-loss and color-shift figures are the panel comparison's, from vendor data, not from a paper.

### EYE-05 — Calibration presents a grid, never a ring; conditioning is computed online and a degenerate constellation refused (0.05 affine, 0.10 second-order)
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, engineering (mathematical), on wl-preproc's measured conditioning table (S5 §2); amended 2026-09-05.
- **Where:** [S5 §2](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S5-eye-tracking-design.md#2-a-ring-of-calibration-targets-cannot-fit-a-second-order-map), "A ring of calibration targets cannot fit a second-order map"; [M0 review §2](../M0-REVIEW.md#2-what-has-actually-been-established-that-wasnt-known-on-2026-08-30) item 4.
- **Basis:** Engineering (mathematical). The ring's degeneracy is arithmetic (points on a circle satisfy dx² + dy² = r², so the constant, dx² and dy² columns are dependent) and needs no citation. Published calibration uses grids: [@kimmel2012tracking] — a 3 × 3 grid (EyeLink HV9) in macaques (Methods, "Optical tracker settings and online calibration"); [@ressmeyer2026openirisdpi] — 7 × 7 or 5 × 5 grids (§2.4); [@blignaut2014mapping] — "the number and arrangement of calibration targets as well as the mapping function is critically important" (p. 1), and targets should cover as many distinct x and y values as possible, a 3 × 3 grid giving only three of each (p. 5). The two thresholds are wl-preproc's; no published source.

### EYE-06 — The constellation is thirteen targets: a 3 × 3 grid at the region's reach plus four diagonal intermediates at half; it survives losing five targets 95% of the time; extrapolation is reported, not refused
- **Decided:** 2026-09-05, engineering, by a committed simulation (S5 §2, amended); its use at every session start is the PI's (2026-10-07, EYE-09).
- **Where:** [S5 §2](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S5-eye-tracking-design.md#2-a-ring-of-calibration-targets-cannot-fit-a-second-order-map) (amended 2026-09-05) and [§7](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S5-eye-tracking-design.md#7-the-calibration-procedure); [engine spec §12 item 2](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#12-calibration-procedures-and-test-screens-n9-nr5); [`calibration.py`](../../tasks/calibration.py) (docstring).
- **Basis:** Engineering, by simulation; the layout is a vendor standard. The count and the survival figure are the lab's simulation (`docs/measurements/dev-machine/2026-09-05-calibration-constellation.md`). The same layout is a vendor standard: [@srresearch2020faqb] — EyeLink's 13-point model numbers a 3 × 3 grid plus four points centered between its rows and columns (the "FOR 13-POINT ALGORITHM" diagram); [@srresearch2020faq] — HV9 or HV13 suit stimuli that occupy most or all of the screen, and "using more calibration points will result in better spatial accuracy across the entire screen". [@blignaut2014mapping] — the best configuration for that (pupil-glint) system had fourteen targets (abstract, p. 1), and more distinct target coordinates help (p. 5); the four intermediates add two distinct x and two distinct y values to the grid's three.

### EYE-07 — The constellation is placed per setup over a calibration region: ±15° × ±15° in direct view, the mask's ±12° in the stereoscope; reach 85% and 100% (close calls); corner targets (~21°) fit worse
- **Decided:** 2026-09-27, the PI (the 15° stimulus range); 2026-09-28, engineering by simulation (the reaches).
- **Where:** [S5 §2](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S5-eye-tracking-design.md#2-a-ring-of-calibration-targets-cannot-fit-a-second-order-map) (amended 2026-09-28); [direct-view spec §6](../superpowers/specs/2026-09-28-direct-view-design.md#6-calibration-per-setup); [`calibration.py`](../../tasks/calibration.py) (`target_x`, `target_y`).
- **Basis:** PI decision and engineering. Calibrating the region where the stimuli go: [@srresearch2020faq] (the model is chosen by how much of the screen the stimuli occupy); [@blignaut2014mapping] — "To limit the end effects of polynomial interpolation, it is important that there are targets on the edges of the display area" (p. 3, "Calibration Targets"). The reaches and the finding that panel-corner targets fit worse are the lab's simulation; no published source found.
- **Caveat:** Targets on the edges of the area measured limit polynomial end effects [@blignaut2014mapping, p. 3]. Along the axes, direct view's stimuli from 10.8° to 15° lie beyond the constellation's edge targets, which S5 §2 reports as extrapolation rather than refusing, by design.

### EYE-08 — Calibration targets placed where P4 is lost fit noise; the conditioning clause sees reach but not trackability
- **Decided:** 2026-09-27, a research finding (the panel comparison §7); no decision was taken on it.
- **Where:** [panel comparison §7](../research/2026-09-27-panel-27-vs-32.md#7-what-the-viewing-distance-costs-direct-view).
- **Basis:** A research finding, on published figures. [@ressmeyer2026openirisdpi] — P4 obscured by the iris beyond about 10° (§2.2); [@wu2023highresolution] — P4's occlusion by the pupil limits the measurable range ("Measuring eye movements from Purkinje reflections"). That a fit through such targets is fit to noise is the lab's inference, and that conditioning cannot see it is arithmetic (it is scale-invariant).

### EYE-09 — A full tracker calibration (the thirteen-target constellation) runs at every session start, before any task; no first task without a validated map; in-task calibration epochs track drift
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, the PI (S3 §7, S5 §7), and 2026-10-07 ("Full calibration, every session", the brainstorm notes' §9, Batch 1).
- **Where:** [engine spec §12 item 2](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#12-calibration-procedures-and-test-screens-n9-nr5); the brainstorm notes, [§9, Batch 1](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#9-calibration-procedures-and-test-screens); [S5 §7](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S5-eye-tracking-design.md#7-the-calibration-procedure); [S3 §7](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S3-sync-integration-design.md#7-the-constraint-nobody-would-have-predicted).
- **Basis:** PI decision, matching published macaque practice: [@kimmel2012tracking] — the optical tracker was calibrated "before each day's experiments" (Methods, "Optical tracker settings and online calibration"), and drift within a session was small, at most 0.3° in an hour (Results, "Experiment 1, analysis 4: trial-to-trial drift"). [@hornof2002cleaning] — fixations a task requires at known places ("implicit required fixation locations") can monitor the calibration's deterioration, trigger recalibration and reduce systematic error (abstract), which is what in-task calibration epochs do. "Complementary, not alternatives" is wl-preproc's docstring, not a paper.

### EYE-10 — Calibration targets are presented at zero disparity; each eye's map is fitted independently against a shared cyclopean target set
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, engineering (S4 §3), closing S5 open item 4. On the stereoscope it is superseded by the PI's two monocular grids, each eye's map from its own grid (2026-10-07; the brainstorm notes' Batch 2, "One procedure on the stereoscope"; the engine spec §12 item 3), so it now holds for direct view only; S4 §3 still states it for every setup.
- **Where:** [S4 §3](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S4-stimulus-presentation-design.md#3-calibration-targets-are-presented-at-zero-disparity); [S5 §10](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S5-eye-tracking-design.md#10-open-items) item 4; for the stereoscope, [engine spec §12 item 3](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#12-calibration-procedures-and-test-screens-n9-nr5).
- **Basis:** Engineering default. [@svede2015monocular] — in a binocular calibration "a vergence error may be present and may affect the subsequent measurement"; in a monocular calibration the covered eye rests at its heterophoria and does not affect the fit, and monocular calibration gave the physiologically more plausible fixation disparity (abstract). This supports the stereoscope's monocular grids; for direct view, see the caveat. Separate per-eye maps follow from the tracker giving each eye its own raw vector (S5 §1).
- **Caveat:** In a binocular calibration "a vergence error may be present and may affect the subsequent measurement", so later vergence or fixation-disparity measures may be relative to the fixation disparity the animal held while calibrating; a monocular calibration avoids this [@svede2015monocular] (abstract; human, video tracker), as the stereoscope's monocular grids do. A direct-view task that measures vergence or fixation disparity says so.

### EYE-11 — The calibration trial shows a 0.3° target and rewards a held fixation; its window is wider than a fixation window (1-8°, start 4.0°); `cal_hold` 0.1-2 s, start 0.3 s (the demo-mode science review argues 0.5 s; the PI has yet to decide); only CORRECT fixations feed the fit
- **Decided:** 2026-10-06, engineering default (the demo-mode spec §6); the start of `cal_hold` is open for the PI (the engine spec §25).
