- **Where:** [`calibration.py`](../../tasks/calibration.py) (docstring, `Param`s); [demo-mode spec §6](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-06-demo-mode-design.md#6-starting-values-closes-xc-183), "Starting values", and [§14](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-06-demo-mode-design.md#14-parked-2026-10-07-what-the-reviews-found-and-what-comes-first) item 2; [engine spec §25](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#25-not-decided-here-and-why).
- **Basis:** Engineering default. Macaque precedents: [@ressmeyer2026openirisdpi] — 0.2° targets; a target counted as fixated within a 1.4° × 1.4° box held for 600 ms; outlier fixations removed (median + 3 IQR of within-target distances) before the fit, which used the median of the last 100 ms before target offset (§2.4). [@kimmel2012tracking] — 0.5° fixation points; fixation windows of 1.5° and 4° radius and target windows of 2.5° and 4° in two monkeys; calibration fixations held 750 ms, at least five successful per target, the last 300 ms averaged (Methods, "Experiment 1: instructed saccade task" and "Offline calibration"). [@pirdankar2016influence] — in a normal macaque, fixation was least unstable with a 0.5° target (abstract; search coil). The 0.3° target and the 4° starting window lie within these; only successful fixations fed the fit in both papers.

### EYE-12 — Recentering is a single-point offset making a new mapping version; drift correction never overwrites the raw signal, is logged and reversible offline; every trial cites the mapping version in force
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, engineering default (S5 §6 and §7; the controller architecture §9.3).
- **Where:** [S5 §6](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S5-eye-tracking-design.md#6-the-gaze-mapping-is-one-versioned-object) and [§7](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S5-eye-tracking-design.md#7-the-calibration-procedure); [controller architecture §9.3](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-controller-architecture-design.md#93-the-gaze-mapping-is-a-versioned-object).
- **Basis:** Engineering default. Keeping raw samples and applying the map separately is published macaque practice: [@kimmel2012tracking] — the online biquadratic calibration "does not influence the raw offline signal", and all analysis used the raw samples (Methods, "Optical tracker settings and online calibration"; "Offline calibration"); [@ressmeyer2026openirisdpi] — the online signal was used "for online experimental control only", analyses used the digital signals OpenIris saved (§2.4). Correcting systematic error from known fixation locations: [@hornof2002cleaning] (abstract). Versioning and the single-point offset are engineering; no published source sought further.

### EYE-13 — The UDP poll (port 9003) is the control path, polled at least at the frame rate, stamped on arrival, each sample "latest available"; the ACCES analog copy is recorded only (adds ~3-4 ms, capped near 2 kHz) and measures the eye PC's lag by cross-correlation
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, engineering default on the paper's figures (S5 §3; the controller architecture §9.1).
- **Where:** [S5 §3](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S5-eye-tracking-design.md#3-ingest); [controller architecture §9.1](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-controller-architecture-design.md#91-which-path-controls); [OpenIrisDPI note, "Output interfaces"](../research/openiris-dpi.md#output-interfaces-verified-in-sourcewiki); [S6 §5](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S6-io-subsystem-design.md#5-analog-input); [architecture, "Message contracts"](../design/architecture.md#message-contracts-draft-v0).
- **Basis:** Engineering default, on published figures. [@ressmeyer2026openirisdpi] — the analog output inherited processing delays and "imposed an additional 3-4 ms delay, which may limit the use of this signal for gaze-contingent applications" (§3.1); the authors themselves controlled their task from the analog signal (Bessel-filtered at 180 Hz) and analyzed the timestamped digital record (§2.4). The paper does not report the UDP path's latency, so that UDP is the faster path is unmeasured until V3. [@saunders2014direct] — end-to-end latency should be measured directly rather than summed from component specifications (Introduction; Conclusion). The ~2 kHz cap is a converter specification, not a paper.

### EYE-14 — The tracker's ~2% of frames ≥ 10 ms (max ~50 ms) is the authors' processing time; what matters is staleness at poll time, which V3 measures on our rig; the paper's 2% is never quoted as ours
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, engineering, on the published figures (S5 §4).
- **Where:** [S5 §4](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S5-eye-tracking-design.md#4-p6--the-stall-problem-handled-honestly), "P6 — the stall problem"; [pitfalls P6](../pitfalls.md#expanded-notes) (the table and the expanded note); [controller architecture §9.2](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-controller-architecture-design.md#92-online-saccade-detection); [OpenIrisDPI note, "System facts"](../research/openiris-dpi.md#system-facts-verified).
- **Basis:** Engineering, on published figures. [@ressmeyer2026openirisdpi] — processing per frame pair 1.1 ± 0.1 ms (median ± IQR: the documents' "±" is an interquartile range), occasionally up to 50 ms, 2% of frames ≥ 10 ms, "likely due to CPU preemption by background operating system processes" on the authors' PC (§3.1). [@saunders2014direct] — component figures and manufacturer specifications can be wrong or incomplete; system latency should be measured end to end and reported in their place (Introduction; Conclusion).

### EYE-15 — Every gaze decision records the staleness of its sample; hold-last with a staleness ceiling, beyond which gaze is unknown; aborting a trial requires several corroborating samples
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, engineering default (S5 §4.1).
- **Where:** [S5 §4.1](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S5-eye-tracking-design.md#41-the-design-that-tolerates-it); [pitfalls P6](../pitfalls.md#expanded-notes) (the table and the expanded note); [controller architecture §9.2](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-controller-architecture-design.md#92-online-saccade-detection); [OpenIrisDPI note, "Design consequences"](../research/openiris-dpi.md#design-consequences-for-our-controller).
- **Basis:** Engineering default. The stalls it guards against are published [@ressmeyer2026openirisdpi] (§3.1); no published convention for staleness ceilings or corroborated aborts was found (searched 2026-10-08: PubMed for gaze-contingent sample loss, dropout, latency and fixation-window grace periods, no hits; of the gaze-contingent papers opened, [@saunders2014direct], read in full, does not address stale samples, and the abstract of [@santini2007eyeris], all that was read of it, does not mention them).

### EYE-16 — A tracker stall does not abort the trial: the update lands on the last known gaze, the trial completes, marked `TRACKER_STALE` with the staleness used
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, the PI.
- **Where:** [S5 §4.1](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S5-eye-tracking-design.md#41-the-design-that-tolerates-it) item 5; [S2 §5.1](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S2-event-vocabulary-design.md#51-in-wl-exptasks-ranges-ours-to-allocate-no-amendment-required).
- **Basis:** PI decision (keep the trial, decide in analysis). No published basis; none was sought beyond the stall figures of EYE-14.

### EYE-17 — The staleness ceiling and grace periods are frozen only after V3(a); gaze-window grace parameters are set from data, never from the paper's numbers
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, engineering default (S5 §10 item 2; roadmap M3).
- **Where:** [S5 §10](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S5-eye-tracking-design.md#10-open-items) item 2; [roadmap M3](../roadmap.md#m3--eye-loop-live); [controller architecture §9.2](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-controller-architecture-design.md#92-online-saccade-detection); [pitfalls P6](../pitfalls.md#expanded-notes) (the table and the expanded note).
- **Basis:** Engineering default, resting on the repository's rule that no timing figure stands without a measurement of this system (CLAUDE.md, "No timing claim without a measurement") and on S5 §4's point that the paper's stall figures describe its authors' PC, not our rig. No paper is cited for it: Saunders & Woods 2014, cited until 2026-10-08, measure end-to-end display latency, not grace periods or staleness. The values themselves await V3(a). Inventory disagreement 9 (EYE-18's 0.05 s, taken from the paper) is answered by labeling that value a placeholder until V3(a) (S1a §13, corrected 2026-10-08).

### EYE-18 — Blink and tracker-loss graces are independent: blinks not tolerated by default (0.0 s), tracker loss 0.05 s by default; inside a grace the trial's view of gaze freezes
- **Decided:** 2026-09-01, the PI (the outcomes); the 0.05 s is the paper's maximum stall (inventory disagreement 9), labeled a placeholder until V3(a) since 2026-10-08.
- **Where:** [S1a §13](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S1a-vocabulary-design.md#13-outcomes-extended-2026-09-01--signal-detection-and-the-rig), "Outcomes extended 2026-09-01".
- **Basis:** PI decision. One published macaque precedent, not a general practice: [@kimmel2012tracking] — controlling the task from the optical tracker "had the advantage of forcing repetition of trials in which the animal blinked" (Methods, "Apparatus"), a side benefit of the control setup used for one monkey in one study. The 0.05 s is the paper's maximum processing delay [@ressmeyer2026openirisdpi] (§3.1), which S5 §4 and roadmap M3 say must not stand for our rig: the citation records where the number came from, not that it is right here.

### EYE-19 — Online saccade detection is Engbert-Kliegl (velocity threshold, per-trial adaptive), matching wl-preproc's offline suite, one versioned component with logged parameters set from V3(c); velocity only across samples within the staleness ceiling; a saccade across a gap is flagged
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, the PI.
- **Where:** [S5 §5](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S5-eye-tracking-design.md#5-online-saccade-detection); [M0 review §3.1](../M0-REVIEW.md#31-science-6) item 3; [controller architecture §9.2](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-controller-architecture-design.md#92-online-saccade-detection).
- **Basis:** PI decision, on a published method. [@engbert2003microsaccades] — the method's source, by name (abstract only: the algorithm is in its methods, which could not be opened, so no location is given). [@kimmel2012tracking] — the method as applied in macaques, citing Engbert & Kliegl 2003: per-axis velocity thresholds set as a multiple λ of each trial's velocity spread, combined as an ellipse in 2-D velocity space, with a minimum duration (λ 6 and 10, 5 ms), and sub-saccades within 30 ms merged (Methods, "Saccade detection"). The DPI caveat — [@ressmeyer2026openirisdpi] (§4.1; Introduction) and [@deubel1995fourth] (abstract) — is the caveat. The stall rule and gap flag are engineering.
- **Caveat:** DPI "is ill-suited for measuring saccade kinematics": the lens oscillates at saccade onset and end, so the trace rings and briefly decouples from the eye's rotation [@ressmeyer2026openirisdpi, §4.1, Introduction], with deviations from the search coil during and just after saccades [@deubel1995fourth]; pupil-based tracking rings after saccades too [@kimmel2012tracking] (separately, they merged sub-saccades within 30 ms, which they attribute to "noise in the position signal"). Engbert-Kliegl stands, but saccade offsets ("update on landing", EYE-21) and amplitudes detected on the DPI signal are distorted; V3(c) sets the detector's parameters with that in view (XC-072).

### EYE-20 — A saccade-triggered display change must land inside saccadic suppression; a displacement meant to go unseen must land during the saccade (after it, it may be seen); feasibility measured end to end in V3(b)
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, S5 §5 and the controller architecture §9.1 (the budget); 2026-10-07, the engine spec §5 item 6 (the correction, from the engine science review's finding 12). Its "after it, it is readily seen" was corrected to "may be seen" on 2026-10-08, from the papers below.
- **Where:** [S5 §5](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S5-eye-tracking-design.md#5-online-saccade-detection) and [§9](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S5-eye-tracking-design.md#9-measurement); [engine spec §5 item 6](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#5-where-n2-nr5); [controller architecture §9.1](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-controller-architecture-design.md#91-which-path-controls) and [§14](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-controller-architecture-design.md#14-latency-budgets--requirements-not-measurements); [pitfalls P6](../pitfalls.md#expanded-notes) (the table and the expanded note); [roadmap M3](../roadmap.md#m3--eye-loop-live).
- **Basis:** Published (the budget); engineering (the measurement). [@ross2001changes] — saccades suppress visual sensitivity, specifically in the magnocellular pathway, dampening the sensation of motion (abstract). [@diamond2000extraretinal] — for luminance gratings, suppression begins about 50 ms before the saccade, is maximal at its onset and outlasts it by about 50 ms; it is weaker at low luminance and absent for chromatic gratings (abstract). [@deubel1996postsaccadic] — displacement of a target during a saccade "is normally detected only at a high threshold" in humans (abstract). Macaque: [@joiner2013corollary] — monkeys trained on the same displacement task detected displacements as humans did, but did not gain from a post-saccadic blank as humans do (abstract); [@cavanaugh2016saccadic] — the target was displaced during the saccade "because thresholds for detecting displacement are elevated during saccades; transients associated with target displacement (ie, apparent motion) are not detected" (Materials and Methods, "Psychophysical task"): their design choice, the finding itself Bridgeman et al. 1975's, whom they cite for it (not opened). End-to-end measurement: [@saunders2014direct] — direct photographic measurement of the whole system's latency (Method, "Measurement of system latency"), and a target of under 25 ms mean latency at screen midpoint for masking central vision (Discussion, "What is an acceptable level of system latency?"); the 5-25 ms within which they say suppression lessens is their summary of other studies (McConkie & Loschky 2002, Ross et al. 2001, Shioiri 1993, Volkmann and others), not opened here, so it is not cited as theirs. The clause "after it, it is readily seen" had no support: suppression of luminance contrast outlasts the saccade by about 50 ms [@diamond2000extraretinal], and peripheral resolution updates as late as 60 ms after an eye movement "did not significantly increase the detectability of image blur and/or motion transients" [@loschky2007how] (abstract); chromatic gratings showed no saccadic suppression [@diamond2000extraretinal]; and trained monkeys did not gain from a post-saccadic blank as humans do [@joiner2013corollary]. Hence "may be seen". Bridgeman, Hendry & Stark 1975, which the science review cites, could not be opened and is not entered.

### EYE-21 — A gaze-anchored stimulus uses a selectable gaze rule (newest by default; smoothed; predicted to the frame), each frame's sample age recorded; behavior when gaze is lost and during a saccade is declared per task
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI.
- **Where:** [engine spec §5 item 6](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#5-where-n2-nr5); the brainstorm notes, [§2, Batches 2-4](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#2-where); [S4 §5](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S4-stimulus-presentation-design.md#5-motion-is-a-function-not-a-trajectory).
- **Basis:** PI decision; published practice supports offering these choices. [@santini2007eyeris] — eye-movement-contingent display, including retinal stabilization, as a general method, the system designed to update the stimulus with a delay of only 10 ms (abstract). [@crane1985generationv] — the DPI driving retinal-image stabilization and simulated scotomas (abstract). [@saunders2014direct] — moving a gaze-locked patch to the newest sample every refresh can jitter during fixation, so relocating it only at fixation onset, or smoothing, "may be desirable" (Method, "Synchronizing eye position data and experimental stimulus recordings"); sampling as late as possible before the refresh uses a fresher sample (Discussion, "Reducing system latency"). For prediction to the frame no published source was opened.

### EYE-22 — Gaze windows may be any shape (circle, ellipse, rectangle, polygon, a stimulus's outline grown by a margin), in cyclopean degrees, with position and size declared
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI.
- **Where:** [engine spec §5 item 7](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#5-where-n2-nr5); the brainstorm notes, [§2, Batch 2](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#2-where); [S1a §1](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S1a-vocabulary-design.md#1-windows-are-declared-not-named-in-passing); [S5 §6](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S5-eye-tracking-design.md#6-the-gaze-mapping-is-one-versioned-object).
- **Basis:** PI decision. Both square and circular windows are in published macaque use — a 1.4° × 1.4° box [@ressmeyer2026openirisdpi] (§2.4) and circles of 1.5-4° radius [@kimmel2012tracking] (Methods, "Experiment 1") — but nothing published bears on allowing arbitrary shapes.

### EYE-23 — Per-eye window criteria are honored: under dichoptic presentation the non-viewing eye deviates to its phoria, so a conjugate estimate averages in an eye not looking at the stimulus; a window scoring the eye its stimulus is not shown to is refused
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, engineering default (S1a §12, "Added 2026-08-31 after review"); its stated reason corrected on 2026-10-08.
- **Where:** [S1a §12](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S1a-vocabulary-design.md#12-added-2026-08-31-after-review--what-the-graph-could-not-see), "Per-eye criteria".
- **Basis:** Engineering default, on published findings. [@gantz2020synchronization] — heterophoria is the deviation of the eyes' axes without fusion, revealed by covering one eye or by showing the eyes elements that cannot be fused (Introduction); the covered eye moves to its heterophoric position (Results, Fig. 4), which in 30 normally sighted adults ranged from 14Δ exophoria to 4Δ esophoria (abstract). [@svede2015monocular] — under monocular viewing the vergence state is the heterophoria (abstract). Macaque: [@pirdankar2016influence] — in a normal monkey the covered eye was nearly as stable as the viewing eye (mean BCEA difference 0.03 deg², Results; covered-eye BCEA 0.20 deg², Fig. 3 legend). The rule stands; its stated reason, that the non-viewing eye "drifts", was corrected on 2026-10-08: it deviates to its phoria, and in a normal macaque stays nearly as stable as the viewing eye.

### EYE-24 — Two IR bands: the eye tracker at 940 ± 25 nm and the behavior cameras at 850 ± 25 nm, each group's band-pass rejecting the other's light
- **Decided:** 2026-09-27, the PI.
- **Where:** [S0 §7](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S0-hosts-and-hardware-design.md#7-parts-lists-addendum-2026-09-27-revised-the-same-day), "What binds the lists"; [P9 §1](../superpowers/specs/2026-09-27-P9-camera-system-design.md#1-what-the-pi-decided-2026-09-27-asked-in-plain-terms) and [§2](../superpowers/specs/2026-09-27-P9-camera-system-design.md#2-hardware-and-timing).
- **Basis:** PI decision. [@ressmeyer2026openirisdpi] — the published tracker lights the eyes at 940 nm (Thorlabs M940L3; §2.2), chosen as "minimally visible", with the cameras' quantum efficiency under 10% there (§4.2); [@wu2023highresolution] — a digital DPI at 850 nm ("System prototype"), so DPI also works in the other band. The band split and its filters are engineering; no published source.

### EYE-25 — The tracker cameras sit below the screen at 35° with each 940 nm light 10° shallower (25°) to center P4; camera and light ride together on per-eye IPD carriages; brackets hold angle because DPI cancels head translation but not camera rotation
- **Decided:** 2026-09-27 and 2026-09-28, the PI, on the paper's geometry.
- **Where:** [direct-view spec §5](../superpowers/specs/2026-09-28-direct-view-design.md#5-the-eye-camera-and-its-light); [S0 §7.1](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S0-hosts-and-hardware-design.md#71-the-eye-tracker-changes-from-the-openirisdpi-reference-build) rows 5 and 8, [§7.1.3](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S0-hosts-and-hardware-design.md#713-the-ipd-adjustment-system-requirements) and [§7.9](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S0-hosts-and-hardware-design.md#79-what-this-list-cannot-settle-yet); [optics drawing §5](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-stereoscope-optics-drawing.md#5-the-field-stop-and-the-photodiode-strip); [panel comparison §4.1](../research/2026-09-27-panel-27-vs-32.md#41-what-sets-the-angle).
- **Basis:** PI decision, on the published geometry. [@ressmeyer2026openirisdpi] — cameras 35° below the line of sight with the LED above them at 25° (§2.2); placing the illuminator shallower than the cameras "centers P4 in the pupil for neutral gaze, which maximizes the range of eye positions over which P4 is visible" (§3.1); DPI shares P-CR's invariance to head translation (Introduction). [@wu2023highresolution] — P1-P4 motion is little affected by eye translation (Introduction), while rotating the illumination and imaging system about the eye is optically an eye rotation (they model eye movements exactly so; "Measuring eye movements from Purkinje reflections"), which is why a bracket that turns reads as gaze. The per-eye carriages are engineering.

### EYE-26 — Tracker cameras: FLIR BFS-U3-16S2M-CS at 500 Hz in the paper's 720 × 450 region; lens magnification about 0.2× from an ASSUMED 3.4 mm pupil, the PI's working figure
- **Decided:** 2026-09-27, the PI (camera and region from the paper); the pupil is the PI's working figure, unmeasured.
- **Where:** [S0 §7.1](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S0-hosts-and-hardware-design.md#71-the-eye-tracker-changes-from-the-openirisdpi-reference-build) row 1 and [§7.1.1](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S0-hosts-and-hardware-design.md#711-the-tracker-lens-magnification-and-focal-length); [P10 spike §4](../research/2026-09-27-p10-dpi-spike.md#4-what-the-paper-specifies) and [§5.2](../research/2026-09-27-p10-dpi-spike.md#52-optical-model-for-arcmin-stated-and-assumed-arithmetic-shown).
- **Basis:** PI decision, on the published build; the pupil is a working figure. [@ressmeyer2026openirisdpi] — FLIR BFS-U3-16S2M-CS cameras with 100 mm macro lenses at 57 cm (§2.2); 500 Hz by cutting the region of interest from 1440 × 1080 to 720 × 450 (§2.1). No published basis was found for the 3.4 mm pupil, and the paper does not report pupil size (searched 2026-10-08: PubMed for macaque pupil diameter in millimeters; Pong & Fuchs 2000 [@pong2000characteristics] and Clarke, Zhang & Gamlin 2003 give no absolute diameters in their abstracts, and the journal refused the full text; Gamlin et al. 2007 was not released by PubMed Central; ipRGC-ablation work in rhesus reports normalized diameters).

### EYE-27 — A clean-room tracker replaces OpenIrisDPI only after matching or beating it on the same frames from our rig; it reports invalid frames (blinks, lost P4, implausible pupil) as "no sample"; validity logic is designed in
- **Decided:** 2026-09-27, the PI.
- **Where:** [P9 §8](../superpowers/specs/2026-09-27-P9-camera-system-design.md#8-p10-the-eye-tracker-spike--done-2026-09-27); [P10 spike §2](../research/2026-09-27-p10-dpi-spike.md#2-answer), [§7.5](../research/2026-09-27-p10-dpi-spike.md#75-blinks-the-published-algorithm-has-no-failure-logic), [§7b.3](../research/2026-09-27-p10-dpi-spike.md#7b3-blinks), [§10](../research/2026-09-27-p10-dpi-spike.md#10-recommendation) and [§11](../research/2026-09-27-p10-dpi-spike.md#11-the-pis-questions) ("The PI's answers").
- **Basis:** PI decision. The two published algorithms: [@ressmeyer2026openirisdpi] — pupil, then CR, then P4 as the brightest pixel inside the pupil outside the CR's neighborhood (§3.1; Appendix B), with no blink or validity handling described in the text; [@wu2023highresolution] — a detect-then-localize algorithm in which "failure in detecting one of the two reflections is labeled as blink" (Fig. 4B legend, "A robust tracking algorithm"), a published precedent for reporting such frames as no sample. The failure modes named (confident wrong P4 on partial blinks; jumps within ~50 ms of a blink) are the lab's own spike findings (the P10 spike §7.5), not published.

### EYE-28 — The OpenIris PC is tuned (real-time priority, CPU isolation, power management off) with a measured before and after
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, engineering default (S5 §4.2).
- **Where:** [S5 §4.2](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S5-eye-tracking-design.md#42-attack-the-source-not-only-the-symptom).
- **Basis:** Engineering default, against a published cause. [@ressmeyer2026openirisdpi] — the stalls are "likely due to CPU preemption by background operating system processes" on a consumer i9-12900K PC (§3.1; §2.1), which is what the tuning attacks. No published before-and-after for OpenIris was found; whether tuning helps is to be measured.

## Reward and fluid

### REW-01 — Fluid has a floor, not a ceiling: the daily figure is a minimum, topped up by hand after the session; no upper limit on earned reward; a delivery is never refused on volume
- **Decided:** 2026-09-06, the PI (the correction at the head of S8: "It does not. Fluid has a floor"). Institutional practice is implied, not cited.
- **Where:** [S8, the correction at its head](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S8-session-management-design.md#s8--session-and-experiment-management), and [§4](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S8-session-management-design.md#4-the-bounded-config); [controller architecture §7.3](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-controller-architecture-design.md#73-the-bounded-config); [architecture.md, "Components"](../design/architecture.md#components); [pitfalls P17](../pitfalls.md#expanded-notes) (the table and the expanded note); [M0 review §3.3](../M0-REVIEW.md#33-kiosk-protocol-and-process-4) item 12 and [§4](../M0-REVIEW.md#4-engineering-calls-i-have-made--overrule-any-of-these); [`tasks/reference_bounds.py`](../../tasks/reference_bounds.py) (the module docstring).
- **Basis:** PI decision, 2026-09-06; it matches published guidance and practice. [@nc3rs2012refining] — the NC3Rs recommends letting the monkey obtain its whole daily fluid requirement under controlled or scheduled access rather than reducing the daily amount (recommendation 3), and a supplement after the session when the session did not earn the requirement (recommendation 5). [@prescott2010refinement] — the NC3Rs working group's report that the page summarizes: practical guidance on refining food and fluid control for macaques, offered as a framework to tailor to local requirements (abstract). [@gray2016physiological] — in a published rhesus protocol the monkey could work for as much fluid as it wanted within the session, and an animal that did not earn its individually established minimum was supplemented to that minimum after the session (Materials and Methods, "Fluid control protocols"); blood measures did not differ between protocols, while urine osmolality, creatinine and specific gravity were significantly higher under both fluid-control protocols, which the authors read as healthy kidneys retaining fluid (Results, "Physiological measures"; Table 4). [@nrc2003guidelines] — under "fluid scheduling" the experimenter sets when the animal has access to fluid, and the animal may drink for as long as it keeps performing the task (ch. 3, "Fluid-Regulation Design", p. 56). The legal frame: [@eu2010directive] — Art. 33(1)(b) requires that restrictions on an animal's physiological needs be kept to a minimum, and Art. 33(3) lets member states allow exemptions from the care standards for scientific reasons; the Directive names no figure, and that a project's fluid minimum is set within that frame is our reading.
- **Caveat:** The NC3Rs asks that each monkey's baseline daily intake be established and periodically re-established, and that body weight be recorded daily [@nc3rs2012refining, recommendations 2 and 6]. The NRC asks for daily records of intake, body weight and hydration at a new protocol's start [@nrc2003guidelines, p. 56] and otherwise that animals "should be weighed several times a week, ideally before experimental sessions" [@nrc2003guidelines, p. 60], and it warns that voluntary intake "might not be equivalent to the animal's minimal fluid-requirement levels" [@nrc2003guidelines, p. 56]. Individually established minima were 14-26 mL/kg/day in one published protocol [@gray2016physiological]; a kiosk study's 150-350 mL (about 20-25 mL/kg) is the volume completing its task gave, not a minimum or a daily total, and "all monkeys would work for more fluid reward on some days" [@womelsdorf2021kiosk]. A subject's bounded config holds a fixed mL floor (XC-067), which does not follow weight on its own; the reference configs' 20 mL is a deliberately implausible placeholder, not 20 mL/kg.

### REW-02 — Per-delivery volume and rate are bounded by ceilings a console cannot exceed and a task cannot touch; a task never expresses a magnitude
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, engineering default (a welfare structure, S8 §4).
- **Where:** [S8 §4](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S8-session-management-design.md#4-the-bounded-config); [S1 §2.3](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S1-task-model-design.md#23-actions); [S1a §3](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S1a-vocabulary-design.md#3-actions); [controller architecture §7.3](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-controller-architecture-design.md#73-the-bounded-config).
- **Basis:** Engineering default. No published source sets a per-delivery or rate ceiling for fluid reward (searched 2026-10-08: the NC3Rs page, NRC 2003 ch. 3, Prescott et al. 2010's abstract, and PubMed for reward-volume limits in macaque fluid control). Keeping magnitudes out of task code is a software-safety structure, not a literature choice.

### REW-03 — `reward_correct`'s maximum is 10 mL, a runaway-fluid fault bound far above any dose; its value (0.05 mL) is a placeholder
- **Decided:** 2026-09-19, the PI (S8 open item 6: a runaway-fluid fault limit is wanted, "and it is `reward_correct`'s maximum").
- **Where:** [S8 §8](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S8-session-management-design.md#8-open-items) item 6; [architecture.md, "Components"](../design/architecture.md#components); [`tasks/reference_bounds.py`](../../tasks/reference_bounds.py); [`tasks/eight_hour_bounds.py`](../../tasks/eight_hour_bounds.py).
- **Basis:** PI decision. It is a fault bound, which no published source sets. Context for "far above any dose": [@gray2016physiological] — individually established daily minima for adult rhesus were 150-355 mL (14-26 mL/kg/day) (Results, "Fluid intake of individual animals"); [@womelsdorf2021kiosk] — the reward volume was set so that a day's task delivered about 150-350 mL (about 20-25 mL/kg) (Materials and Methods, "Testing Procedure"). One 10 mL delivery is under 7% of the smallest of these daily amounts (arithmetic).

### REW-04 — The daily fluid floor in the reference configs (20 mL) is a deliberately implausible placeholder; no approved protocol figure exists in the repository
- **Decided:** 2026-09-06, engineering placeholder (`tasks/reference_bounds.py`, written that day; `tasks/eight_hour_bounds.py` carries the same placeholders since 2026-09-20). The real figure is the institution's protocol's.
- **Where:** [`tasks/reference_bounds.py`](../../tasks/reference_bounds.py); [`tasks/eight_hour_bounds.py`](../../tasks/eight_hour_bounds.py).
- **Basis:** Engineering placeholder, correctly not a published figure. Published daily amounts, about ten times larger, confirm that 20 mL is implausible as a daily total: [@gray2016physiological] — individually established minima of 150-355 mL/day per animal, set by stepping down from free intake (Materials and Methods, "Fluid control protocols"; Results, "Fluid intake of individual animals"); [@womelsdorf2021kiosk] — a reward volume adjusted so that completing the task gave about 150-350 mL, a working amount rather than a minimum (Materials and Methods, "Testing Procedure"); [@nrc2003guidelines] — minimum fluid needs are hard to state in general ("It is difficult to specify…", p. 55), and intake, body weight and hydration should be recorded daily for each animal at the start of a protocol (ch. 3, "Determination of Minimum Fluid Consumption", pp. 55-56); [@nc3rs2012refining] — each monkey's baseline daily intake should be established and periodically re-established (recommendation 2).
- **Caveat:** REW-01's caveat applies: guidance sets the baseline per animal, with body weight recorded daily [@nc3rs2012refining, recommendations 2 and 6] or several times a week [@nrc2003guidelines, p. 60]; the published minima are per kg [@gray2016physiological]; and the placeholder's 20 mL is not 20 mL/kg.

### REW-05 — Fluid accounting reconciles against the sync box's record of the delivered line; the commanded total is a lower bound
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, engineering default (S8 §5.1).
- **Where:** [S8 §5.1](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S8-session-management-design.md#51-fluid-is-reconciled-not-tallied); [S6 §4](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S6-io-subsystem-design.md#4-reward-and-why-our-total-is-a-floor); [controller architecture §7.4](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-controller-architecture-design.md#74-fluid-accounting-has-a-floor-not-a-total); [pitfalls P17](../pitfalls.md#expanded-notes); [M0 review §4](../M0-REVIEW.md#4-engineering-calls-i-have-made--overrule-any-of-these).
- **Basis:** Engineering default. The guidance supports the purpose, not the mechanism: [@nrc2003guidelines] — the ongoing daily records of the fluid earned in the behavioral task and of any supplements (ch. 3, "Methods of Assessing Nutrition and Hydration", p. 60); a daily record of each animal's intake is asked at a new protocol's start (p. 56). Reconciling against a hardware record of deliveries has no published source.

### REW-06 — Volume is time, so the pump calibration (mL per ms) is measured per rig, re-measured on a schedule, its identity recorded, and checked in date at preflight
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, engineering default (S6 §4).
- **Where:** [S6 §4](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S6-io-subsystem-design.md#4-reward-and-why-our-total-is-a-floor); [S9 §2](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S9-operations-console-design.md#2-preflight); [S9a §10](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S9a-console-design.md#10-preflight-semantics).
- **Basis:** Engineering default, with published precedent for dosing by activation time: [@calapai2017cagebased] — the XBI cage-mounted system doses fluid with a peristaltic pump at a stated volume per second of activation, to about 0.01 mL (Methods, "Animal interface (AI)"). No source was found for a recalibration interval or a preflight date check; those are ours.

### REW-07 — A day that cannot be counted (a restart) keeps paying the animal; the shortfall is reported unknown, never zero; the day is reconstructed from the delivered line and from each trial's `fluid_ml` and each hand reward
- **Decided:** 2026-09-06, the PI (S8 §5.2 item 3, reversed: an uncountable day must not stop paying an animal that is working); 2026-10-01, the PI (XC-026: the fluid so far is written per trial, and a resume adds it up).
- **Where:** [S8 §5.2](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S8-session-management-design.md#52-a-restart-must-not-reset-the-day) items 2-3 and [§6](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S8-session-management-design.md#6-restart-and-resume); [XC-026 resume spec §2](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-01-xc026-resume-design.md#2-decided) and [§4](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-01-xc026-resume-design.md#4-the-fluid-record-welfare-critical); [M0 review §4](../M0-REVIEW.md#4-engineering-calls-i-have-made--overrule-any-of-these).
- **Basis:** PI decision, consistent with guidance: [@nrc2003guidelines] — "careful monitoring of animals under food or fluid control is necessary every day to avoid additional nonprogrammed restriction" (ch. 3, "Training Considerations", p. 59, quoting NIH 2002), and equipment malfunctions and programming errors, variables "that might hinder training or disrupt performance", are to be ruled out before restriction is tightened (p. 59); the records of the fluid earned and of supplements it asks for (p. 60) are what the per-trial and hand-reward sums rebuild. Reporting "unknown" rather than zero has no published source; it is ours.

### REW-08 — Cage-side fluid counts toward the same daily floor as rig work; wl-works holds the ledger; each deployment reports floor − already delivered − earned here as the supplement
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, the PI (kiosk fluid counts toward the same daily figure); 2026-09-06, the PI (that figure is a floor, so each deployment reports what is still to supplement).
- **Where:** [S8 §5.2b](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S8-session-management-design.md#52b-one-fluid-budget-across-rig-and-kiosk); [S13 §4.0](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S13-kiosk-design.md#40-a-kiosk-session-has-no-duration-bound-and-says-so); [M0 review §3.3](../M0-REVIEW.md#33-kiosk-protocol-and-process-4) item 12.
- **Basis:** PI decision, with published precedent for cage-side fluid counting as daily intake: [@calapai2017cagebased] — two animals under fluid control had the cage-mounted device as their only access to fluid on working days (Methods, "Animals, grouping and fluid control"); [@womelsdorf2021kiosk] — in a cage-mounted kiosk the water reward volume was adjusted so that completing the task "would provide between ~150–350 ml of water" (Materials and Methods, "Testing Procedure"), which the paper does not call the daily amount. A supplement after the session when the requirement was not earned: [@nc3rs2012refining] (recommendation 5); [@gray2016physiological] (Materials and Methods, "Fluid control protocols"). A single ledger across deployments is ours.
- **Caveat:** NC3Rs: a monkey that misses its daily requirement in the session gets a supplement afterwards, and "Supplements should be the maximum amount consistent with reliable performance and should be regularly reviewed" [@nc3rs2012refining, recommendation 5]. The figure reported here, "mL to reach the day's floor" (floor − already delivered − earned), is the least that may be given, not a recommended amount; the floor-not-ceiling rule (PI, 2026-09-06) is not in question.

### REW-09 — A reward volume of zero is allowed because it stays visible: a reward period pays an on-screen token that later becomes real reward
- **Decided:** 2026-09-20, the PI (asked twice that day; the second answer gave the token).
- **Where:** [S8 §5.2c](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S8-session-management-design.md#52c-every-number-entering-the-welfare-path); [S9a §9](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S9a-console-design.md#9-the-telemetry-contract); [P4d-2b spec §4.0](../superpowers/specs/2026-09-26-P4d2b-browser-console-design.md#40-rulings-held-from-the-mockup-rounds).
- **Basis:** PI decision, with a macaque precedent: [@seo2009behavioral] — rhesus monkeys earned red disks shown on the screen, exchanged for six drops of juice once six had built up (Materials and Methods, "Behavioral task"; Fig. 1a); about half the trials gained a token (50.8%), the rest were neutral (31.1%) or a loss (18.2%), and juice came only at the sixth token, on average every 9.5 trials (Results, "Effects of tokens on choices"; Fig. 1b). Keeping session fluid and the supplement always on screen is ours.

### REW-10 — Tokens: token-to-fluid conversion and maximum accumulation are bounded config, never exceeding the per-delivery ceiling; the token vocabulary is specified but unimplemented
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, engineering default (S8 §4, §5.3); the design is open (S8 §8 item 9, 2026-09-20: nothing in the task vocabulary models a token yet).
- **Where:** [S8 §4](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S8-session-management-design.md#4-the-bounded-config), [§5.3](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S8-session-management-design.md#53-tokens) and [§8](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S8-session-management-design.md#8-open-items) item 9; [S1 §10](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S1-task-model-design.md#10-open-items) item 3; [S1a §3](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S1a-vocabulary-design.md#3-actions); [controller architecture §5.6](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-controller-architecture-design.md#56-cross-trial-state).
- **Basis:** Engineering default. The two bounded quantities are the parameters of the published token schedule: a fixed exchange rate (one drop per token) and a fixed count at which the exchange happens (six) [@seo2009behavioral] (Materials and Methods, "Behavioral task"). No source sets limits on either.

### REW-11 — A change to a bounded setting (a reward size) is validated when offered and applied at the next trial boundary; presets, revert and carried-over values never carry it
- **Decided:** 2026-09-19, the PI (a welfare-bounded change defers like an ordinary parameter, S9a §8.1); b3a's rule that a setting made in a session lasts that session, across its runs (XC-026 §3); 2026-10-07, the PI (Batch R7: a bounded setting is never instant, and presets, revert and carried values never carry one).
- **Where:** [S9a §8.1](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S9a-console-design.md#81-a-welfare-bounded-change-used-to-apply-immediately-and-no-longer-does); [engine spec §17](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#17-parameters-and-live-control-n14-nr7) items 2 and 6; the brainstorm notes, [Batch R7 and "Taken without asking"](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#the-design-reviews-questions-asked-2026-10-07); [XC-026 resume spec §3](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-01-xc026-resume-design.md#3-what-a-resume-does).
- **Basis:** PI decision and engineering; no published basis was found (a brief search, 2026-10-08).

### REW-12 — An adaptive procedure may choose which named reward entry a trial pays (e.g. `reward_streak`), never an amount
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI.
- **Where:** [engine spec §15](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#15-adaptivity-n12) item 7 and [§20](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#20-welfare) item 5; the brainstorm notes, [§12, Batch 2](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#12-adaptivity).
- **Basis:** PI decision; no published basis.

### REW-13 — A manual reward is one delivery of the session's `reward_correct`, strobed and recorded; it works whenever the console is up; it counts toward "stop after X mL"; with no session open it counts toward nobody
- **Decided:** 2026-09-28 and 2026-09-29, the PI.
- **Where:** [P4d-2b spec §5.0](../superpowers/specs/2026-09-26-P4d2b-browser-console-design.md#50-rulings-pi-2026-09-27-asked-in-plain-terms), [§5.1](../superpowers/specs/2026-09-26-P4d2b-browser-console-design.md#51-what-the-rig-does) and [§6.0](../superpowers/specs/2026-09-26-P4d2b-browser-console-design.md#60-rulings-pi-2026-09-29-asked-in-plain-terms); [architecture.md, "Components"](../design/architecture.md#components).
- **Basis:** PI decision. "Supplemental manual rewards are common" is the PI's statement of practice; no published source for it was found. Recording every hand reward follows the guidance to keep records of the fluid earned and of supplements [@nrc2003guidelines] (ch. 3, "Methods of Assessing Nutrition and Hydration", p. 60).

### REW-14 — The reference tasks reward only a CORRECT trial, after the target (or calibration target) hold
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, engineering default (`tasks/fixation_detection.py`, the first reference task, written that day; the others since).
- **Where:** [`tasks/fixation_detection.py`](../../tasks/fixation_detection.py), [`tasks/adaptive_detection.py`](../../tasks/adaptive_detection.py), [`tasks/visual_search.py`](../../tasks/visual_search.py), [`tasks/calibration.py`](../../tasks/calibration.py) (the `verify` and `hold` states).
- **Basis:** Engineering default, matching common practice: [@russ2015functional] — in training, monkeys held fixation on a small dot for 2 s to receive juice (Methods, "Training and Experimental Design"). That is one published instance of the convention, not an argument for it.

### REW-15 — The continuous mode can reward on a schedule or for gaze on the screen or a region
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI.
- **Where:** [engine spec §13](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#13-structure-n10-nr1-nr6) item 6 and [§20](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#20-welfare) item 4; the brainstorm notes, [§10, Batch 2](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#10-structure).
- **Basis:** PI decision, with direct published precedent in macaque free viewing: [@russ2015functional] — while watching movies, monkeys received a drop of juice every 2 s as long as their gaze stayed within the movie region, and in the face localizer every 2 s while gaze stayed within a 6° radius window (Methods, "Training and Experimental Design" and "Face Localizer Block Design").

### REW-16 — A session may be scheduled to stop at a clock time, after N trials, or after X mL of session fluid
- **Decided:** 2026-09-26, the PI (accepted from the GUI review).
- **Where:** [P4d-2b spec §4.0](../superpowers/specs/2026-09-26-P4d2b-browser-console-design.md#40-rulings-held-from-the-mockup-rounds) (the GUI review's item 8) and [§5.1](../superpowers/specs/2026-09-26-P4d2b-browser-console-design.md#51-what-the-rig-does).
- **Basis:** PI decision; operational, with no published basis. Stopping at X mL leaves any shortfall to the supplement (REW-01).

## Welfare limits

### WEL-01 — The one welfare duration limit is out of the home cage to back in it, 8 hours; chair (head-fixation) time is recorded and bounds nothing; no session-length or trial maximum
- **Decided:** 2026-09-19, the PI: the one duration limit runs out of the cage to back into it (quoted then as twelve hours), and there is no session-length maximum; 2026-10-01, the PI: "the institution's out-of-cage limit is eight hours", the twelve having been wrong. An institutional rule. M0 §3.1 item 4 carries a dated note since 2026-10-08, and S0 §7.6's storage arithmetic and P9's bring-up check 2 keep twelve hours as a stated sizing margin (inventory disagreement 2).
- **Where:** [S8 §4](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S8-session-management-design.md#4-the-bounded-config), [§5.2](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S8-session-management-design.md#52-a-restart-must-not-reset-the-day) item 4 and [§8](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S8-session-management-design.md#8-open-items) items 4 and 7; [architecture.md, "Components"](../design/architecture.md#components); [controller architecture §7.3](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-controller-architecture-design.md#73-the-bounded-config); [S9 §3](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S9-operations-console-design.md#3-running-a-session); [S9a §9](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S9a-console-design.md#9-the-telemetry-contract); [P4d-2a spec §2](../superpowers/specs/2026-09-26-P4d2a-return-to-cage-design.md#2-rulings-this-slice-rests-on); [session-levels spec §6](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-01-session-levels-and-strip-design.md#6-the-consoles-strip-decided-drawn-in-mockup-v13); [S13 §4.0](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S13-kiosk-design.md#40-a-kiosk-session-has-no-duration-bound-and-says-so); [`tasks/eight_hour_bounds.py`](../../tasks/eight_hour_bounds.py).
- **Basis:** Institutional rule; the documents name no source document; that it is set in the project authorization is our reading of the decree. No public text read sets an hour figure. [@eu2010directive] — no chair-time or out-of-cage limit: Article 33 asks for minimal restriction, and Annex VIII's severity examples give restraint durations only for metabolic cages (under 24 h mild, up to 5 days moderate), its other mentions being of "minimal restraint" with no duration. [@belgium2013koninklijk] — no restraint or out-of-cage duration; Art. 23 §1 says only that the ethics committee decides within forty working days whether to authorize a project. [@nrc2003guidelines] (ch. 3, "Physical Restraint", p. 49) — the US Guide's rule that prolonged restraint, including chairing nonhuman primates, be avoided unless it is essential and approved ("prolonged" is not defined); attention to the animal, and endpoint criteria for the restraint, growing with its duration; and the US Animal Welfare Regulations' provision (3.81(d)) that restraint longer than 12 h include a daily opportunity of at least one continuous hour unrestrained, "unless continuous restraint is justified for scientific reasons and approved by the IACUC". Chair time lies inside the out-of-cage interval, so an eight-hour cage-to-cage limit also bounds it (arithmetic), and the AWR's 12-hour provision cannot arise here. No literature found argues for a separate chair-time bound below the out-of-cage limit; the NC3Rs chair-training page (updated 21 January 2022, read 2026-10-08, not entered) gives no duration.
- **Caveat:** The eight hours are the institution's: the documents name no source document, and that the figure is set in the project authorization is our reading of the decree. Neither the EU Directive [@eu2010directive] nor the Belgian Royal Decree [@belgium2013koninklijk] sets an hour figure. The limit is cage to cage, per excursion, and a second excursion the same day starts a new interval and a new session (PI, 2026-09-20, weighing the two-record consequence). The one numeric restraint rule found is per day (US AWR 3.81(d), restraint past 12 h, unless continuous restraint is justified and approved by the IACUC, via [@nrc2003guidelines, p. 49]) and is not this institution's.

### WEL-02 — The 8-hour figure lives in each subject's bounded config, never as a code constant
- **Decided:** 2026-09-20, engineering default (S8 §5.2 item 4, "documented, not configured", written with the out-of-cage clock).
- **Where:** [S8 §5.2](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S8-session-management-design.md#52-a-restart-must-not-reset-the-day) item 4; [`tasks/eight_hour_bounds.py`](../../tasks/eight_hour_bounds.py) (the module docstring).
- **Basis:** Engineering default; no literature applies. It fits our reading that the figure is a condition of the authorized project rather than of law: the decree sets no hour figure, and its Art. 23 §1 says only that the ethics committee decides whether to authorize a project [@belgium2013koninklijk].

### WEL-03 — The out-of-cage marks are wall-clock times an operator types; every welfare duration is read on the wall clock; a departure in the future or older than the ceiling is refused; non-finite or negative numbers are refused
- **Decided:** 2026-09-20, the PI ("A clock time is what an operator reads"); 2026-09-26, the P4d-2a amendment (every welfare duration on the wall, both ends being the wl-works ELN's).
- **Where:** [S8 §5.2](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S8-session-management-design.md#52-a-restart-must-not-reset-the-day) item 4 and [§5.2c](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S8-session-management-design.md#52c-every-number-entering-the-welfare-path); [architecture.md, "Components"](../design/architecture.md#components); [P4d-2a spec §7](../superpowers/specs/2026-09-26-P4d2a-return-to-cage-design.md#7-for-pi-review-welfare-critical) and [§10](../superpowers/specs/2026-09-26-P4d2a-return-to-cage-design.md#10-amendment-2026-09-26-the-eln-owns-the-interval); [ADR-0009](../design/decisions/ADR-0009-ntp-time-synchronization.md).
- **Basis:** PI decision and engineering; no published basis.

### WEL-04 — A mark more than 30 minutes from now must be confirmed by a person; an amendment needs a reason and a named person
- **Decided:** 2026-09-20, the PI.
- **Where:** [S8 §5.2](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S8-session-management-design.md#52-a-restart-must-not-reset-the-day) item 4; [`tasks/eight_hour_bounds.py`](../../tasks/eight_hour_bounds.py) (the module docstring).
- **Basis:** PI decision ("his number, not a derived one"); no published basis.

### WEL-05 — A warning shows 30 minutes (1,800 s) before the out-of-cage limit; amber within the warning, red at the limit
- **Decided:** 2026-09-20, the PI (1,800 s accepted as a starting value, derived from no measurement); 2026-09-27, the PI (no earlier warning, P4d-2b spec §5.0); amber and red are the console strip's, decided with the session-levels spec on 2026-10-01 (§6). S9 §3 called the default a proposal until 2026-10-08 (inventory disagreement 19).
- **Where:** [S8 §5.2](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S8-session-management-design.md#52-a-restart-must-not-reset-the-day) item 4; [S9 §3](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S9-operations-console-design.md#3-running-a-session); [S9a §9](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S9a-console-design.md#9-the-telemetry-contract); [P4d-2b spec §5.0](../superpowers/specs/2026-09-26-P4d2b-browser-console-design.md#50-rulings-pi-2026-09-27-asked-in-plain-terms); [session-levels spec §6](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-01-session-levels-and-strip-design.md#6-the-consoles-strip-decided-drawn-in-mockup-v13).
- **Basis:** PI decision, labeled in S8 as not derived from any measurement; no published basis.

### WEL-06 — Each session declares its deployment (rig fixed, rig chaired, cage-side), with no default; head-fixation marks only for rig fixed; chair time absent, never zero, where unmeasured; cage-side has no duration bound but keeps the fluid floor
- **Decided:** 2026-09-19, the PI (a cage-side session has no time-based limit); 2026-09-20, the PI (three kinds, not two).
- **Where:** [S8 §5.2](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S8-session-management-design.md#52-a-restart-must-not-reset-the-day) item 4; [S13 §4.0](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S13-kiosk-design.md#40-a-kiosk-session-has-no-duration-bound-and-says-so); [S9 §2](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S9-operations-console-design.md#2-preflight) and [§3](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S9-operations-console-design.md#3-running-a-session); [architecture.md, "Components"](../design/architecture.md#components).
- **Basis:** PI decision. On the cage-side half: [@calapai2017cagebased] — a published cage-based system lets animals work in the home cage with minimal physical restraint (abstract), and fluid earned there counted as intake under fluid control (Methods, "Animals, grouping and fluid control"); [@womelsdorf2021kiosk] — "All testing proceeded in cage-mounted KS-1 kiosks in the housing rooms", and each monkey's water reward was set so that completing the task "would provide between ~150–350 ml of water", the same paragraph describing the monkeys "Without fluid control" as pausing (Materials and Methods, "Testing Procedure"); the paper does not say whether that water counted toward a daily figure. With no removal from the home cage and no restraint, the out-of-cage limit has nothing to measure (our reading); no source states a limit for cage-side sessions, or their exemption from one.

### WEL-07 — Out and back is one session: an animal returned to its cage and brought out again starts a new session
- **Decided:** 2026-09-20, the PI (asked and answered after the code was written; he weighed the two records it produces).
- **Where:** [S8 §5.2](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S8-session-management-design.md#52-a-restart-must-not-reset-the-day) item 4; [architecture.md, "Components"](../design/architecture.md#components).
- **Basis:** PI decision; no published basis.
- **Caveat:** WEL-01's caveat applies: the limit is the institution's and per excursion, a second excursion starting a new interval and a new session, while the one numeric restraint rule found is per day and not this institution's [@nrc2003guidelines, p. 49].

### WEL-08 — The out-of-cage marks get no event code, since they are operator-entered rather than measured
- **Decided:** 2026-09-20, the PI (closing S8 open item 8).
- **Where:** [S8 §5.2](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S8-session-management-design.md#52-a-restart-must-not-reset-the-day) item 4 and [§8](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S8-session-management-design.md#8-open-items) item 8; [S2 §5.1](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S2-event-vocabulary-design.md#51-in-wl-exptasks-ranges-ours-to-allocate-no-amendment-required); [S9 §3](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S9-operations-console-design.md#3-running-a-session).
- **Basis:** PI decision; no published basis.

### WEL-09 — The daylight-saving spring-forward hour is left unhandled because no experiments run at night
- **Decided:** 2026-09-20, the PI ("the dst switches happen in the night, when no experiments occur").

Pausing when the cameras fail is the PI's rule, with no source.

### WEL-16 — The kiosk uses the same bounded config with kiosk-appropriate values and no weaker path; supervision is readings plus an active alert raised by wl-works
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, the PI.
- **Where:** [S13 §4](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S13-kiosk-design.md#4-welfare-one-mechanism-different-numbers) and [§4.1](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S13-kiosk-design.md#41-supervision-and-why-the-alert-cannot-come-from-the-kiosk); [spec map, S13](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-spec-map.md#s13--cage-side-kiosk-deployment); [M0 review §3.3](../M0-REVIEW.md#33-kiosk-protocol-and-process-4) item 13.
- **Basis:** PI decision, with published context for unsupervised cage-side work: [@berger2018standardized] — automated training ran unsupervised, across sessions, in the housing environment (abstract); [@womelsdorf2021kiosk] — remote video monitoring of the animal (Materials and Methods, "Video Monitoring of Animal Behavior"). Neither describes an alerting design, and "no weaker path" is ours.

### WEL-17 — Stimulation is bounded per subject (amplitude, pulse width, frequency, train duration, duty cycle, charge per phase, charge density, refractory, deliveries per session); charge balance is verified; deliveries are counted against the RHS stim-output line
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, engineering default (S8 §4; controller architecture §10.4). The inventory noted its published safety basis as uncited.
- **Where:** [S8 §4](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S8-session-management-design.md#4-the-bounded-config); [S7 §4.2](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S7-neural-plane-design.md#42-bounds-and-why-they-are-enforced-against-observation); [controller architecture §10.4](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-controller-architecture-design.md#104-stimulation-three-tiers-and-their-bounds); [architecture.md, "Neural plane and stimulation"](../design/architecture.md#neural-plane-and-stimulation).
- **Basis:** Engineering default; the bounded quantities are the ones the stimulation-safety literature is built on. [@mccreery1990charge] — charge density and charge per phase act together to set the threshold of neural injury from stimulation (cat cortex; abstract). [@shannon1992model] — safe stimulation levels can be computed from a model fitted to the published damage data (abstract). [@cogan2008neural] — charge-balanced waveforms, and limits on the electrode's potential excursion, are the criteria for reversible charge injection (abstract), the basis for verifying charge balance. [@merrill2005electrical] — a review of charge-injection mechanisms, reversibility, and the efficacy and safety of stimulation protocols (abstract). Macaque: [@rajan2015effects] — chronic intracortical microstimulation of rhesus S1 for six months (4 h/day, 5 days/week), charge-balanced pulses of 200 µs per phase at 300 Hz and 10-100 µA (2-20 nC/phase; about 0.1-1.0 mC/cm² on the maker's stated tip area), in 1 or 5 s trains at two duty cycles, caused no detectable damage beyond that of implantation and no fine-motor deficit (Abstract; Methods, "Stimulation protocol") — a published envelope against which per-subject values can be set. The per-subject values remain protocol figures; counting against the output line is ours.

### WEL-18 — Stimulation in three tiers; tiers 1-2 in v1; tier 3 needs a refractory and a latching runaway detector on rate over a window and a session total
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, the PI.
- **Where:** [controller architecture §2](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-controller-architecture-design.md#2-decisions-taken-2026-08-31) (D8) and [§10.4](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-controller-architecture-design.md#104-stimulation-three-tiers-and-their-bounds); [S7 §4.3](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S7-neural-plane-design.md#43-tier-3-needs-two-things-tiers-1-and-2-do-not) and [§7](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S7-neural-plane-design.md#7-open-items) item 4; [M0 review §3.1](../M0-REVIEW.md#31-science-6) item 6; [roadmap M6](../roadmap.md#m6--first-real-task-first-animal) and [M9](../roadmap.md#m9--neural-contingent-loop-stimulation-tier-3).
- **Basis:** PI decision. No published source was found for a runaway detector on closed-loop stimulation (searched 2026-10-08: PubMed for closed-loop microstimulation with safety, runaway, seizure or afterdischarge in primates, no hits). The kindling literature (Goddard et al. 1969) is the natural source for limiting repeated stimulation, but no abstract could be opened, so it is not entered.

### WEL-19 — Stimulation parameters are pushed only at safe points and read back before any trial can trigger; the gating decision stays in `taskd`
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, engineering default.
- **Where:** [S7 §2](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S7-neural-plane-design.md#2-the-decision-stays-in-taskd) and [§4.1](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S7-neural-plane-design.md#41-the-path); [controller architecture §10.4](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-controller-architecture-design.md#104-stimulation-three-tiers-and-their-bounds).
- **Basis:** Engineering default; no published basis.

### WEL-20 — The tracker's IR at each eye is measured with a power meter at bring-up and the driver current capped for the longest session; human limits apply; transfer to macaques is UNVERIFIED; IEC 62471 was not read
- **Decided:** 2026-09-27, S0 §7.1.2's eye-safety requirement (a published guideline for human eyes, applied).
- **Where:** [S0 §7.1.2](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S0-hosts-and-hardware-design.md#712-the-per-eye-light-its-driver-and-eye-safety), "Eye safety".
- **Basis:** [@icnirp2013icnirp] (full text, ICNIRP's PDF with its erratum page). S0's figures are right: for the cornea and lens at 780 nm-3 µm the limit is 18·t^−0.75 kW m⁻² for t < 1000 s and 100 W m⁻² for t ≥ 1000 s (eqns 20-21, p. 88). An unweighted thermal-detector reading is conservative (p. 88); the eqn 19 action spectrum weights 780-1000 nm by 0.3 for infrared LEDs, about a threefold allowance (p. 94). The transfer question is narrowed, not closed: the limits rest mainly on injury thresholds from experimental exposure of animals (p. 82), and the guideline does not say which species underlie the cornea and lens IR limit. Eqn 9's constant, which S0 could not read from the garbled text layer, is restated on the erratum page as 6300 × α⁻¹ W m⁻² sr⁻¹ for t ≥ 100 s (the near-IR retinal radiance limit for a weak visual stimulus). ICNIRP relies on the avoidance response that pain induces at painfully warm levels (p. 81; p. 88 for skin past 10 s), and says it fails at very high irradiance: pain sensations "usually induce an avoidance response within a few seconds that prevent a burn, with the exception of very high irradiances that rapidly heat the skin" (p. 81, citing Randolph & Stuck 1976 on the rhesus cornea, not entered). A head-fixed animal largely cannot make that response; S0 caps at the long-exposure limit, which does not depend on it (our reading). No macaque-specific IR exposure limit with an abstract was found (searched 2026-10-08); Goldman, Ham & Mueller 1977 gives rhesus damage thresholds for ultrashort laser pulses, has no abstract in PubMed, and is not entered.

### WEL-21 — A hard optical cap: a neutral-density attenuator per eye, OD chosen at bring-up for the driver's maximum current; the trim pot at 1000 mA or less and sealed
- **Decided:** 2026-09-28, the PI.
- **Where:** [S0 §7.10](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S0-hosts-and-hardware-design.md#710-eye-light-drivers-and-the-safety-cap), "Eye-light drivers and the safety cap"; [S0 §7.1](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S0-hosts-and-hardware-design.md#71-the-eye-tracker-changes-from-the-openirisdpi-reference-build) row 5c.
- **Basis:** PI decision. It is an engineering implementation of WEL-20's limit [@icnirp2013icnirp] (eqns 20-21, p. 88); no published source exists for the cap's design.

### WEL-22 — Behavior lamps (850 nm) sit outside the animal's view with a visible-cut filter, strobe only during exposures, and are checked dark at the eye position by a dark-adapted observer; whether rhesus see 850 nm at these levels is UNVERIFIED
- **Decided:** 2026-09-27, the PI; the lamps' IEC 62471 Exempt Group rating is Smart Vision Lights' own. S0 §7.7's account of the filter was corrected on 2026-10-08 (below).
- **Where:** [S0 §7.7](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S0-hosts-and-hardware-design.md#77-the-behavior-lamps-850-nm) and [§7.2](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S0-hosts-and-hardware-design.md#72-the-behavior-camera-system-p9) row C20; [P9 §2](../superpowers/specs/2026-09-27-P9-camera-system-design.md#2-hardware-and-timing) and [§6](../superpowers/specs/2026-09-27-P9-camera-system-design.md#6-testing-and-bring-up).
- **Basis:** PI decision. [@sliney1976visual] — the human eye responds to near-infrared laser light out to at least 1064 nm (abstract); the publisher's abstract page also shows the paper's Table I, with a measured human foveal threshold at 850 nm orders of magnitude above that for red light, so 850 nm itself can be seen at enough radiance, and an observer check at session settings is the right test. The LP830 long-pass passes 850 nm, so it only trims the lamps' shorter-wavelength tail and the darkness check at the eye position is the safeguard; S0 §7.7 said the filter cut "where a faint red glow comes from" until 2026-10-08. Whether rhesus see 850 nm at these levels stays UNVERIFIED: no macaque near-IR psychophysics was found (searched 2026-10-08: PubMed, titles with infrared and monkey, macaque or rhesus and vision, visible or sensitivity; one hit, a damage-threshold paper). The IEC 62471 rating is the maker's own test; the standard was not read.

### WEL-25 — There is no break rule: "Mandatory breaks", listed among the bounded session settings with no rule or value, was a leftover and is removed
- **Decided:** 2026-10-08, the PI (asked in the question box): S8 §4's "Mandatory breaks" is a leftover, and no break rule exists. The phrase had stood at the end of the table's Session row since S8 was written (2026-08-31), with no rule or value defined anywhere; it was removed on 2026-10-08 with a dated note.
- **Where:** [S8 §4](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S8-session-management-design.md#4-the-bounded-config) (the table, "Session", and its dated note).
- **Basis:** PI decision. The only numeric break rule found is American: restraint longer than 12 h must include a daily opportunity of at least one continuous hour unrestrained, "unless continuous restraint is justified for scientific reasons and approved by the IACUC" [@nrc2003guidelines] (ch. 3, p. 49, citing AWR 3.81(d)), which cannot arise under an eight-hour out-of-cage limit. The EU Directive, the Belgian decree and the NC3Rs chair-training page give no break rule; nothing found requires breaks.

## Task design and psychophysics

### TSK-01 — The experimental program: mostly saccadic choice, sometimes joystick or touch, sometimes passive fixation; gaze-contingent changes; free viewing and discrete trials; working-memory delays; microstimulation; auditory stimuli and feedback; v1 is the training ladder plus a first 2D monocular recording task
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, the PI (the controller architecture's D2 and §15).
- **Where:** [controller architecture §2](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-controller-architecture-design.md#2-decisions-taken-2026-08-31) (D2) and [§15](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-controller-architecture-design.md#15-feature-inventory-derived-from-the-science); [roadmap, "Governing constraint"](../roadmap.md#roadmap); [M0 review §1](../M0-REVIEW.md#1-the-decisions-this-rests-on).
- **Basis:** PI decision, which sets the lab's program; no paper supports the program as a whole. The core macaque paradigms it names are established: [@fuchs1967saccadic] — monkeys can be trained to follow a large variety of target trajectories, and their saccades and pursuit are qualitatively like humans' (abstract, items 1-2 and 6); [@bruce1985primate] — visually guided saccades, and saccades delayed after target onset, in trained macaques (abstract); [@funahashi1989mnemonic] — the oculomotor delayed-response (working-memory) task: the monkey fixates through a 0.5 s cue and a 1-6 s delay, then saccades to the remembered location (abstract, item 1).

### TSK-02 — The engine must serve the PI's thirty-four named paradigms (RSVP, adaptation, rule-based cues, priming, memory- and visually-guided saccades, audiovisual decisions, binocular rivalry and flash suppression, RF mapping, tuning, curve tracing, figure-ground, search, 2+AFC, masking, most-exciting images, gaze-contingent and closed-loop presentation, M/P/K mapping, betting, and more)
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (the brainstorm notes, §1).
- **Where:** [engine spec §1](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#1-why); the brainstorm notes, [§1, "What can be shown"](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#1-what-can-be-shown).
- **Basis:** PI decision; the list is the PI's. Canonical sources a methods section would cite for several of the named paradigms: memory-guided saccades, [@funahashi1989mnemonic] (abstract); visual search in macaques, [@schall1993neural] (abstract), and pop-out search, [@thompson1996perceptual] (abstract); priming of pop-out, [@bichot2002priming] (abstract) and [@westerberg2020priming] (full text); binocular rivalry with a perceptual report in macaques, [@logothetis1989neuronal] (abstract); continuous flash suppression, [@tsuchiya2005continuous] (abstract; human); curve tracing, [@roelfsema1998objectbased] (abstract); texture-defined figure-ground, [@lamme1995neurophysiology] (abstract); most-exciting images by closed-loop image evolution, [@ponce2019evolving] (abstract); opt-out wagering ("betting") on a motion decision, [@kiani2009representation] (abstract); RSVP, [@potter1976shortterm] (abstract; human); backward masking and metacontrast, [@breitmeyer2000recent] (abstract; a review); reverse-correlation RF mapping, [@ringach1997subspace] (abstract).

### TSK-03 — A stimulus is a shape, filled, seen through an edge, placed and layered; named kinds are shorthands (a Gabor is a circle, a grating fill and a Gaussian edge); a new block enters as reviewed framework code with a person-readable report of example frames, reviewed by a lab member
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (the brainstorm notes, §1).
- **Where:** [engine spec §4.1](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#41-the-stimulus-model-building-blocks-and-reviewed-extensions) and [§4.10](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#410-adding-a-block-reviewed-extensions); the brainstorm notes, [§1](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#1-what-can-be-shown).
- **Basis:** PI decision; the Gabor shorthand is published. [@daugman1985uncertainty] — the 2D Gabor function is a sinusoid under a Gaussian envelope, the elliptic generalization of Gabor's 1946 elementary functions (abstract). The block model, and the rule that a lab member reviews each new block, have no published basis.

### TSK-04 — The fill set: gratings (sine, square, triangle, sawtooth; drift and counterphase); noise (white, binary, pink 1/f, band-pass, color); RF mapping (sparse noise, dense noise or m-sequence, flashed and swept bars, subspace gratings); texture figures (orientation, motion, color, disparity); Mondrians; masks (noise, pattern, metacontrast, four-dot object substitution, full-screen)
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (the brainstorm notes, §1 Batches 2, 5 and 6). The inventory found the published methods behind it uncited.
- **Where:** [engine spec §4.2](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#42-the-first-set-of-blocks) and [§4.3](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#43-what-each-fill-must-do-n1-batches-2-5-6) (the table); the brainstorm notes, [§1, Batches 2, 5 and 6](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#1-what-can-be-shown).
- **Basis:** PI decision; the established methods behind its members: [@campbell1968application] — grating thresholds for sine, square, rectangular and sawtooth waveforms, related through their Fourier components (abstract, items 1-4); [@field1987relations] — the amplitude spectrum of natural images falls roughly as 1/f, so power falls as 1/f² and each octave carries equal energy (p. 2385, Eqs. 7-9; Fig. 8), the spectrum the decision's "pink 1/f" names (Field does not use the word "pink"); [@ringach1997subspace] — subspace reverse correlation: each refresh shows an image drawn from a finite orthonormal set, and the cross-correlation recovers the RF's projection onto that subspace (abstract, which does not name the Hartley basis); [@reid1997use] — spatiotemporal m-sequence (dense binary noise) RF mapping (abstract); [@chichilnisky2001simple] — white-noise analysis of light responses (abstract); [@gur1997visual] — bars swept across the RF in alert macaques (abstract); [@lamme1995neurophysiology] — figures defined against the ground by orientation or by motion direction (abstract); [@dilollo2000competition] — masking by object substitution, where the target and mask are followed by the mask alone (abstract); [@breitmeyer2000recent] — backward masking and metacontrast (abstract). No source was opened for Mondrians, color noise or disparity-defined texture.

### TSK-05 — Moving dots declare coherence, direction, speed, lifetime, size, density and aperture, and the noise-dot rule (replotted, random direction, random walk) per task, since it changes what a coherence level means
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (the brainstorm notes, §1 Batch 2). The inventory found the published basis uncited.
- **Where:** [engine spec §4.3](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#43-what-each-fill-must-do-n1-batches-2-5-6) (the moving-dots row); [S1a §4](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S1a-vocabulary-design.md#4-stimuli); the brainstorm notes, [§1, Batch 2](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#1-what-can-be-shown).
- **Basis:** PI decision, on published comparisons. [@pilly2009what] — the four common algorithms (white noise, Movshon/Newsome, limited lifetime and Brownian; §1, A-D) differ mostly in how the signal dots are chosen and displaced, Brownian motion being the only one that changes the noise rule ("noise dots are only given random directions"); it reports "dramatic" differences in performance between and within algorithms and asks for RDM details in methods (abstract); [@scase1996what] — random-position, random-walk and random-direction noise gave similar coherence thresholds in its range, with small but systematic differences, thresholds higher for random direction (abstract), and its practical message is that thresholds "are not much affected by the choice of noise"; [@britten1992analysis] — coherence as the motion-strength variable in macaque direction discrimination (abstract). The three noise rules the decision names (replotted, random direction, random walk) are Scase et al.'s. The two comparisons disagree on how much the choice matters.

### TSK-06 — Random content is deterministic (a counter-based generator of seed, frame and element, with frozen reference outputs); seeds recorded, a fresh seed per run, replayable; a task may have its frames saved (for reverse correlation on pixels)
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (the brainstorm notes, §1 Batch 6 and §11 Batch 2); the generator and its frozen outputs are engineering.
- **Where:** [engine spec §4.3](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#43-what-each-fill-must-do-n1-batches-2-5-6) ("Random content is deterministic") and [§14](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#14-variation-n11-nr1-nr2) item 6; [S4 §5](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S4-stimulus-presentation-design.md#5-motion-is-a-function-not-a-trajectory); the brainstorm notes, [§1, Batch 6](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#1-what-can-be-shown) and [§11, Batch 2](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#11-variation).
- **Basis:** PI decision and engineering, on published methods. [@salmon2011parallel] — counter-based generators are keyed transformations of a counter: each value can be computed independently, without sequential state (abstract; §1); the paper's own generators pass TestU01's BigCrush ("All our PRNGs pass…", abstract), a claim about those generators, not about counter-based generators as a class. [@chichilnisky2001simple] (abstract) and [@reid1997use] (abstract) — RF estimates come from the exact stimulus sequence shown, which is why frames must be exactly reconstructable; an m-sequence is itself a deterministic pseudorandom stimulus. Seeding and replay as such are engineering.

### TSK-07 — Four ways to overlap (opacity; contrasts add, for plaids, signal in noise, transparent motion; a window or scotoma; multiplication); layouts (ring, grid, listed, random with spacing rules, clusters); each item its own stimulus, target and distractor being roles; a group is shown, moved and coded as one
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (the brainstorm notes, §1 Batches 1, 6 and 7).
- **Where:** [engine spec §4.4](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#44-combining-grouping-and-layouts); the brainstorm notes, [§1, Batches 1, 6 and 7](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#1-what-can-be-shown).
- **Basis:** PI decision, with published precedent for adding contrasts and for roles. [@adelson1982phenomenal] — two superimposed moving gratings form a plaid, which coheres or slides depending on their relative contrasts, spatial frequencies and directions (abstract); [@snowden1991response] — transparent motion from superimposed random-dot surfaces, recorded in macaque V1 and MT (abstract); [@westerberg2023feedforward] — target and distractors are roles within one array (Methods, "Task design: Pop-out search"). Groups and layouts are the PI's.

### TSK-08 — Gabor and Grating stay separate because the envelope (Gaussian against a hard aperture) changes edge artifacts and spatial-frequency bandwidth; field names follow methods sections (`sf`, `coherence`, `sigma`)
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, engineering default (S1a §4 and §9). The inventory found the published convention uncited.
- **Where:** [S1a §4](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S1a-vocabulary-design.md#4-stimuli) ("Field names throughout") and [§9](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S1a-vocabulary-design.md#9-appearances).
- **Basis:** Engineering default; the Gaussian envelope's property is published. [@daugman1985uncertainty] — Gaussian-windowed sinusoids reach the theoretical lower limit of joint uncertainty in position, spatial frequency and orientation (abstract). That a hard edge adds broadband spatial-frequency energy follows from Fourier analysis; no single paper was opened for it. Naming fields after methods-section usage has no published source.
