### TSK-09 — A catch trial shows an explicit `Blank` at the moment a stimulus would have appeared, keeping catch and non-catch trials structurally identical
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, engineering default (S1a §9).
- **Where:** [S1a §9](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S1a-vocabulary-design.md#9-appearances), "Appearances".
- **Basis:** Engineering default; what catch trials are for is published. Catch (target-absent) trials make false alarms and correct rejections measurable: [@luo2015neuronal] — a macaque detection task with target and nontarget trials, each scored as hit, miss, false alarm or correct rejection (Experimental Procedures, "Dissociation Task"); [@stanislaw1999calculation] (abstract). Showing an explicit `Blank`, so that both kinds of trial share one structure, has no published source.

### TSK-10 — The display is state: `Show` persists until `Hide`; `Update` changes a stimulus without the offset transient `Hide`+`Show` inserts (a confound for change detection); a window names the stimulus it scores, or `REMEMBERED`
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, engineering default (S1a §12, added after that day's review).
- **Where:** [S1a §12](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S1a-vocabulary-design.md#12-added-2026-08-31-after-review--what-the-graph-could-not-see), "The display is state"; [pitfalls P18](../pitfalls.md#expanded-notes).
- **Basis:** Engineering default; the confound is published. [@rensink1997see] — the flicker paradigm puts 80 ms blank fields between the original and the changed image, on a hypothesis the introduction states conditionally, that they "swamp the local motion signals" a change otherwise produces (p. 369, before the General Method); with the blanks, central-interest changes took on average 7.3 alternations (4.7 s) to be seen and marginal-interest changes 17.1 (10.9 s), against 1.4 (0.9 s) without the blanks (p. 370, Experiment 1). An inserted offset transient is therefore not neutral for change detection. The state model itself is engineering.

### TSK-11 — Outcome taxonomy: correct, early and late responses; wrong target, early and late errors; no fixation, no response, abort (not a break); fixation, target, catch and motion breaks; fault split from abort; blink break apart from tracker loss
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, engineering default (S1a §7, the thirteen outcomes); 2026-09-01, the PI (S1a §13, the five added after review).
- **Where:** [S1a §7](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S1a-vocabulary-design.md#7-outcomes) and [§13](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S1a-vocabulary-design.md#13-outcomes-extended-2026-09-01--signal-detection-and-the-rig); [`tasks/allocation.py`](../../tasks/allocation.py) (the outcome reason codes).
- **Basis:** Engineering default and PI decision; the field's standard codes are the precedent. [@nimhmonkeylogic2026runtime] — software documentation: the trialerror codes 0-9 are correct, no response, late response, break fixation, no fixation, early response, incorrect, lever break, ignored and aborted (the `trialerror` entry); [@asaad2008flexible] — a task script saves an error code and aborts the trial when the subject fails a step, such as breaking fixation (Results, "Usability: Task Scripting"); [@hwang2019nimh] — NIMH MonkeyLogic is backward compatible with those scripts (abstract). The lab's further splits have no published source: fault from abort, blink break from tracker loss, and target, catch and motion breaks. Today a look that leaves the target before its hold ends is a `TARGET_BREAK` in S1a §7 and in `visual_search`, and a `WRONG_TARGET` in `fixation_detection` and `adaptive_detection` (inventory disagreement 10); no dated decision covers which.

### TSK-12 — Correct rejection and false alarm are outcomes so d′ and criterion are computable; a correct rejection marks `TRIAL_CORRECT` and counts as correct on the console strip
- **Decided:** 2026-09-01, the PI (S1a §13); 2026-09-26, the PI (the strip's one rollup). The inventory found signal detection theory uncited.
- **Where:** [S1a §13](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S1a-vocabulary-design.md#13-outcomes-extended-2026-09-01--signal-detection-and-the-rig); [S9a §9](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S9a-console-design.md#9-the-telemetry-contract); [P4d-2b spec §3](../superpowers/specs/2026-09-26-P4d2b-browser-console-design.md#3-telemetry-and-health-decided) ("One rollup is ruled") and [§4.0](../superpowers/specs/2026-09-26-P4d2b-browser-console-design.md#40-rulings-held-from-the-mockup-rounds).
- **Basis:** PI decision, on signal detection theory. [@luo2015neuronal] — macaque trials scored as hit, miss, false alarm or correct rejection, with c = −½[Φ⁻¹(H) + Φ⁻¹(FA)] and d′ = Φ⁻¹(H) − Φ⁻¹(FA) (Experimental Procedures, "Criterion and Sensitivity" and "Dissociation Task"); hit rate alone conflates criterion and sensitivity: "any improvements in hit rate could depend on a change in only criterion, only sensitivity, or both" (Introduction). [@stanislaw1999calculation] — formulas for SDT measures across the common tasks (abstract). Counting a correct rejection as correct on the strip is a console convention.

### TSK-13 — A trial may record several scored responses (several correct targets; free viewing) with one terminal outcome; the census counts responses as well as outcomes
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, settled in S1a §8.
- **Where:** [S1a §8](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S1a-vocabulary-design.md#8-multiple-responses-in-one-trial--settled-2026-08-31).
- **Basis:** Engineering; no published basis. Searched 2026-10-08: the NIMH MonkeyLogic runtime documentation (one trialerror code per trial), PubMed for free-viewing macaque task conventions, and the Asaad & Eskandar 2008 full text; none gives a convention for scoring several responses under one terminal outcome.

### TSK-14 — Sequences (RSVP, predictive, masking streams) are one sequence stimulus or one state per item; motion paths are straight sweeps, pursuit targets (step-ramp, sinusoidal, circular, Lissajous), waypoints and seeded random walks
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (the brainstorm notes, §3 Batch 2).
- **Where:** [engine spec §6](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#6-when-n3-nr3) items 6-7; the brainstorm notes, [§3, Batch 2](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#3-when).
- **Basis:** PI decision; two of the named forms have classic sources. [@potter1976shortterm] — RSVP of pictures at 113-333 ms each (abstract; human); [@fuchs1967saccadic] — macaques trained to track "a large variety of target trajectories" with saccades and smooth pursuit (abstract, item 1). The step-ramp's source (Rashbass 1961) could not be opened and is not entered.

### TSK-15 — A non-aging (constant-hazard) foreperiod declares its minimum, mean, maximum and tail rule; the review report plots the hazard it actually produces (a truncated exponential's hazard rises near its maximum)
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (the brainstorm notes, §11 Batch 2). The inventory found the published basis uncited.
- **Where:** [engine spec §6](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#6-when-n3-nr3) item 11, [§14](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#14-variation-n11-nr1-nr2) item 4 and [§18](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#18-recording-and-review-n15) item 1; the brainstorm notes, [§11, Batch 2](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#11-variation).
- **Basis:** PI decision, on the PI's own published practice. [@westerberg2020priming] — the fixation-to-array time was drawn from a non-aging foreperiod function of 750-1250 ms "to reduce expectancy" (Materials and Methods, "Experimental design and behavior"); [@westerberg2023feedforward] — the same, "to eliminate any potential effect of stimulus expectation" (Methods, "Task design: Pop-out search"); [@han2022revisiting] — a non-aging distribution keeps the conditional probability of stimulus onset constant over the foreperiod ("Prior studies", "Repetition priming account"). Why an uncontrolled hazard is a confound: [@janssen2005representation] — macaque LIP represents the hazard rate of a go signal (abstract); [@ghose2002attentional] — macaque V4 attentional modulation follows the time-varying probability of a change (abstract). That a truncated exponential's hazard rises near its maximum is a mathematical property.

### TSK-16 — Every trial records the time since the last interruption and its realized interval before the stimulus; a block may declare what to do on resuming (e.g. rerun an initial adaptation)
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, engineering default (from the design reviews, engine spec §6 item 12).
- **Where:** [engine spec §6](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#6-when-n3-nr3) item 12.
- **Basis:** Engineering default, on published findings. [@patterson2013distinct] — in macaque V1, how quickly responses recover after the adapter goes off depends on how long adaptation lasted, recovery being slower after prolonged adaptation (abstract): an interruption changes the adaptation state, which supports recording it and rerunning an initial adaptation. [@janssen2005representation] — macaques track the elapsed-time hazard (abstract), which is why the realized pre-stimulus interval matters.

### TSK-17 — Between trials the screen shows what the task declares: the background by default, or kept stimuli (a fixation point, a topped-up adapter), or an ITI display
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (the brainstorm notes, §3 Batch 3).
- **Where:** [engine spec §6](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#6-when-n3-nr3) item 9; the brainstorm notes, [§3, Batch 3](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#3-when).
- **Basis:** PI decision. Supporting the kept adapter: [@patterson2013distinct] — adaptation effects decay after the adapter goes off (abstract), so keeping an adapter between trials is how an adaptation state is held. No source specific to a top-up protocol was opened (Kohn & Movshon 2003's abstract does not state one).

### TSK-18 — Parameter changes are staged and applied atomically at the trial boundary; if regenerating stimuli overruns, the ITI extends rather than dropping frames; logic changes only by a recorded task reload
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, the PI (D9: live parameter control, applied atomically at trial boundaries); the ITI rule and the reload are engineering (S8 §3.2, the controller architecture §7.2). The engine spec §17 item 2 adds parameters a task declares instant (TSK-20), and lists S8 §3.2's amendment (§22).
- **Where:** [S8 §3.2](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S8-session-management-design.md#32-application); [controller architecture §2](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-controller-architecture-design.md#2-decisions-taken-2026-08-31) (D9) and [§7.2](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-controller-architecture-design.md#72-application-and-provenance); [pitfalls P16](../pitfalls.md#expanded-notes).
- **Basis:** PI decision and engineering; precedent, not evidence. [@asaad2008flexible] (Results, "Performance: General Performance" and "Video Performance") — MonkeyLogic selects the next condition and loads its stimuli into video memory during the ITI, never within a trial, and shortens the scheduled ITI by the measured preparation time to keep the actual ITI constant. This rule instead lengthens the ITI on an overrun. Atomic staging and the reload rule are engineering.

### TSK-19 — A live edit to a value a condition sets wins, and the trial still counts toward its condition; the parameter-change code goes out and the trial table carries an "overridden" flag
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (the brainstorm notes, Batch R2; offered against: the condition wins, or the trial is not counted).
- **Where:** [engine spec §14](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#14-variation-n11-nr1-nr2) item 7; the brainstorm notes, [§11, Batch 1](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#11-variation) and [Batch R2](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#the-design-reviews-questions-asked-2026-10-07).
- **Basis:** PI decision; an operational and data-integrity choice with no published basis. The MonkeyLogic and Asaad & Eskandar descriptions of between-trial changes cover loading at the ITI, not live edits (searched 2026-10-08).

### TSK-20 — A live edit takes effect at the next trial boundary, except a parameter the task declares instant, which changes on the next frame
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (the brainstorm notes, Batch R7), superseding the PI's 2026-09-19 "every name alike".
- **Where:** [engine spec §17](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#17-parameters-and-live-control-n14-nr7) item 2; the brainstorm notes, [§14, Batch 1](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#14-parameters-and-live-control) and [Batch R7](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#the-design-reviews-questions-asked-2026-10-07).
- **Basis:** PI decision; no published basis.

### TSK-21 — Trial-to-trial variation is named conditions for the design's factors (counted, balanced, numbered) plus drawn values for nuisance variables (array rotation, jitter); a parameter is set by one or the other, never both
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (the brainstorm notes, §11 Batch 1).
- **Where:** [engine spec §14](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#14-variation-n11-nr1-nr2) item 1; the brainstorm notes, [§11, Batch 1](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#11-variation).
- **Basis:** PI decision. Precedent for named conditions: [@asaad2008flexible] — a conditions table defines each trial type, "the collection of stimuli that are employed for a particular trial" (Results, "Design"). The rule that a parameter is set by a condition or drawn, never both, has no published source.

### TSK-22 — Factorial designs: factors and levels, combinations minus declared exclusions, each named from its levels
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (the brainstorm notes, §11 Batch 1).
- **Where:** [engine spec §14](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#14-variation-n11-nr1-nr2) item 2; the brainstorm notes, [§11, Batch 1](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#11-variation).
- **Basis:** PI decision; standard experimental design, which a methods section does not normally cite. No source was opened (Fisher's *The Design of Experiments* was not).

### TSK-23 — Orderings, declared per block: shuffled passes, weighted draws (e.g. 20% catch trials), limits on repeats, sequence-balanced (e.g. de Bruijn); randomization seeded and recorded
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, the PI (an order declared per block, in `scheduler.py`), with randomization seeded and recorded (S8 §2); 2026-10-07, the PI (the orderings, the brainstorm notes, §11 Batch 1). The inventory found the published basis uncited.
- **Where:** [engine spec §14](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#14-variation-n11-nr1-nr2) item 3; [S8 §2](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S8-session-management-design.md#2-the-trial-scheduler); [runtime-state note §1.4](../research/2026-10-07-engine-runtime-state.md#14-ordering-and-randomization); the brainstorm notes, [§11, Batch 1](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#11-variation).
- **Basis:** PI decision, on published methods. [@aguirre2011de] — de Bruijn cycles as pseudo-random stimulus sequences with arbitrary levels of counterbalance, for studies of habituation, bias, anticipation and stimulus history (abstract); [@brooks2012counterbalancing] — serial-order carryover makes counterbalancing condition order "an essential part of experimental design", and counterbalanced sequences come from Euler circuits through a graph of conditions (abstract); [@nimhmonkeylogic2026task] — software documentation: condition selection random with or without replacement, listed alongside increasing, decreasing and user-defined orders ("Conditions"); [@bichot2002priming] — macaque pop-out performance depends on whether the previous trial's features and target position repeat (abstract), which is why the realized sequence is worth recording and controlling.

### TSK-24 — Drawn-value distributions: uniform; weighted set; normal, truncated to the range; a prepared list; a non-aging foreperiod
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (the brainstorm notes, §11 Batch 2).
- **Where:** [engine spec §14](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#14-variation-n11-nr1-nr2) item 4; the brainstorm notes, [§11, Batch 2](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#11-variation).
- **Basis:** PI decision. For the non-aging foreperiod, as TSK-15: [@han2022revisiting] ("Prior studies", "Repetition priming account") and [@westerberg2020priming] (Materials and Methods). The other distributions (uniform, a weighted set, truncated normal, a prepared list) have no published source.

### TSK-25 — Structure: block types are named sets of conditions and blocks their temporal instances; block order fixed, cyclic, seeded and balanced, or by criterion with advance and fall-back; a block ends on N trials, all targets met, a performance criterion (e.g. 80% correct over the last 20 completed) or a time limit; interludes (recalibration, quick RF map, rest) return to the same block; several trial structures per task
- **Decided:** 2026-10-01, the PI (block types and blocks, the session-levels spec §2); 2026-10-07, the PI (the brainstorm notes, §10 Batches 1 and 3).
- **Where:** [engine spec §13](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#13-structure-n10-nr1-nr6) items 3-5; [session-levels spec §2](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-01-session-levels-and-strip-design.md#2-the-vocabulary-decided); [S8 §1](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S8-session-management-design.md#1-structure); [controller architecture §5.5](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-controller-architecture-design.md#55-session-structure); the brainstorm notes, [§10, Batches 1 and 3](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#10-structure).
- **Basis:** PI decision, with published precedent. [@asaad2008flexible] (Results, "Design" and "Usability: Configuration menu") — blocks can switch on a learning criterion, "detecting when a subject has learned and only then switching blocks", and performance can be judged by a moving average over the last n trials against a threshold; [@nimhmonkeylogic2026task] — software documentation: a block-change function "is called after every trial to determine whether a new block should be initiated" ("Blocks"); [@berger2018standardized] — standardized, automated macaque training through successively more complex task levels, with task conditions updated automatically from each animal's progress (abstract). The 80%-over-20 example and the interlude rule have no published source.

### TSK-26 — What counts toward a condition's target is declared per block ("run until ten correct" and "show the array a hundred times" are both ordinary); a criterion is judged over a set separate from the counted set, or it tracks engagement
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, the PI (what counts is declared per block, which `scheduler.Counting`'s docstring attributes to the PI, as the runtime-state note quotes it); judging a criterion over a set separate from the counted one (`Block.criterion_over`) is engineering, its comment attributing it to no one.
- **Where:** [runtime-state note §1.3](../research/2026-10-07-engine-runtime-state.md#13-targets-counts-length-rules) (quoting `scheduler.py`); [`wl_xcon/scheduler.py`](../../wl_xcon/scheduler.py) (`Counting`, `Block.criterion_over`).
- **Basis:** PI decision (what counts) and engineering (the criterion set). Precedent: [@asaad2008flexible] judges a performance threshold over a declared subset, "only a certain subset of conditions, say conditions 3 and 4, in only the current block" (Results, "Design"), the same separation of the judged set from the counted set.

### TSK-27 — By default every trial with an answer counts toward its condition (correct, wrong, early and late variants, and where withholding is an answer, misses, correct rejections and false alarms); trials ending before the decision (breaks, no fixation, faults) do not
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (the brainstorm notes, Batch R1), superseding §13 Batch 1's "responded trials".
- **Where:** [engine spec §16](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#16-counting-and-repeats-n13-nr1-nr2) item 1; the brainstorm notes, [Batch R1](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#the-design-reviews-questions-asked-2026-10-07) (superseding [§13, Batch 1](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#13-aborts-and-repeats)).
- **Basis:** PI decision. That a miss, a correct rejection and a false alarm are answers is signal detection theory: [@luo2015neuronal] (Experimental Procedures, "Dissociation Task"); [@stanislaw1999calculation] (abstract). Not counting trials that end before the decision has no published source.

### TSK-28 — Counters distinguish attempted, completed and correct per condition, and the console shows achieved against target
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, engineering default (S8 §2; the controller architecture §5.5).
- **Where:** [S8 §2](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S8-session-management-design.md#2-the-trial-scheduler); [controller architecture §5.5](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-controller-architecture-design.md#55-session-structure).
- **Basis:** Engineering default; precedent only. [@asaad2008flexible] — the experimenter's display shows "the performance within each of these groups" (trial, condition and block), beside a reaction-time histogram (Results, "Usability: Experimenter's display").

### TSK-29 — Repeated by default: fixation break, no fixation, tracker lost, fault; wrong target is not repeated; for other breaks the task chooses whether the trial is spent (counted, marked abort) or owed
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, the PI (a wrong target is not repeated); 2026-10-07, the PI (the brainstorm notes, Batch R1; inventory disagreement 3). S8 §2 still gives the 2026-08-31 rule (fixation breaks re-queued at the end of the block), and the engine spec §22 lists its amendment.
- **Where:** [engine spec §16](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#16-counting-and-repeats-n13-nr1-nr2) item 2 and [§22](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#22-amendments-to-other-documents-each-with-the-build-that-changes-it); [S8 §2](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S8-session-management-design.md#2-the-trial-scheduler); the brainstorm notes, [§13, Batch 1](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#13-aborts-and-repeats) and [Batch R1](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#the-design-reviews-questions-asked-2026-10-07).
- **Basis:** PI decision. How error trials are handled is a standard configurable choice: NIMH MonkeyLogic offers to ignore errors, to repeat immediately ("the same condition played repeatedly until the trial succeeds") or to repeat later [@nimhmonkeylogic2026task] ("On error"); the 2008 paper names only immediate repetition ("immediately repeat incorrect trials") [@asaad2008flexible] (Results, "Usability: Configuration menu"). The PI's reason, that an animal must not choose its next trial by erring, is the PI's. An analogy only: monkeys offered a rewarded sure option chose it according to their uncertainty [@kiani2009representation] (abstract), which does not show animals erring or breaking fixation to avoid a condition.

### TSK-30 — A repeated trial goes to a random later point in its block, never the very next trial, unless it is the only condition still owed (then at once, recorded); this supersedes "end of the block"
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (the brainstorm notes, Batch R2), superseding the PI's rule of 2026-08-31, "end of the block" (inventory disagreement 3).
- **Where:** [engine spec §16](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#16-counting-and-repeats-n13-nr1-nr2) item 3; the brainstorm notes, [§13, Batch 1](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#13-aborts-and-repeats) and [Batch R2](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#the-design-reviews-questions-asked-2026-10-07). The superseded rule stands in [S8 §2](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S8-session-management-design.md#2-the-trial-scheduler) and [§8](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S8-session-management-design.md#8-open-items) item 3 until their amendment, and in [M0 review §3.1](../M0-REVIEW.md#31-science-6) item 5 as that review's record.
- **Basis:** PI decision, matching an established option. [@nimhmonkeylogic2026task] — "repeat delayed" "throws error trials back into the queue, but at a later point in the block", while "repeat immediately" is the alternative the PI rejected ("On error"). No paper evaluating the two was found.

### TSK-31 — Blocks whose order is the design (priming, sequence-balanced) declare their repeat rule, default none, and the realized sequence is recorded (aborted predecessors included); a repeat's drawn values are fresh or the same per task; past a per-condition cap the debt is forgiven, the shortfall recorded and flagged as possible avoidance; stated limitation: conditions broken on more drift toward the block's end
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (the brainstorm notes, Batches R1 and R2).
- **Where:** [engine spec §16](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#16-counting-and-repeats-n13-nr1-nr2) items 4-7; the brainstorm notes, [§13, Batch 1](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#13-aborts-and-repeats) and [Batches R1 and R2](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#the-design-reviews-questions-asked-2026-10-07).
- **Basis:** PI decision, on published findings. Order as the design: in priming of pop-out the previous trial's features and target position change performance, [@bichot2002priming] (abstract) and [@westerberg2020priming] (Materials and Methods: blocks of constant target color, then a swap); [@brooks2012counterbalancing] — carryover from one condition to the next (abstract). Flagging possible avoidance is the PI's; as an analogy only, monkeys chose an offered, rewarded sure option more when uncertain [@kiani2009representation] (abstract), which does not show animals erring or breaking fixation to avoid a condition. The cap and the stated limitation are the PI's.

### TSK-32 — Adaptive methods: transformed up-down staircases (fixed or shrinking steps), interleaved staircases, Bayesian methods (QUEST, QUEST+, Psi), training progressions that advance and fall back on criteria; a reviewed library plus task code; state may carry per animal; a procedure may read outcomes, RTs, gaze traces and neural features; a live value on its parameter holds it
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (the brainstorm notes, §12 Batches 1-2). The inventory found the methods cited by name only.
- **Where:** [engine spec §15](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#15-adaptivity-n12) items 1-6; the brainstorm notes, [§12, Batches 1-2](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#12-adaptivity).
- **Basis:** PI decision; the named methods' primary sources. [@levitt1971transformed] — each transformed up-down rule converges where the probability of a "down" sequence is 0.5, so two-down/one-up converges on 70.7% (Sec. II.B, the derivation and Fig. 5, p. 472; Table I entry 2, p. 471); shrinking steps halve the step after the first, third, seventh, fifteenth, … runs (p. 471); "the presentations for two or more strategies can be interleaved at random", so the subject cannot anticipate (pp. 471-472). [@kaernbach1991simple] — weighted up-down with unequal steps can target any point on the psychometric function (abstract). [@garciaperez1998forcedchoice] — with fixed steps, where a staircase converges depends on the ratio of its steps (abstract; TSK-34's caveat). [@watson1983quest] — QUEST places each trial at the current most probable Bayesian estimate of threshold (abstract). [@watson2017quest] — QUEST+ (abstract). [@kontsevich1999bayesian] — Psi, the Bayesian estimate of threshold and slope by expected information gain (abstract). Reviews of adaptive procedures: [@treutwein1995adaptive] (abstract); [@leek2001adaptive] (abstract). Automated macaque training progressions: [@berger2018standardized] (abstract). The library-plus-code arrangement, per-animal state and the live hold are the PI's.

### TSK-33 — A staircase's default rule: correct is success; wrong target and no response are failures; trials ending before the decision are ignored
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (the brainstorm notes, §12 Batch 1; inventory disagreement 11).
- **Where:** [engine spec §15](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#15-adaptivity-n12) item 3; the brainstorm notes, [§12, Batch 1](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#12-adaptivity).
- **Basis:** PI decision, agreeing with the method's definition. [@levitt1971transformed] — the transformed up-down rule classifies every trial as a positive or a negative response, and convergence is defined on those sequences (p. 472, Sec. II.B), so leaving out a trial with an answer breaks that definition. In a detection task a miss is an answer, the negative one: [@stanislaw1999calculation] (abstract); [@luo2015neuronal] (Experimental Procedures). Trials that end before the decision carry no response to the stimulus, so ignoring them is consistent with this. `adaptive_detection`'s own rule differs today (TSK-34).

### TSK-34 — `adaptive_detection`: a one-up two-down staircase on contrast "converging near 71% correct", step factor 0.8, floor 0.02, ceiling 1.0; only correct and wrong-target trials move it; contrast 0.02-1.0 as a bare fraction; eccentricity 2-16° in mini-blocks of held eccentricity
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, engineering default (the task, written for S1's bake-off); the transformed up-down literature was uncited. The task's own rule is not decided: the engine spec §25 leaves `adaptive_detection`'s staircase and catch trials to the PI when the reference tasks are made right (inventory disagreements 11 and 12).
- **Where:** [`tasks/adaptive_detection.py`](../../tasks/adaptive_detection.py) (`Staircase`, `next_params`, the `Param`s); [S1 §5](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S1-task-model-design.md#5-the-bake-off-two-tasks); [engine spec §25](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#25-not-decided-here-and-why).
- **Basis:** Engineering default. Today `next_params` moves the staircase only on `CORRECT` and `WRONG_TARGET`; in this task, which shows no distractor, a `WRONG_TARGET` is a look that left the target before its hold ended (inventory disagreement 10), and a `NO_RESPONSE` leaves the staircase where it was, where the engine's default counts a miss as a failure (TSK-33). Nothing calls `next_params` yet (XC-242), so `contrast` never moves and no stimulus reads it. [@levitt1971transformed] — one-up two-down converges on 70.7% (Table I entry 2, p. 471), derived in Sec. II.B from [P(X)]² = 0.5 with no condition on step size (p. 472). [@peli1990contrast] — Michelson contrast, (Lmax − Lmin)/(Lmax + Lmin), is used for periodic patterns and Weber contrast, ΔL/L, for a target on a uniform background (p. 2032, Eqs. 1-2); the two "do not coincide or even share a common range of values", Michelson running from 0 to 1 and Weber from −1 to +∞ (p. 2032, Eq. 3); RMS contrast is defined over an image (p. 2033, Eq. 4). The `contrast` parameter is a bare fraction with no convention, where the engine spec §4.12 writes every contrast with one (COL-24; inventory disagreement 12). The floor, ceiling, step factor and eccentricity range are engineering defaults; the mini-blocks of held eccentricity the docstring describes are performed by nothing yet (inventory disagreement 18).
- **Caveat:** Levitt derives one-up two-down's 70.7% with no condition on step size [@levitt1971transformed, p. 472]; that where a staircase converges depends on its steps is García-Pérez's finding: with equal up and down steps (Δ−/Δ+ = 1), forced-choice transformed staircases "fail to reach their presumed targets", and a two-down one-up rule with Δ−/Δ+ = 0.5488 converges on 80.35% [@garciaperez1998forcedchoice]. This task is not forced choice, and its steps are equal in log contrast (×0.8 down, ÷0.8 up). In detection a miss is the negative answer [@stanislaw1999calculation; @luo2015neuronal], and the transformed up-down rule classifies trials as positive or negative responses [@levitt1971transformed, p. 472]. With no target-absent trials a detection task measures a hit rate, and "any improvements in hit rate could depend on a change in only criterion, only sensitivity, or both" [@luo2015neuronal, Introduction]; "Criterion effects are minimized by the use of an objective task: multiple-alternative forced-choice detection or identification" [@pelli2013measuring, abstract].

### TSK-35 — Reference-task timing and windows: `fix_timeout` 0.5-10 s (start 4.0), `fix_hold` 0.05-2 s (0.3), `response_window` 0.1-3 s (0.6), `target_hold` 0.05-1 s (0.2), `fix_window` 0.5-5° (2.0), `target_window` 0.5-6° (3.0)
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, engineering defaults (the ranges, in the reference tasks as written); 2026-09-30, the starting values, `fixation_detection`'s, which are the values of the console mockup the PI reviewed ([P4d-2b spec §6.2](../superpowers/specs/2026-09-26-P4d2b-browser-console-design.md#62-opening-running-and-ending-a-session-from-the-page) and [§6.7](../superpowers/specs/2026-09-26-P4d2b-browser-console-design.md#67-decided-by-the-b3a-2-plan-2026-09-30-for-the-pis-review-with-64), the b3a-2 plan's decision 1); the demo-mode spec §6 gives the other reference tasks the same.
- **Where:** [`tasks/fixation_detection.py`](../../tasks/fixation_detection.py); [`tasks/adaptive_detection.py`](../../tasks/adaptive_detection.py) (`next_params`); [`tasks/visual_search.py`](../../tasks/visual_search.py); [demo-mode spec §6](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-06-demo-mode-design.md#6-starting-values-closes-xc-183).
- **Basis:** Engineering defaults, within common practice in macaque saccade tasks, the PI's own included. [@westerberg2020priming] — fixation within 1° to start a trial, a saccade within 1000 ms, and fixation held within a 2-5 dva window around the target for 500 ms (Materials and Methods, "Experimental design and behavior"); [@westerberg2023feedforward] — fixation within 0.5 dva, with the same response and hold rules (Methods, "Task design: Pop-out search"); [@luo2015neuronal] — fixation within a 1.5° window for 400-600 ms, and a response window of 100-500 ms (Experimental Procedures, "Dissociation Task"); [@opstal1989scatter] — saccade endpoint scatter grows roughly linearly with target eccentricity (abstract; human), which is why target windows are sized to eccentricity. Every range contains these values.
- **Caveat:** `fix_window` starts at 2.0°; published recording windows are 0.5-1.5° [@westerberg2020priming; @westerberg2023feedforward; @luo2015neuronal], inside the range (0.5-5°). A recording session sets it lower.

### TSK-36 — The fixation point is a 0.3° disc at (0, 0); targets are 1.0° discs or squares; the target's appearance is a value swapped between trials, not a new task
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, engineering default (the reference tasks as written; `calibration` since 2026-09-05).
- **Where:** [`tasks/fixation_detection.py`](../../tasks/fixation_detection.py), [`adaptive_detection.py`](../../tasks/adaptive_detection.py), [`visual_search.py`](../../tasks/visual_search.py), [`calibration.py`](../../tasks/calibration.py).
- **Basis:** Engineering default. [@westerberg2023feedforward] — "a small, white fixation dot (diameter = 0.3 dva)" (Methods, "Task design: Pop-out search"), the size used here. The PI's published arrays scaled their items with eccentricity, 0.3 dva per dva (about 3° at 10°), so that they stayed smaller than the estimated receptive field [@westerberg2020priming] (Materials and Methods); [@westerberg2023feedforward] (Methods), which takes V4 receptive fields as 0.84 dva per dva, about 8.4° at 10°. The fixed 1.0° target has no published source.

### TSK-37 — `fixation_detection`, the first animal task: fixate, hold, a target appears, saccade to it, hold, reward; the target on the horizontal meridian at `target_position` ±16° (start 10°), in direct view
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, engineering default (the task, S1 §5's Task A, for roadmap M6); 2026-09-28, the PI (direct view). Its docstring and S1 §5 say "one of six positions", where the task has one horizontal `target_position` (inventory disagreement 18).
- **Where:** [`tasks/fixation_detection.py`](../../tasks/fixation_detection.py); [S1 §5](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S1-task-model-design.md#5-the-bake-off-two-tasks); [roadmap M6](../roadmap.md#m6--first-real-task-first-animal); [direct-view spec §8](../superpowers/specs/2026-09-28-direct-view-design.md#8-what-changes-and-in-what-order).
- **Basis:** Engineering default and PI decision; the visually guided saccade task is the standard first oculomotor task. [@bruce1985primate] — visually guided saccades in trained macaques (abstract); [@fuchs1967saccadic] — the training technique (abstract, item 1). The single horizontal position against the docstring's six is a matter of the documents, not of the literature.

### TSK-38 — Stimuli go out to about 15° in direct view (the calibration region); the detection tasks reach ±16°, past it, with extrapolation reported
- **Decided:** 2026-09-27, the PI ("out to about 15°"); 2026-09-28, engineering (the direct-view spec puts the detection tasks back at ±16°); S5 §2 reports extrapolation beyond the constellation since its amendment of 2026-09-05.
- **Where:** [direct-view spec §6](../superpowers/specs/2026-09-28-direct-view-design.md#6-calibration-per-setup) and [§8](../superpowers/specs/2026-09-28-direct-view-design.md#8-what-changes-and-in-what-order); [S5 §1](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S5-eye-tracking-design.md#1-most-of-the-calibration-is-already-decided-and-not-by-us) and [§2](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S5-eye-tracking-design.md#2-a-ring-of-calibration-targets-cannot-fit-a-second-order-map); [`tasks/fixation_detection.py`](../../tasks/fixation_detection.py) and [`tasks/adaptive_detection.py`](../../tasks/adaptive_detection.py) (`target_position`).
- **Basis:** PI decision and engineering: the 15° is the PI's; the ±16° and reporting gaze measured outside the calibrated region as extrapolated are engineering, with no published basis. (Nyström et al. 2013, entered for it at first, reports results only for calibration method and eye physiology, nothing on gaze outside the calibrated region, and was removed on 2026-10-08.)

### TSK-39 — `visual_search`: pop-out, the target differing from distractors in exactly one feature (color or shape), the feature a value; set size 2-12 on a ring of 5-14°; item windows 0.5-1.0° so twelve items on a 5° ring cannot overlap (2.59° apart); `array_phase` 0-360° "randomised between trials so the animal cannot learn positions"; a distractor saccade is a wrong target, leaving the target during the hold a target break
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, engineering default (the task as written; its item windows bounded on 2026-09-01, after the crowding check, pitfalls P20). The inventory found the published basis uncited. The engine spec §25 leaves `item_window` to the PI when the reference tasks are made right.
- **Where:** [`tasks/visual_search.py`](../../tasks/visual_search.py); [S1a §12](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S1a-vocabulary-design.md#12-added-2026-08-31-after-review--what-the-graph-could-not-see), "Set size as a value"; [pitfalls P20](../pitfalls.md#expanded-notes); [engine spec §25](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#25-not-decided-here-and-why).
- **Basis:** Engineering default, on published pop-out findings. [@treisman1980featureintegration] — separable features "should be detectable by parallel search" (p. 101), and the display "was apparently searched spatially in parallel whenever targets could be detected on the basis of a single feature, either color or shape" (p. 107, Experiment I Discussion); [@wolfe2017five] — target salience rises with the target-distractor difference and with distractor homogeneity ("Bottom-up guidance by stimulus salience"); [@bravo1992role] — for an odd-colored target, reaction times stayed constant as the number of distractors grew (abstract; human), which supports small and large set sizes alike for that judgment (its exception is in the Caveat); [@nothdurft1993role] — color targets were "always detected fast", independent of local feature contrast (abstract; human). In macaques: pop-out search with saccades to the oddball, [@thompson1996perceptual] (abstract, which does not name the feature), and color pop-out, [@westerberg2023feedforward] (Methods); isoluminant red and green items on a ring, the target at each position with equal probability, and a saccade to a distractor unrewarded and followed by a time-out [@westerberg2020priming] (Materials and Methods), which supports scoring it as a wrong target. The window bound, the hold rule and rotating the array (`array_phase`) are engineering. Nothing varies `array_phase` between trials yet (inventory disagreement 18).
- **Caveat:** Color is an "undoubted" guiding attribute and shape only "probable" [@wolfe2017five]; color targets were detected fast regardless of local feature contrast, orientation targets not [@nothdurft1993role]; Treisman and Gelade's parallel shape search used letters [@treisman1980featureintegration, p. 107]. Bravo and Nakayama's flat reaction times were for detecting the odd color; when the target's shape was judged and the colors reversed unpredictably, reaction time fell with set size [@bravo1992role] (abstract). The PI's published macaque pop-out work held gaze within a 2-5 dva window around the target [@westerberg2020priming; @westerberg2023feedforward], and saccade endpoint scatter grows roughly linearly with target eccentricity [@opstal1989scatter] (human); this task's item windows are 0.5-1.0° in radius at 5-14°, bounded so that twelve items on a 5° ring cannot overlap. Changing the target and distractor colors between trials produces priming of pop-out, and repeating the target's position produces location-based inhibition of return [@bichot2002priming]; the PI's published design held the colors for blocks of 5-50 trials, median 12 [@westerberg2020priming]. Here the feature is a value that may change between any two trials. The PI's published designs did not rotate the array between trials: in 2020 "The array was then oriented so that its eccentricity coincided with the location of the receptive field… a single array item was placed at the center of the receptive field" [@westerberg2020priming] (Materials and Methods, "Experimental design and behavior"), and in 2023 "The polar angle positioning of the items… varied from session to session so that one item… at the center of the population receptive field" [@westerberg2023feedforward] (Methods, "Task design: Pop-out search"). Rotating the array every trial, as `array_phase`'s comment intends, would remove the item centered on the receptive field. The 2020 colors were "rendered isoluminant" [@westerberg2020priming] (Materials and Methods); 2023 calls its red and green isoluminant "to a human observer" and qualifies them as distinct features "at the intersection of color and luminance information" [@westerberg2023feedforward] (Methods, "Task design: Pop-out search").

### TSK-40 — The simulated animal follows declared functions (psychometric functions and RT distributions) with named profiles: perfect, chance, incorrect strategy, normal (85% correct), trying to break the task, trying to exploit the reward schedule; it sees the screen; blinks and tracker loss are rates; simulation is advised, not enforced
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (the brainstorm notes, §15 Batch 1); that it sees the screen and that blinks and tracker loss are rates date from S1a §12 and §13 (2026-08-31 and 2026-09-01).
- **Where:** [engine spec §18](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#18-recording-and-review-n15) items 2-3; [S1a §12](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S1a-vocabulary-design.md#12-added-2026-08-31-after-review--what-the-graph-could-not-see) ("The simulated animal sees the screen") and [§13](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S1a-vocabulary-design.md#13-outcomes-extended-2026-09-01--signal-detection-and-the-rig); [pitfalls P18](../pitfalls.md#expanded-notes); [M0 review §4](../M0-REVIEW.md#4-engineering-calls-i-have-made--overrule-any-of-these); the brainstorm notes, [§15, Batch 1](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#15-recording-and-review).
- **Basis:** PI decision; the declared functions' form is published. [@wichmann2001psychometric] — observers make stimulus-independent errors (lapses), and failing to model them biases psychometric-function estimates (abstract), which supports a simulated animal whose psychometric function includes lapses; [@carpenter1995neural] — in "one such model" of saccadic latency, a decision signal rises linearly from an initial value to a fixed threshold, and experiments manipulating the target's prior probability "confirm this notion", the latency distribution changing as expected if the initial value reflects the prior log likelihood (abstract; human). The profiles and "advised, not enforced" are the PI's.

### TSK-41 — A session declares what it is for (training, piloting, recording), fixed for the session; each imperfection on the warnings list states the kinds it is acceptable in, accepted once at open; a mid-run warning unacceptable for the kind pauses at the next trial; an unknown pre-flight item proceeds on a recorded acknowledgment
- **Decided:** 2026-09-19, the PI (an unknown pre-flight item proceeds on a recorded acknowledgment); 2026-10-07, the PI (session kinds and the warnings list, the brainstorm notes, Batch R6); 2026-10-08, the PI (the kind fixed for the session, "The morning after").
- **Where:** [engine spec §19](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#19-warnings-and-what-a-session-is-for-n4-nr6); [S9a §10](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S9a-console-design.md#10-preflight-semantics); [demo-mode spec §14](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-06-demo-mode-design.md#14-parked-2026-10-07-what-the-reviews-found-and-what-comes-first); the brainstorm notes, [§4, Batch 3](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#4-color-and-luminance), [Batch R6](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#the-design-reviews-questions-asked-2026-10-07) and ["The morning after"](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#the-morning-after-asked-2026-10-08).
- **Basis:** PI decision; operational, with no published basis (searched 2026-10-08 only for analogues in MonkeyLogic's documentation).

### TSK-42 — A continuous (trial-less) mode for long presentations: probes on a schedule, gaze- or neural-contingent changes, marks and pauses; ends on a duration, its media's end, a stop or a criterion; analyzed as one epoch with timed events
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (the brainstorm notes, §3 Batch 3 and §10 Batch 2).
- **Where:** [engine spec §13](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#13-structure-n10-nr1-nr6) item 6; the brainstorm notes, [§3, Batch 3](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#3-when) and [§10, Batch 2](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#10-structure).
- **Basis:** PI decision. Precedent for analyzing a long natural-viewing presentation as one epoch against timed features: [@russ2015functional] — macaques viewed extended video clips, and responses were compared with time courses of features extracted from the movies (abstract).

### TSK-43 — Per-animal session programs, presets, last values and procedure state live on lab storage (one writer); at session open the operator chooses last values, the task's defaults or a preset; edits have a history with revert
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (the brainstorm notes, §10 Batch 4, §14 Batch 1 and Batch R6).
- **Where:** [engine spec §13](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#13-structure-n10-nr1-nr6) item 7 and [§17](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#17-parameters-and-live-control-n14-nr7) items 3-5; the brainstorm notes, [§10, Batch 4](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#10-structure), [§14, Batch 1](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#14-parameters-and-live-control) and [Batch R6](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#the-design-reviews-questions-asked-2026-10-07).
- **Basis:** PI decision. Only per-animal procedure state has a precedent: [@berger2018standardized] trained each animal on a self-paced schedule, with task conditions updated automatically from its own progress (abstract). Storage, presets and revert are engineering, with no published source.

### TSK-44 — Manual RF mapping by mouse uses a live schematic whose markers lag by the neuron's latency plus the operator's reaction, so receptive fields are reconstructed from the per-frame record, not the markers
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (the brainstorm notes, §2 Batch 3).
- **Where:** [engine spec §5](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#5-where-n2-nr5) item 10; the brainstorm notes, [§2, Batch 3](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#2-where).
- **Basis:** PI decision, on published findings. Manual mapping with spots and patterns of light is the classic method: [@hubel1968receptive] (abstract). Neuronal latency is substantial and differs by area: [@schmolesky1998signal] — onset latencies are earliest in V1, with V2 and V4 later and more broadly distributed (abstract); [@thompson1996perceptual] — FEF visual latency had a mean of 67 ms and a range of 35-138 ms (abstract, item 3). Reconstructing the RF from the recorded stimulus sequence is reverse correlation: [@ringach1997subspace] and [@chichilnisky2001simple] (abstracts). The operator's reaction time was not sourced.

### TSK-45 — Burn-in mitigation may not touch the stimulus: fixation-point jitter is rejected because microsaccade analyses, fixation-stability measures and RF mapping assume a fixed fixation point
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, a ruling (S0 §5.4, "ruled 2026-08-31"). The inventory found the published basis uncited.
- **Where:** [S0 §5.4](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S0-hosts-and-hardware-design.md#54-panel-acceptance-test--written-now-before-the-panel-exists); [M0 review §3.2](../M0-REVIEW.md#32-rig-and-animals-5) item 10; [display-state note §1.6](../research/2026-10-07-engine-display-state.md#16-color-gamma-lut-s4-9-wl_xconphotometrypy-s1a-line-276-pitfalls-p19).
- **Basis:** Ruling, on published findings. [@gur1997visual] — V1 receptive fields move with the eye during fixation, with a tight correlation and a near-unity ratio between eye position and RF position (abstract): anything that moves the eye moves the mapped RF. [@hafed2009neural] — in macaques, the inactivation experiments show the rostral superior colliculus's causal role in generating microsaccades (main text); that a microsaccade is triggered when the center of mass of rostral activity, which represents the fixated target as the foveal goal, deviates from it is the paper's computational model, which "provides a plausible mechanism" (main text, the model and its closing paragraphs). [@engbert2003microsaccades] — microsaccade rate and direction are modulated by visual cues (abstract; human). A moving fixation point is therefore not neutral for microsaccade analyses.

### TSK-46 — Online RT is approximate by decision (`rt_approx_ms`), the real RT recovered offline from sync ticks; touch uses a wired sensor, not a tablet, so RT stays recoverable
- **Decided:** 2026-09-19, the PI.
- **Where:** [S9a §9](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S9a-console-design.md#9-the-telemetry-contract); [S13 §3](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S13-kiosk-design.md#3-what-is-present).
- **Basis:** PI decision; the wired sensor rests on published timing. [@pronk2020mental] — touchscreen and keyboard devices running web applications overestimated RTs, the per-device means running 57.6-132.9 ms (Table 4) with single trials as low as 44.9 ms, the overestimation differing between devices and browsers and bimodal on some (Experiment 2 Results; Discussion), which supports a wired sensor whose timing the rig records. That an online RT may be approximate is the PI's.

### TSK-47 — The joystick is calibrated per rig and per animal (range, center, dead zone, hold and release); the chair-motion trigger gates task progression
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, engineering default (S6 §5; the controller architecture §5.4 and §9.4).
- **Where:** [S6 §1](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S6-io-subsystem-design.md#1-the-lines) and [§5](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S6-io-subsystem-design.md#5-analog-input); [controller architecture §3.2](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-controller-architecture-design.md#32-the-task-pcs-interface-from-the-breakout-spec-3-92), [§5.4](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-controller-architecture-design.md#54-state-primitives-that-are-not-obvious) and [§9.4](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-controller-architecture-design.md#94-other-behavioral-inputs); [S1 §2.2](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S1-task-model-design.md#22-guards).
- **Basis:** Engineering default. Precedent for calibrating the joystick in the task software: [@asaad2008flexible] — eye and joystick signals are calibrated alike, by presenting dots in sequence and marking "joystick acquisition" (Results, "Usability: Signal calibration"). The chair-motion trigger has no published source (searched 2026-10-08: PubMed for macaque chair-motion or body-movement gating of task progression).

### TSK-48 — Demo mode cannot judge time windows or window sizes: a hand takes a second or more from onset to landing where a monkey's eye takes about 0.25 s
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the demo-mode spec §14 item 17 (from the demo-mode science review's finding 10), the latency figures given without a source. Its "roughly 0.6-1 s" was corrected on 2026-10-08 to "a second or more", from the papers below.
- **Where:** [demo-mode spec §14](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-06-demo-mode-design.md#14-parked-2026-10-07-what-the-reviews-found-and-what-comes-first) item 17.
- **Basis:** Published, for both figures. The monkey: [@fischer1983saccadic] — with no gap between fixation-point offset and target onset, macaque saccadic reaction times were about 200 ms (abstract); [@fuchs1967saccadic] — monkeys make their saccades faster than humans (abstract, item 3); onset to landing in about 0.25 s is consistent with these. The hand: [@card1977evaluation] — a mean mouse positioning time of 1.29 s (SD 0.42), from cursor motion to the button press, after a 0.36 s homing time, in a text-selection task (Table 2, report p. 10, PDF p. 12; the 1977 PARC report, the 1978 Ergonomics version not opened). The conclusion, that demo mode cannot judge time windows, stands either way.

### TSK-49 — Images are made between trials by reviewed, seeded procedures (scrambling, filtering, cropping, recoloring) or by a GPU server (generated images), and kept in the record; text in bundled fonts with glyph height in degrees; curve-tracing curves fixed or seeded
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (the brainstorm notes, §1 Batches 3-4).
- **Where:** [engine spec §4.5](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#45-text-and-curves), [§4.7](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#47-image-procedures-between-trials) and [§4.8](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#48-generated-images); the brainstorm notes, [§1, Batches 3-4](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#1-what-can-be-shown).
- **Basis:** PI decision, with published precedents. [@rainer2001nonmonotonic] — images degraded by interpolating between natural images and matched noise, the macaque BOLD response depending non-monotonically on the mixing level (abstract): how an image is degraded matters to the result, which is why the procedure is reviewed and recorded. [@portilla2000parametric] — synthesizing random images subject to a texture's measured statistics (abstract). [@ponce2019evolving] — closed-loop generated images, evolved by a genetic algorithm through a generative network to maximize a neuron's firing (abstract). [@roelfsema1998objectbased] — curve tracing (abstract). Text rendering has no published source.

## Recording and events

### REC-01 — Anything scientifically meaningful becomes an edge or word in a recorded stream; hardware timestamps are for analysis, software ones for control; our log carries meaning, not the timing record
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, engineering default (S3 §1), carried into architecture.md and the controller architecture.
- **Where:** [architecture.md, "Principles"](../design/architecture.md#principles) item 3; [S3 §1](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S3-sync-integration-design.md#1-what-wl-sync-actually-owns) and [§4](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S3-sync-integration-design.md#4-our-obligations-to-the-sync-fabric) item 3; [controller architecture §3.1](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-controller-architecture-design.md#31-topology).
- **Basis:** Engineering default resting on standard practice. [@plant2004selfvalidating] — an empirical study of many paradigms found numerous sources of timing error (scripting, specification, hardware) and stresses independent hardware validation of timing (abstract). [@hwang2019nimh] — behavioral and neural data are often collected by two separate systems and synchronized by digital event markers exchanged between them, with marker latency measured by loopback (Results, "Time measure and latencies of stimuli", ¶3; Table 1). [@karsh2026sync] — software documentation: streams with separate sample clocks are aligned offline by a common sync square wave recorded in every stream, mapping event times with typical errors of about 0.01 ms after calibration (Overview). [@siegle2021survey] — the Allen Neuropixels survey synchronized every device to one master clock by recorded digital barcodes and a photodiode, not by software clocks (Methods, "Data acquisition and synchronization"). [@rubel2022neurodata] — NWB stores neural, behavioral and stimulation data with all time bases aligned to a common clock (Results, "NWB enables unified description and storage of multimodal data and derived products", ¶2).

### REC-02 — Event codes carry identity and timing; the session record carries content (parameter values, stimulation settings), encoded into the stream only where the recording must stay interpretable alone
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, engineering default (S2 §4), generalizing wl-preproc's reason for putting the task type inside `BLOCK_START`.
- **Where:** [S2 §4](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S2-event-vocabulary-design.md#4-the-rule-for-what-goes-in-the-stream).
- **Basis:** Engineering default. The closest published precedent: [@asaad2008flexible] — numeric markers in a data file referenced to tables stored in separate files or notebooks can be lost or mis-associated, leaving the events undecipherable, and MonkeyLogic's remedy was to carry the event-marker descriptions, the conditions table and the task configuration in the data file so any trial can be reconstructed (Results, Usability, "Data file record", ¶1-2), the concern S2 §4 answers by keeping identity in the stream and content in a record that travels with the session directory; [@rubel2022neurodata] — an NWB file holds all the measurements of one experiment with the metadata needed to understand them (Results, "NWB enables unified description…", ¶1). The particular split (identity and timing in 16-bit codes, content in the record) is the lab's engineering choice; no paper prescribes it.

### REC-03 — Event codes go out at the decision, not at the flip; the light sensor gives the exact onset time
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (the brainstorm notes, §5 Batch 1).
- **Where:** [engine spec §8](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#8-sync-and-evidence-n5-nr3-nr5) item 2; the brainstorm notes, [§5, Batch 1](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#5-sync-and-evidence).
- **Basis:** PI decision, supported as follows. [@hwang2019nimh] — every LCD tested had display lag, one 24.78 ms even when the frame buffers were submitted early, so experiments sensitive to the timing of visual stimuli must account for these delays, measured with a photodiode (Results, "Time measure and latencies of stimuli", ¶6-7; Table 2). [@bridges2020timing] — across packages, a constant lag of about 4 ms between the trigger pulse and the pixels changing, largely hardware-based and dependent on the stimulus's position on the screen (Results, "Lab-based package results", ¶2), and package comparisons are no replacement for testing the timing on the system in use (Introduction). [@elze2010achieving] — stimulus durations on LCDs vary across models and even across luminance levels on one monitor, so brief presentations need extensive display measurements before the experiment (abstract). [@ibl2021standardized] — to time changes in the visual stimulus, a patch of pixels flipped between white and black at every stimulus change and a photodiode captured the flip (Materials and methods, "Materials and apparatus"), the published form of the engine spec's task patch (§8 item 1). All four measured CRTs or LCDs; that the same reasoning holds for the QD-OLED is our inference (the display decisions cover OLED timing).

### REC-04 — `STIMULUS_ON`, `STIMULUS_OFF`, `STIMULUS_CHANGED` are task codes allocated once in wl-xtasks' range, one per group; which stimulus is in the record by frame; tasks' own onset codes stay
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (Batch R5, replacing §5 Batch 3's framework escapes).
- **Where:** [engine spec §8](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#8-sync-and-evidence-n5-nr3-nr5) item 3; the brainstorm notes, [Batch R5](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#the-design-reviews-questions-asked-2026-10-07); [S2 §5.1](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S2-event-vocabulary-design.md#51-in-wl-exptasks-ranges-ours-to-allocate-no-amendment-required).
- **Basis:** PI decision on code allocation within the lab's frozen codec (wl-preproc `contracts/events.py`). No scientific content; no published basis exists or was expected (searched 2026-10-08 for event-code allocation conventions: nothing).

### REC-05 — Frames are matched to the recording by counting flip-patch edges from each trial's start code, checked against the trial's length; the continuous mode strobes an anchor code about once a second
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (the brainstorm notes, §5 Batch 1).
- **Where:** [engine spec §8](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#8-sync-and-evidence-n5-nr3-nr5) item 4; the brainstorm notes, [§5, Batch 1](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#5-sync-and-evidence).
- **Basis:** PI decision; the practice it refines is published. [@siegle2021survey] — a photodiode over a sync square that flips every 60 frames, thresholded and recorded on the master clock, with individual frame times reconstructed by interpolating between the photodiode's on and off events (Methods, "Data acquisition and synchronization"); a flip patch that alternates every refresh, as here, removes that interpolation (our inference). [@karsh2026sync] — software documentation: a common sync square wave of period 1, 2 or 3 s is recorded in every stream, so any event is "no more than one period away from a nearest (preceding) sync wave edge", and the error on a mapped time is bounded by "sync_period * rate_error/rate" (0.1 ms in its example of a 3 Hz rate error at 30 kHz with a 1 s period); "Shorter periods tighten bounds on final alignment error" (Overview). The once-a-second anchor that confines a miscount to one interval applies the first of these to edge counting, where the page applies it to mapping times between clocks (our analogy, not the source's claim). Counting edges from a trial-start code is this design's own; no paper describing it was found (searched 2026-10-08: PubMed and the web for photodiode sync-square practice, the IBL frame2ttl, the Allen SDK and SpikeGLX).

### REC-06 — The screen log keeps every change with its frame and fully resolved description, every live-driven value per frame, the seeds, and the display's per-refresh report, so the exact drawer rebuilds any frame
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (the brainstorm notes, §5 Batch 1).
- **Where:** [engine spec §8](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#8-sync-and-evidence-n5-nr3-nr5) item 5 and [§4.11](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#411-live-driven-stimuli); the brainstorm notes, [§5, Batch 1](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#5-sync-and-evidence).
- **Basis:** PI decision, with published precedent. [@asaad2008flexible] — MonkeyLogic's data file holds the stimulus images used, the text descriptions of the event markers, the conditions-table structure and task configuration, a record that allows any trial to be reconstructed and replayed from the one file (Results, Usability, "Data file record", ¶2). MonkeyLogic stores the images themselves; storing resolved descriptions and seeds for an exact drawer is this design's alternative.

### REC-07 — A frame-clock fault is a warning and a warnings-list entry, affected trials marked, the session going on; frame timing per trial is shown in the console
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (the brainstorm notes, §5 Batch 2).
- **Where:** [engine spec §8](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#8-sync-and-evidence-n5-nr3-nr5) items 6-7; the brainstorm notes, [§5, Batch 2](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#5-sync-and-evidence).
- **Basis:** PI decision. [@nwb2026nwb] — data-standard documentation: NWB defines `/intervals/invalid_times`, "Time intervals that should be removed from analysis" (Format Specification 2.11.0, §2.6.2.46), a way to mark bad intervals within a session; the specification takes no position on discarding a whole session.
- **Caveat:** The Allen survey failed a whole session past 60 delayed frames [@siegle2021survey]; this design marks affected trials and never fails a session (PI, 2026-10-07), which NWB's invalid intervals can represent [@nwb2026nwb]. No session-level threshold exists.

### REC-08 — Target positions in the stream are degrees, offset-binary, in hundredths of a degree, refused rather than clamped out of range
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, an external contract: wl-preproc's `contracts/events.py` (read that day at `f7fb10a`).
- **Where:** [S2 §3](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S2-event-vocabulary-design.md#3-what-already-exists-do-not-redesign); [S4 §1](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S4-stimulus-presentation-design.md#1-what-already-exists).
- **Basis:** External contract, with no scientific content beyond its resolution. At the 50 cm viewing distance and the panel's 0.15364 mm horizontal pitch (optics drawing §1), one pixel subtends atan(0.15364/500) ≈ 0.0176°, so 0.01° steps are finer than a pixel (arithmetic, not a source).

### REC-09 — Every trial records a complete resolved parameter snapshot; every live change is event-coded (a `PARAM_CHANGE` escape is requested; the interim `PARAM_CHANGED` code carries no payload)
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, engineering default (S2 §5.2, S8 §3.3, pitfalls P16).
- **Where:** [S2 §5.2](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S2-event-vocabulary-design.md#52-in-wl-preprocs-ranges-amendment-required); [S8 §3.3](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S8-session-management-design.md#33-provenance); [pitfalls P16](../pitfalls.md#expanded-notes); [`tasks/allocation.py`](../../tasks/allocation.py) (4130).
- **Basis:** Engineering default. [@asaad2008flexible] — the case for carrying the fullest account of the behavioral record thought possible and practical, the conditions table and configuration included, so that there is no ambiguity about what events took place (Results, Usability, "Data file record", ¶2). That a live change must also land on the recording clock (P16) has no published source found.

### REC-10 — An outcome's reason is strobed as a task event immediately before its marker (the outcomes share five markers)
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, engineering default (S1a §7; `tasks/allocation.py`).
- **Where:** [S1a §7](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S1a-vocabulary-design.md#7-outcomes); [`tasks/allocation.py`](../../tasks/allocation.py).
- **Basis:** Engineering default; recording a per-trial outcome with its reason is common practice. [@nimhmonkeylogic2026runtime] — software documentation: MonkeyLogic marks every trial with one of ten error codes, from 0 (correct) through no response, late response, break fixation, no fixation, early response, incorrect, lever break and ignored to 9 (aborted) (Runtime functions, `trialerror`). [@asaad2008flexible] — in the authors' delayed-match-to-sample example, 28 of 43 script lines are spent saving an error code and aborting the trial when the subject fails a step (Results, Usability, "Task Scripting"). The reason-before-marker strobe order is this design's own.

### REC-11 — Restraint is recorded as `HEAD_FIXED`/`HEAD_RELEASED`, emitted only for rig-fixed sessions; after a resume, restraint counts from the latest `HEAD_FIXED` (an undercount)
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, engineering (the two codes, S2); 2026-09-20, the PI (emitted only for `RIG_FIXED`; the out-of-cage marks get no codes).
- **Where:** [S2 §5.1](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S2-event-vocabulary-design.md#51-in-wl-exptasks-ranges-ours-to-allocate-no-amendment-required); [S8 §5.2](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S8-session-management-design.md#52-a-restart-must-not-reset-the-day) item 4; [architecture.md, "Message contracts"](../design/architecture.md#message-contracts-draft-v0); [`tasks/allocation.py`](../../tasks/allocation.py).
- **Basis:** A recording convention and a PI decision; no published basis. The welfare limit itself is institutional (WEL-01).

### REC-12 — An operator's mark is stamped in the frame it reaches the rig, with the press, receipt and stamp instants all recorded; pause and resume are strobed so the recording shows the gap
- **Decided:** 2026-09-27, the PI.
- **Where:** [P4d-2b spec §5.0](../superpowers/specs/2026-09-26-P4d2b-browser-console-design.md#50-rulings-pi-2026-09-27-asked-in-plain-terms) and [§5.1](../superpowers/specs/2026-09-26-P4d2b-browser-console-design.md#51-what-the-rig-does); [architecture.md, "Message contracts"](../design/architecture.md#message-contracts-draft-v0).
- **Basis:** PI decision; no published basis (searched 2026-10-08 for experimenter annotations timestamped and synchronized with neural recordings: only video-scoring tools).

### REC-13 — Sounds play through two analog outputs of the task PC's PCIe-6343, armed at the decision and started by the task patch's next edge (a declarable offset allowed); onset is measured into the recording by a speaker tap (V7); speaker-to-ear delay is outside V7
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI (the brainstorm notes, §6 Batch 1, corrected by Batches R5 and R6). Architecture.md and the controller architecture still say audio is host-side (inventory disagreement 13).
- **Where:** [engine spec §9](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#9-media-and-sound-n6-nr5-nr6) item 1; the brainstorm notes, [§6, Batch 1](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#6-media-and-sound), [Batches R5 and R6](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#the-design-reviews-questions-asked-2026-10-07) and ["Where the pieces meet"](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#how-it-fits-put-to-the-pi-2026-10-07); [S4 §8](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S4-stimulus-presentation-design.md#8-audio); [controller architecture §8.5](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-controller-architecture-design.md#85-audio).
- **Basis:** PI decision, supported as follows. [@hwang2019nimh] — measured against an event marker, analog-output stimulation reached the DAQ output at a median −0.05 ms, while sound through the on-board codec took a median 39.92 ms and through a USB adapter 40.98 ms (Results, "Time measure and latencies of stimuli", ¶4-5; Table 1), the case for a DAQ output over a sound card. [@babjack2015reducing] — differing platforms and audio devices introduce variable time between the intended and the actual sound delivery, which matters for EEG/ERP and multimodal timing (abstract). [@bridges2020timing] — audio onset measured against a hardware trigger, packages that pre-schedule audio reaching under 1 ms lag (Results, "Lab-based package results", ¶2): a sound card can also be made precise, but in every case the onset was measured, which is what the speaker tap does. The card's four analog outputs are a vendor fact the documents carry (NI, read 2026-10-07); NI's specification page did not render for the backfill and is not entered.

### REC-14 — Simple sounds (tones, clicks, noise bursts, sweeps) are made from parameters and a seed; movie soundtracks optional and in step; every sound emits `AUDIO_ON`/`AUDIO_OFF`; feedback tones are ordinary stimuli
- **Decided:** 2026-10-07, the PI; engineering.
- **Where:** [engine spec §9](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#9-media-and-sound-n6-nr5-nr6) items 2-3; the brainstorm notes, [§6, Batches 1-2](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#6-media-and-sound); [S4 §8](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S4-stimulus-presentation-design.md#8-audio); [S2 §5.1](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S2-event-vocabulary-design.md#51-in-wl-exptasks-ranges-ours-to-allocate-no-amendment-required).
- **Basis:** PI decision; the reconstruction argument is REC-06's [@asaad2008flexible]. No further source was sought.

### REC-15 — The recording card's analog scan is set by vocalization monitoring, 40 kHz on the microphone
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, external: wl-sync's breakout spec §9.3, which states that vocalizations have energy to about 10 kHz and an adequate rate of about 25 kHz, paired with a 4th-order low-pass at about 12 kHz (§6.4; read in wl-sync 2026-10-08).
- **Where:** [S6 §5](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S6-io-subsystem-design.md#5-analog-input).
- **Basis:** External design. What the literature on rhesus calls says: [@schwartz2022vocal] — rhesus coos have a fundamental from 400 to 3000 Hz and screams are relatively high-fundamental (3-10 kHz) vocalizations (Introduction, the paragraph introducing coos and screams), and the coo audio was analyzed as 44.1 kHz recordings (Methods, "Coo Recording, Preparation and Selection"); [@rendall1998role] — rhesus noisy screams are continuous, broadband noise bursts that can include a high-frequency, periodic component (abstract). A 40 kHz scan (20 kHz Nyquist) keeps every reported fundamental and only the harmonics below 20 kHz (arithmetic), and wl-sync's anti-alias low-pass at about 12 kHz (its §6.4) limits the recorded band below that anyway.

### REC-16 — Behavior cameras: 2-4 (at most 8) mono cameras at 200 fps for the whole session, on the face, hands and body, for 3D pose; exposures synchronized by the primary; compressed visually lossless; everything kept
- **Decided:** 2026-09-27, the PI.
- **Where:** [P9 §1](../superpowers/specs/2026-09-27-P9-camera-system-design.md#1-what-the-pi-decided-2026-09-27-asked-in-plain-terms) and [§2](../superpowers/specs/2026-09-27-P9-camera-system-design.md#2-hardware-and-timing); [S0 §7.2](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S0-hosts-and-hardware-design.md#72-the-behavior-camera-system-p9).
- **Basis:** PI decision, supported as follows. [@mathis2018deeplabcut] — markerless pose estimation from video by transfer learning reaches human-level accuracy from about 200 labeled frames (abstract). [@karashchuk2021anipose] — for 3D, each joint must be visible from at least two cameras at all times, and for tracking several limbs or body parts the authors recommend at least three equally spaced cameras, with a dramatic reduction in error from two cameras to three (Discussion, "Limitations and practical recommendations"). [@bala2020automated] — in macaques, precise synchronization is a key enabler of 3D reconstruction, one TTL pulse opening and closing the shutters of all cameras at the same moment (Methods, "Multi-camera system design"), with the same camera family (FLIR Blackfly S). [@mathis2018inference] — preprint: DeepLabCut is highly robust to standard video compression, compression ratios above 1,000 costing about half a pixel at 640 × 480 (abstract). The 200 fps figure has no published source found (searched 2026-10-08: PubMed for macaque licking-rhythm frequency, no results; the pose-estimation papers cited use 30 fps and set no rate for the face or licking).
- **Caveat:** For several limbs Anipose recommends at least three cameras, with "a dramatic reduction in error from 2 to 3 cameras" [@karashchuk2021anipose]; two, the low end of "2 to 4 in practice", is below that for 3D pose of the hands and body. (The count is the PI's procurement.)

### REC-17 — Cameras take the barcode every frame and report exposure to the sync box; wl-xcon does not trigger cameras
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, engineering: wl-sync's design (since 2026-08-16), kept by P9 on 2026-09-27.
- **Where:** [S3 §8](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S3-sync-integration-design.md#8-photodiode-patches-cameras-and-reconstruction); [S6 §1](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S6-io-subsystem-design.md#1-the-lines); [P9 §2](../superpowers/specs/2026-09-27-P9-camera-system-design.md#2-hardware-and-timing).
- **Basis:** Engineering default resting on published practice. [@siegle2021survey] — synchronization signals for each frame were acquired by a dedicated computer treated as the master clock, and a 32-bit digital barcode was sent every 30 s to synchronize all devices with the neural data (Methods, "Data acquisition and synchronization"); [@bala2020automated] — hardware-triggered, simultaneous exposures across cameras (Methods, "Multi-camera system design"). That the sync box rather than the task controller owns this is the lab's architecture (S3), not a literature question.

### REC-18 — Closed-loop features of two types, selectable per experiment: an envelope (band-pass, rectify, boxcar) on the SpikeGLX path and threshold-crossing rate on the Intan path; band, threshold, window and channels are the PI's per experiment; type, parameters and a channel-map hash travel with every message; whether the two paths are matched is open
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, the PI (two types, per experiment); whether the paths are matched is open (S7 §7 item 3).
- **Where:** [S7 §5](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S7-neural-plane-design.md#5-features) and [§7](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S7-neural-plane-design.md#7-open-items) items 1 and 3; [M0 review §3.1](../M0-REVIEW.md#31-science-6) items 1-2; [controller architecture §16](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-controller-architecture-design.md#16-open-questions) item 8.
- **Basis:** PI decision; both feature types are established in macaque work.
