The envelope (multiunit activity): [@legatt1980averaged] — the derivation of digitally averaged MUA (recorded in macaque, per PubMed's indexing); with full-wave rectification volume-conducted activity does not significantly affect the averaged MUA waveform, and half-wave rectification should not be used (abstract), the source for S7's "full-wave rectify"; [@super2005chronic] — chronic MUA in behaving animals, compared with single neurons in primary visual cortex, is a reliable and useful technique where the neurons at the electrode share response properties (abstract); [@stark2007predicting] — MUA estimated without spike detection predicted movement better than single-unit spikes or LFP in monkey motor cortices (abstract). Threshold crossings: [@fraser2009control] — a single threshold across channels, without spike sorting, gave macaque BCI control comparable to decoding schemes based on sorted spikes (abstract); [@chestek2011longterm] — decoding used a threshold at −4.5 × RMS per electrode without sorting, citing prior reports of little or no gain from sorted spikes (Methods §2.2), and threshold-crossing decoding held its performance over years (abstract); [@christie2015comparison] — best thresholds between −3 and −4.5 × V_RMS, with thresholding instead of sorting changing mean classification accuracy by 5% on average (abstract), and its Introduction distinguishes band-passed RMS MUA from threshold crossings as different alternatives to sorting, published support for S7's statement that the two paths compute different things unless deliberately matched.

### REC-19 — Stimulation artifacts are blanked by RHS amp-settle (~1 ms post as a starting point) plus our own window, timed from the observed stim-output line, not the command; the window's duration is measured (V4) and recorded per session
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, engineering default on vendor guidance (S7 §4.4).
- **Where:** [S7 §4.4](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S7-neural-plane-design.md#44-blanking); [controller architecture §10.2](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-controller-architecture-design.md#102-verified-rhx-facts-user-guide-read-2026-08-31).
- **Basis:** Vendor guidance and published practice. [@intan2026intan] — vendor documentation: for the RHS amp-settle duration after a pulse, 1 ms has been found to work well, but the best value may change with the type of electrode and users are encouraged to experiment (User Guide, "Amplifier Settle", p. 24), the source of "~1 ms as a starting point" and a reason to measure it (V4). [@intan2021rhs] — vendor datasheet: the chip's fast-settle function clamps the amplifier output at baseline after a saturating artifact (p. 9, "Amplifier Fast Settle"). [@wagenaar2002realtime] — the artifact outlasts amplifier saturation: their method subtracts a fitted artifact, checked immediately after the amplifiers become operational, so that spikes can be detected by thresholding, shortening the post-stimulus blind period to under 2 ms (abstract), support for a feature-extractor window on top of amp-settle. [@oshea2018eraasr] — common approaches discard a typical window of several milliseconds after each pulse (Introduction, ¶5), which is why the window is a measured, recorded quantity. Timing the blank from the observed stim-output line is this design's engineering; no paper on it was found.

### REC-20 — Closed-loop latency: neural event to stim TTL budgeted at ~3-6 ms (estimate); proposed gate median ≤ 6 ms on the SpikeGLX path, the RHX path measured without a target; Open Ephys plus Falcon as the measured fallback
- **Decided:** 2026-08-30, engineering estimates (the SpikeGLX research note), and 2026-08-31, a proposed gate (S7 §6). Three citation errors in the research notes and pitfalls P5 were corrected on 2026-10-08 (below).
- **Where:** [S7 §6](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S7-neural-plane-design.md#6-measurement); [controller architecture §14](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-controller-architecture-design.md#14-latency-budgets--requirements-not-measurements); [roadmap M9](../roadmap.md#m9--neural-contingent-loop-stimulation-tier-3); [SpikeGLX research note](../research/spikeglx-realtime.md#sdk-facts-verified), "Latency" and [the fallback](../research/spikeglx-realtime.md#consequences-for-our-architecture); [pitfalls P5](../pitfalls.md#expanded-notes); [landscape note, "Real-time neural layer"](../research/landscape.md#real-time-neural-layer).
- **Basis:** Vendor and published figures for configurations that are not ours; the estimate itself is unmeasured. [@karsh2026spikeglx] — software documentation: the remote API lets a client fetch data with low latency, under 4 ms on the same computer (home page, "Remote Control/Scripting SDK"). [@karsh2026spikeglxcppsdk] — the SDK's closed-loop test histogram (C++ API, NP 2.0) concentrates at about 1.7-2.2 ms with a tail to about 4 ms (README figure "CPP API Latency"). [@slack2026opglx] — preprint: OP-GLX's minimum end-to-end latency is about 6.5 ms, which it attributes to the round-trip latency of the SpikeGLX API, citing the SpikeGLX MATLAB SDK, not to a measurement of its own (Discussion); that SDK's README shows only the C++ histogram near 2 ms, so the 6.5 ms's origin is untraced. [@openephys2026falcon] — software documentation: Neuropixels through Open Ephys on Windows, Falcon Output over the network to Falcon on Linux, threshold detection and an Arduino TTL read back into the PXIe gave a median of 9.2 ms, SD 1.3 ms, maximum 13 ms round trip, for one probe (page "Latency Measurements"). [@ciliberti2017falcon] — Falcon's own software latency was below 0.5 ms, its round trip well below 1 ms on Neuralynx hardware and below 15 ms on Open Ephys hardware (abstract). [@siegle2017open] — the Open Ephys GUI as a plugin platform for closed-loop feedback (abstract). On 2026-10-08 the SpikeGLX research note, the landscape note and pitfalls P5 were corrected: OP-GLX's DOI is 10.64898/2026.03.04.709636 (the 10.1101 form they carried does not resolve), its ~6.5 ms is not an independent measurement, and the Falcon Output page now gives 9.2 ms median for one probe, not the "9.241 ms median … 384 channels" read on 2026-08-30.

### REC-21 — Each trial's record holds its outcome, RT, condition, target positions, full parameter snapshot, gaze-mapping version, staleness, token state, observed stimulation, ten position numbers and commanded fluid
- **Decided:** 2026-08-31, engineering (S10's outputs); 2026-10-01, the PI (the ten position numbers, session-levels spec §3).
- **Where:** [S10 §2](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S10-data-and-lab-integration-design.md#2-what-we-write) (the outputs table); [session-levels spec §3](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-01-session-levels-and-strip-design.md#3-ten-position-numbers-decided); [architecture.md, "Message contracts"](../design/architecture.md#message-contracts-draft-v0); [XC-026 resume spec §4](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-01-xc026-resume-design.md#4-the-fluid-record-welfare-critical).
- **Basis:** Engineering default following the data standard. [@nwb2026nwb] — data-standard documentation: NWB's `/intervals/trials` table holds time intervals corresponding to repeated experimental units with consistent structure, such as individual stimulus-response-reward cycles, a `TimeIntervals` table to which per-trial columns are added (Format Specification 2.11.0, §2.6.2.45); [@rubel2022neurodata] — NWB's design for storing behavior and stimulation alongside neural data (Results, ¶1-2). The particular columns are the lab's.

### REC-22 — Condition numbers are fixed per task (stable across runs, sessions, animals and rigs, assigned by a registry, never reused); `CONDITION` is emitted only once conditions exist
- **Decided:** 2026-09-30 and 2026-10-07, the PI.
- **Where:** [engine spec §14](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#14-variation-n11-nr1-nr2) item 5; the brainstorm notes, [§11, Batch 2](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-brainstorm-notes.md#11-variation); [XC-155 trial-markers spec §2.3](../superpowers/specs/2026-09-30-xc155-trial-markers-design.md#23-not-in-this-change).
- **Basis:** PI decision; no published basis found (searched as for REC-04).

### REC-23 — NTP (`ntp.wl.works`) serves bookkeeping time only (which day, when a mark was made), never the timing record
- **Decided:** 2026-09-20, the PI (ADR-0009).
- **Where:** [ADR-0009, "Decision"](../design/decisions/ADR-0009-ntp-time-synchronization.md#decision); [architecture.md, "Data outputs and lab integration"](../design/architecture.md#data-outputs-and-lab-integration).
- **Basis:** PI decision, supported by the standards. [@mills2010network] — standard: typical secondary servers and clients on fast LANs are within a few hundred microseconds, and with long poll intervals precise within a few tens of milliseconds (RFC 5905 §1, p. 4), ample for which day and which minute. [@karsh2026sync] — software documentation: hardware sync edges align recorded streams with typical errors of about 0.01 ms (Overview), one to three orders of magnitude finer, which is why the timing record stays on hardware edges. [@ieee2020ieee] — standard: PTP, the network protocol for experimental-grade time, targets synchronization accuracy and precision in the sub-microsecond range (IEEE Std 1588-2019, abstract), the route if network time were ever wanted for timing; ADR-0009 does not propose it.

## Other

### OTH-01 — Where the cone-fundamental tables come from: bundle the CIE's CSVs as separate CC BY-SA 4.0 files with attribution and the metadata's sha256
- **Decided:** 2026-10-08, the PI (A2 Q3, "Bundle the CIE's files (Recommended)", offered against cvrl.org after asking, Stockman & Rider's formulae, and download on first use); an ADR when A2 is planned. The inventory read the tree before this answer and lists it as open.
- **Where:** [A2 research note, the questions](../research/2026-10-08-engine-a2-color-research.md#questions-for-the-pi-the-agents-framing-to-be-asked-in-plain-terms) (Q3), [§1.2](../research/2026-10-08-engine-a2-color-research.md#12-official-cie-datasets-ciecoatdata-tables) and [§1.3](../research/2026-10-08-engine-a2-color-research.md#13-terms-of-use-and-license-not-legal-advice); the brainstorm notes, "The morning after (asked 2026-10-08)", on engine-b.
- **Basis:** The data sets themselves. [@cie2006cie], [@cie2006cieb] — the CIE 2006 LMS cone fundamentals for 10° and 2° fields in terms of energy (CIE 170-1:2006 Tables 6.2 and 6.7 [@cie2006fundamental]), each a data set with its own DOI and a metadata `rightsList` of Creative Commons Attribution-ShareAlike 4.0 International (the data-set pages and metadata JSON, read 2026-10-08). Checked the same day: the metadata's md5 and sha256 fields match the CSVs served (10° c2e606fd…, bd64f1f6…; 2° dba2e9d1…, f48160ed…), while the pages' displayed MD5s (c92c94a7…, 27c74cc0…) do not, confirming the research note's §1.2. The declined alternative: [@stockman2023formulae] — closed-form formulae (360-850 nm) that reproduce the tabulated CIE LMS cone spectral sensitivities for 2° and 10° with little error (abstract).

### OTH-02 — Camera and light brackets are printed in PAHT-CF or polycarbonate, not PLA, for creep resistance, because a camera roll rotates the P1 − P4 vector
- **Decided:** 2026-09-27, engineering recommendation (S0 §7.1.3), on the PI's point that DPI cancels head translation but not camera rotation.
- **Where:** [S0 §7.1.3](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-S0-hosts-and-hardware-design.md#713-the-ipd-adjustment-system-requirements).
- **Basis:** [@dimitrellou2024mechanical] — of FDM-printed PLA, PC and PAHT-CF, the highest stage II creep resistance was achieved by PAHT-CF, followed by PC and PLA, and PAHT-CF had the highest strength and flexural modulus (abstract): the citation S0 carries, confirmed. [@wu2023highresolution] — under collimated light, eye rotations only change the angle of illumination relative to the eye's optical axis, so the authors model an eye movement by rotating the illumination and imaging system about the eye (Introduction, "Measuring eye movements from Purkinje reflections"), published support for S0's step marked INFERENCE, that a turned light or camera reads as an eye rotation; DPI is particularly robust to artifacts from head translation (Introduction, ¶3).

### OTH-03 — The panel's 26.5 in active area and pixel pitch; whether the pixels are square is UNVERIFIED (0.15364 mm horizontal against 0.15413 mm vertical from the published area)
- **Decided:** 2026-09-28, recorded in the optics drawing §1; whether the pixels are square is unknown, the maker not stating it.
- **Where:** [optics drawing §1](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-stereoscope-optics-drawing.md#1-inputs).
- **Basis:** [@asus2026rog] — manufacturer document: the display viewing area (H × V) is 589.97 × 332.93 mm and the pixel pitch 0.153 mm (spec page, read 2026-10-08; identical on the kr, sg, ch-de, rs-en, nl and uk pages). No source states the vertical pitch or that the pixels are square, so it stays UNVERIFIED (searched 2026-10-08: ASUS spec pages in seven regions, and the web for the panel's datasheet, none public found; a search-engine summary's 0.15525 mm was on no page checked and is not entered).

### OTH-04 — A clean-room reimplementation of the tracker is written only from the paper and general literature, so the method is free of GPL/AGPL derivation
- **Decided:** 2026-09-27 or earlier, the PI's ruling (clean room; its date is not stated, and it was in force for the spike of 2026-09-27).
- **Where:** [P10 spike §2](../research/2026-09-27-p10-dpi-spike.md#2-answer) and [§3](../research/2026-09-27-p10-dpi-spike.md#3-sources-clean-room-compliant).
- **Basis:** The ruling is legal, not scientific; what it needs from the literature is that the method is fully published. [@ressmeyer2026openirisdpi] — the appendix gives pseudocode for OpenIrisDPI's tracking algorithm (Algorithm 1) with prose: the convex hull of the pupil edge, an ellipse fit, and threshold-rectified centroids for the CR and P4 (Appendix, Algorithm 1 and its paragraph). [@wu2023highresolution] — the DPI principle and the paraxial model it attributes to Cornsweet & Crane (1973) (Introduction, "Measuring eye movements from Purkinje reflections"). [@fitzgibbon1999direct] — direct least-squares ellipse fitting by minimizing the algebraic distance under 4ac − b² = 1, ellipse-specific and solved as a generalized eigensystem (abstract; read in the authors' 1996 report version). [@halir1998numerically] — a numerically stable reformulation of that method, non-iterative and ellipse-specific (abstract and body). OpenIrisDPI's own license is stated in the paper: "The source code for the plugin is free and openly available under the GPLv3 license" (Introduction, last paragraph) [@ressmeyer2026openirisdpi]; the AGPL status of OpenIris itself comes from the spike and was not re-checked.
